Rule mode: UF1–UF3 are the original's behaviour in both settings; none is a flaw of the original, so no switch.

## Dialog script stores (ARC-DEV-152)

Source: the MIG8 leftover of ARC-DEV-100 ([object directory](../../architecture/object-directory.md#mig8-leftover-item-events-and-dlg-stores-arc-dev-152),
gap GR29) and the [conversation runtime](../../scripting/dialog-conversation-runtime.md#script-store-operations-arc-dev-152). Question: which DLG
conditions and actions read and write the global variables and the PC's flags and variables, and how. Read on 2026-11-06 by this task only with
`llvm-objdump` (the condition interpreter 0x4150D0 with its code table 0x5A06BC and jump table 0x415B04, the action interpreter 0x415BA0 with its code
table 0x5A0750 and jump table 0x41672C, the second-operand scan 0x4167C0 and the accessors 0x444C80 to 0x444F10), F1. The accessors are GV1–GV4.

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| DG1 | condition interpreter (0x4150D0), code table 0x5A06BC (37 codes), cases `gv` 6 (0x415277), `gf` 7 (0x415299), `pv` 30 (0x415968), `pf` 31 (0x415992) | Each case reads the second operand with 0x4167C0 (DS3) and passes only when the store's answer equals it: `gv` the global variable at the first operand (0x444C80), `gf` the global flag bit (0x444CF0), `pv` the variable and `pf` the flag (0x444DB0, 0x444E30) of the dialog state's first handle, the PC. A flag reads 0 or 1, so `pf i v` with any other v never passes. A passing case goes on to the next code; a failing one returns 0 at once (DS1). | Original |
| DG2 | the same cases, first operand (atoi, DS1) | The index is the first operand as read, with no bounds test: a global variable index outside 0..1999 reads past the 2000-word array, and a PC index is not limited to 16 bits here (the SCR operand packs it in 16, GV3). | Original (the project refuses a global index outside 0..1999 and a negative PC index at parse, as the SCR admission does for GV1; a PC index above 65535, beyond the session's 16-bit stores, is outside the conversation subset) |
| DG3 | action interpreter (0x415BA0), code table 0x5A0750 (35 codes), cases `gv` 5 (0x415ED9), `gf` 6 (0x415EEE), `pv` 22 (0x4163DA), `pf` 23 (0x4163F7) | Each case reads the second operand with 0x4167C0 and calls the setter with the first operand as index and that value: `gv` 0x444C90 (the whole value, GV1), `gf` 0x444D20 (X10), `pv` 0x444DF0 and `pf` 0x444E90 on the dialog's PC. The setter's answer is not read; the string goes on with the next code (0x4166EE). | Original |
| DG4 | `pf` action, 0x444E90 | As GV4: the flag's 32-flag word is read, the flag's bit cleared and the whole value ORed in shifted to the bit, so a value other than 0 or 1 spills into the higher flags of the word (2 at flag 33 sets flag 34). | Original bug (as X10, GV4); the conversation grammar admits only 0 and 1 for `pf` (no installed dialog has another value), so neither setting reaches it |

## Dialog reply gates and rumor conditions (ARC-DEV-171)

Source: bug B17 (the plane-wreck dialog, DLG 1696, ends after one turn; the report suspected the hero's low intelligence) and the
[conversation runtime](../../scripting/dialog-conversation-runtime.md#rumor-conditions-arc-dev-171). Question: does the original hide a
reply from a low-intelligence hero by the reply gate, and which condition keeps the wreck dialog from going on here. Read on 2026-11-07 by
this task only with `llvm-objdump` (the reply lister 0x414F50, the condition interpreter 0x4150D0 with its code table 0x5A06BC and jump
table 0x415B04, the rumor accessors 0x4C58D0 and 0x4C5650), F1; the file's records read with the project's own DLG reader. Every direct
branch stays inside `.text`.

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| IQ1 | reply lister (0x414F50), dumb test (0x45FC00) | Per candidate reply: the gender selector (-1 any, else the PC's stat 26), then the IQ field against the PC's intelligence (stat 4; above 4 and dumb counts 1, GS2): a non-negative IQ needs at least IQ, a negative one at most −IQ (both inclusive; IQ 0 passes at any non-negative intelligence), then the condition string (0x4150D0); five replies at most. | Original; the conversation runtime already followed it (`IsEligible`), no change |
| RU1 | condition `ru`, case 17 (0x415630), known (0x4C58D0) | The operand is read with `atoi` (DS1). n ≥ 0 passes when the dialog PC knows rumor n, n < 0 when it does not know rumor −n; a failing case ends the condition at once (DS1). Known: the object's type (field 0x14F) must be a PC (15) and the PC's rumor field 0x109 at index n − 1000 (0x407540) must read non-zero (the 64-bit game time the learning stored, SS4). A dialog PC that is not a PC makes `ru n` fail and `ru -n` pass. There is no range test in the condition or in 0x4C58D0: a number outside 1000..2999 indexes outside the rumor array (the bounds of the generic field accessor 0x407540 were not read). | Original |
| RU2 | condition `rq`, case 18 (0x41566C), quelled (0x4C5650) | The same sign rule over the game-wide quelled set: bit n − 1000 of the byte array the pointer stored at 0x6876C8 addresses, not the PC's (the set the `rq` action writes). | Original |
| RU3 | installed DLG 1696 (`dlg/01696…`, read in original mode) | By record number: node 1 offers reply 2 (IQ 5) and reply 3 (IQ −4), both to node 5; node 5 offers replies 6 and 7 (IQ 5, conditions `ru 2015` and `ru -2015`) and replies 8 and 9 (IQ −4, the same conditions); the clever replies lead through nodes 11 or 14 and 17 to the closing line (reply 18), the dull ones through nodes 20 or 23 and 26 (reply 27), each with exactly one reply per node. A hero of any intelligence has a complete path, so the dialog does not hide itself from a low-intelligence hero. | Original; the dull replies exist |
| RU4 | the Player condition fields of the installed dialogs | 223 fields in 21 files hold `ru` or `rq` (the wreck dialog 1696 among them). Two are damaged data: 19 conditions of dialog 1934 read rumor 4180, past the 2999 the session keeps, and one `rq - 1143` of dialog 1906 has a space after the sign, which `atoi` reads as 0. | Original data |

B17's verdict: **a reconstruction slip in the project's condition subset, not the intelligence gate**. Until ARC-DEV-171 the conversation
runtime admitted `ru` and `rq` only as actions; the unadmitted condition of replies 6 to 9 made node 5's reply group a frontier, so the
reply to node 1 was shown blocked for every hero and the conversation ended after one turn.

## Dialog condition and action codes (ARC-RES-092)

Source: slice C0 of the condition codes plan (ARC-RES-091, deleted) (ARC-RES-091). Question: for each of the 37 condition codes and
35 action codes of the dialog interpreters, what is its name, how is its operand read, what does it test or do and which state does it read or
write; and how often do the installed dialogs use each. Read on 2026-11-07 by this task only with `llvm-objdump` (the condition interpreter
0x4150D0 with its code table 0x5A06BC and jump table 0x415B04, the action interpreter 0x415BA0 with its code table 0x5A0750 and jump table 0x41672C,
and the callees named below, whose project records are cited), F1; `Arcanum.exe` SHA-256 `9bd80511…3678d52`. Every direct branch stays inside `.text`.
Codes written in lowercase are the table's. The dialog state record holds the dialog's PC handle at +0x08 and the NPC handle at +0x38 ("the PC", "the
NPC"); a "follower" is a direct follower of the PC (0x4411C0, released by 0x4410E0). "The signed rule" is the one of DS2: operand n ≥ 0 passes at value ≥ n,
n < 0 at value ≤ −n. "The second operand" is read by 0x4167C0 (DS3). The earlier rows DS1–DS5, DG1–DG4, DR1, RP8, RU1–RU2, LB12, SU13–SU14, NC1, XP11 and FT8
already hold the codes marked "recorded".

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| DCC1 | condition code table 0x5A06BC, jump table 0x415B04 | The table index is the jump index. In order, with the case address: 0 `ps` 0x4151A9, 1 `ch` 0x4151D3, 2 `pe` 0x4151E2, 3 `al` 0x415202, 4 `ma` 0x415222, 5 `ta` 0x415231, 6 `gv` 0x415277, 7 `gf` 0x415299, 8 `qu` 0x4152BB, 9 `re` 0x4152E5, 10 `$$` 0x415335, 11 `in` 0x4153A6, 12 `ha` 0x41545C, 13 `lf` 0x41549E, 14 `lc` 0x4154CA, 15 `tr` 0x4154F6, 16 `sk` 0x415593, 17 `ru` 0x415630, 18 `rq` 0x41566C, 19 `fo` 0x415698, 20 `le` 0x4156DE, 21 `qb` 0x415720, 22 `me` 0x41574A, 23 `ni` 0x415775, 24 `qa` 0x415831, 25 `ra` 0x415855, 26 `pa` 0x41587D, 27 `ss` 0x4158FF, 28 `wa` 0x415919, 29 `wt` 0x415934, 30 `pv` 0x415968, 31 `pf` 0x415992, 32 `na` 0x4159BC, 33 `ar` 0x4159FE, 34 `rp` 0x415A40, 35 `ia` 0x415A78, 36 `sc` 0x415AAA. The scan, the match and the end rules are DS1 (a case that fails returns 0 at once, all passing returns 1). | Original |
| DCC2 | cases `ch` 1, `pe` 2, `le` 20 (stat getter 0x4B0490) | The PC's stat 7 (charisma), 5 (perception) and 17 (level), the signed rule. The first operand is the threshold. Owner read: the PC's stats (character). | Original |
| DCC3 | cases `al` 3, `na` 32 (stat 19, alignment), `ma` 4, `ta` 5 (stat 16, the magick–technology aptitude) | `al n`: the signed rule on the alignment. `na n`: n ≥ 0 passes at alignment ≥ −n, n < 0 at alignment ≤ n; so `na 5` passes at any alignment from −5 up. An odd form (the two branches are not mirror images); whether it is an original quirk was not judged. `ma n`: the signed rule on the aptitude (positive is magick). `ta n`: the signed rule on the aptitude negated (positive is technology), so the two read one stat from either side. | Original |
| DCC4 | cases `qu` 8, `qa` 24, `qb` 21 (quest state 0x4C4CB0, SG1) | The first operand is the quest number, the second the state: `qu` passes at state equal, `qa` at state at least, `qb` at state at most. The state is the one SG1 describes for a PC. Owner read: the PC's quest log, the owner of the `QU` action the runtime already parses. | Original |
| DCC5 | condition `$$`, case 10 (gold 0x464780, AM7) | The signed rule on the total gold of the PC plus every follower's (each follower's gold read from its inventory). Owner read: the inventories of the PC and the followers. | Original |
| DCC6 | conditions `in` 11, `ni` 23 (item by name 0x462480, SG2; substitute container 0x45F650, BT11) | `in n`, n ≥ 0: passes when the PC or any follower holds an item whose name field (object field 0x16, read by 0x462480) equals n. `in −n`: passes when the NPC holds item n, or else when its substitute container (0x45F650, BT11) does; with no substitute it fails. `ni` is the exact negation of `in` on both branches (`ni n` passes when neither the PC nor a follower holds it; `ni −n` when neither the NPC nor its substitute does; with no substitute and the NPC not holding it it passes). Owner read: item holdings of the PC, the followers, the NPC and its shop container. | Original |
| DCC7 | conditions `ss` 27, `ar` 33, `ia` 35 | `ss n`: the story state (0x445090, SS1), the signed rule. `ar n`: area n is known to the PC (0x4CAF50, WT2), n > 0 requires known, n ≤ 0 requires −n not known. `ia n`: the area of the PC's map (0x4CB6A0, GA2): n > 0 passes when equal, n ≤ 0 when different from −n. Owner read: the script story state; the PC's known areas; the current map's area. | Original |
| DCC8 | conditions `fo` 19, `pa` 26, `wa` 28, `wt` 29 | `fo v`: the NPC's leader (0x45DE00) is the PC; v 1 passes when it is, v 0 when it is not, any other v passes untested. `pa n`: some follower whose spell flags (field 0x14) lack the mind-control bit 0x8000000 has name field 0x16 equal to |n|; n ≥ 0 passes when there is one, n < 0 when there is none. `wa v`, `wt v`: NPC flag word 0x118 bit 0x8 (WAIT) and 0x20 (JILTED): v 1 passes when the bit is set, v 0 when it is clear, any other v passes untested. Owner read: the NPC's leader link, the followers' names and spell flags, the NPC flags. `wa` is recorded (SU14, parsed). | Original (`wt` is not parsed) |
| DCC9 | conditions `lf` 13, `lc` 14 (attachment queries 0x444F10, 0x444FD0; E7) | Both read the NPC's script attachment at point 9 (the dialog point, SA1): `lf i v` the flag i, `lc i v` the counter i; the answer must equal the second operand. `lf` is recorded and parsed; `lc` is the same shape over the counter (the counter's range was not read). | Original |
| DCC10 | condition `sc` 36 (college rank 0x4B1AB0, CP1) | The first operand is the college (the getter 0x4B1AB0 checks only the object type, the range is not read, CK1), the second operand v: the signed rule on the PC's rank in that college (v > 0 passes at rank ≥ v, v ≤ 0 at rank ≤ −v). Owner read: the PC's college ranks (magic). | Original |
| DCC11 | recorded condition codes | `ps ha sk tr` DS2; `gv gf pv pf` DG1; `re` R1 and the reaction rows (0x4C0CC0); `ra` (stat 27, race + 1; the runtime document's `RA` rule); `me` DR1; `rp` RP8; `ru rq` RU1–RU2. | Original |
| DAC1 | action code table 0x5A0750, jump table 0x41672C | In order, with the case address: 0 `$$` 0x415CC6, 1 `re` 0x415D91, 2 `qu` 0x415E82, 3 `fl` 0x415EA7, 4 `co` 0x415ECC, 5 `gv` 0x415ED9, 6 `gf` 0x415EEE, 7 `mm` 0x415F03, 8 `al` 0x415F19, 9 `in` 0x415FED, 10 `lf` 0x4161AD, 11 `lc` 0x4161CC, 12 `tr` 0x4161EB, 13 `ru` 0x416229, 14 `rq` 0x41623F, 15 `jo` 0x41624D, 16 `wa` 0x4162AD, 17 `lv` 0x4162C2, 18 `ss` 0x4162D9, 19 `sc` 0x4162E7, 20 `so` 0x4162FC, 21 `uw` 0x416311, 22 `pv` 0x4163DA, 23 `pf` 0x4163F7, 24 `xp` 0x416414, 25 `nk` 0x416430, 26 `rp` 0x416445, 27 `np` 0x416477, 28 `ce` 0x41648C, 29 `fp` 0x4164C6, 30 `su` 0x4164EE, 31 `or` 0x416528, 32 `ii` 0x41653E, 33 `ri` 0x416578, 34 `et` 0x4165B2. The first operand is read with `atoi` before the case (DS1). After a case the string goes on (0x4166EE), so later operations still run even after a case cleared the result; the result (1 unless a case cleared it) is returned, and a flag set by `co` makes the attack event run once at the end (0x4A9650, SU13). | Original |
| DAC2 | action `$$` 0 | n > 0: n gold goes to the PC from no source object (0x464830 with a null source; that callee's handling of the null source was not read). n < 0: −n gold moves from the PC to the NPC; what the PC lacks is taken from the followers in list order until −n is paid or the list ends (this is the intent only: each payer's transfer is all or nothing and the amount owed falls by the payer's whole gold, so the real order of events is [GM3](#dialog-money-code-arc-dev-179)). Owner write: the inventories of the PC and the followers, the NPC's gold. | Original (n > 0 read in GM2 and GM3, n < 0 corrected by GM3) |
| DAC3 | actions `re` 1, `al` 8 (reaction change 0x4C0DE0, base stat setter 0x4B0980; stat 19) | The first operand is read, then the first non-space character after the code decides: `+` and `-` add the signed operand, `>` raises the value to at least the operand and `<` lowers it to at most the operand when it is not already there, any other character sets it to the operand. `atoi` stops at `>` and `<`, so those forms read the operand 0 (the runtime leaves them outside the subset). The reaction is the NPC's toward the PC (0x4C0CC0); `al` writes the PC's alignment stat. Recorded: SU14. | Original |
| DAC4 | actions `qu` 2, `fl` 3, `co` 4, `gv gf pv pf` 5 6 22 23, `mm` 7, `tr` 12, `ru rq` 13 14, `jo` 15, `wa` 16, `lv` 17, `uw` 21, `rp` 26, `fp` 29, `su` 30, `et` 34 | `qu q s`: quest state s for quest q (0x4C4D20, with the NPC for the experience). `fl n`: stores n as the dialog's node, speaks it (0x413A30), sets the pending code 4 and clears the result. `co`: DAC1. `gv gf pv pf`: DG3. `mm n`: marks area n known to the PC (0x4CAFD0, WT2). `tr`: DS4. `ru rq`: LB12. `jo n node`, `uw n node`: after the follow check (0x4AD950) the NPC joins (0x45DE90) or stops waiting (0x4AA8C0) and the dialog goes to the node, a refusal speaks the generated line instead. `wa`: the NPC waits (0x4AA7A0). `lv`: the NPC is forced to leave the party (0x45DFC0). `rp`: RP8. `fp`: FT8. `su`: NC1. `et`: DS5. Recorded: SU13, SU14, DS4, DS5, DG3, LB12, RP8, FT8, NC1. | Original |
| DAC5 | action `in` 9 (item by name 0x462480; transfer 0x4617F0; substitute 0x45F650) | n ≥ 0: the PC (failing that, the first follower holding it) hands item n to the NPC: the item is transferred (0x4617F0) and its holder re-read (0x4612A0); when it did not end up with the NPC, an NPC for which 0x45DDA0 returns a leader (presumably the PC's) has it detached and placed at its own location (0x466E00, 0x466E50) and any other NPC has it destroyed (0x43CCA0). n < 0: the NPC (failing that, its substitute) gives item −n to the PC the same way, placed at the PC's location when the transfer fails and never destroyed. The calls 0x466E00 and 0x466E50 were named by their use only. Owner read and write: inventories. | Original (the runtime parses only the negative form) |
| DAC6 | actions `lf` 10, `lc` 11 (setters 0x444F60, 0x445020) | Write the second operand into the NPC's attachment point 9 flag i and counter i (DCC9). `lf` is recorded and parsed. | Original |
| DAC7 | action `ss` 18 (set 0x4450A0, SS1) | Sets the story state to n. | Original |
| DAC8 | actions `sc` 19, `so` 20 (0x45F5C0, 0x45F600; OL8) | Clear and set the NPC's spread-out flag (NPC flag 0x10); they read no operand. | Original |
| DAC9 | actions `nk` 25, `xp` 24 (critter kill 0x45D900; experience 0x45F110, quest experience 0x4C53C0, XP8) | `nk`: the NPC is killed. `xp n`: the PC gets the experience of quest level n (`quest_get_xp`, XP11); wired in the viewer dialog host since ARC-DEV-178 (any operand is looked up, a level without an `xp_quest.mes` entry gives 0 and changes nothing, XP1). | Original |
| DAC10 | action `np` 27 (0x4BF140) | The first and second operands go to a queue of 25 entries (the newspaper issues the reading window prints, WR1); an operand below 5000 is looked up first. The rest of the rule is read in [NQ1–NQ5](#dialog-newspaper-queue-and-npc-origin-arc-dev-230). | Original |
| DAC11 | actions `ce` 28, `ii` 32, `ri` 33 (generated line 0x413BE0) | Each composes a generated NPC line, sets the pending code 5, 8 and 9 and clears the result. What the reply UI opens for those codes was not traced (the same mechanism as `su`, NC1). | Original (UI open) |
| DAC12 | action `or` 31 (0x45D2D0) | Sets the NPC's origin (object field 0x123, the origin the reputation effects match, RP5) to n; nothing happens to a non-NPC. | Original |
| DCX1 | the installed dialogs, scan of DS1 over every condition and action field, 709 DLG files (the opt-in census `DialogCodeCensusRealDataTests`, 2026-11-07) | Counts only (the plan that held the per-code table is finished and deleted: C0-C10 are done and the semantics are rows DCC1-DCC11 and DAC1-DAC12; the table can be regenerated by the opt-in census with `ARC_DIALOG_CENSUS_OUT`). Player condition fields 15,512: 8,487 parse whole under the grammar, 7,025 stop (6,989 first at a code outside the subset, 30 at a form, 6 at a non-code). Player action fields 16,644: 13,210 whole, 3,434 stop (3,047 first at a code outside it, 383 at a form, 4 at a non-code). NPC action fields 5,211: 4,825 whole, 386 stop (348 / 38). The counts are static occurrences: the scan does not stop at a code outside the table the way the interpreter does. The 2,716 NPC-record condition fields are numbers (2,715) and one text field, not operation strings. Unused in every installed dialog: condition `wt`, `pv`, `pf`; action `pv`, `pf`. ## Dialog money code (ARC-DEV-179)

Source: slice C1 of the condition codes plan (ARC-RES-091, deleted) (ARC-RES-091). Question: how exactly does the `$$` condition (case 10
of the condition interpreter 0x4150D0) read the party's gold, and what do the action's two signs (case 0 of 0x415BA0) do, in particular with a null
source and with a PC that holds less than the debt? Read on 2026-11-07 with `llvm-objdump` (the two cases, the gold getter 0x464780 with its stack
finder 0x4647D0, the transfer 0x464830, the follower list 0x4411C0), F1; `Arcanum.exe` SHA-256 `9bd80511…3678d52`. Every direct branch stays inside
`.text`. "The PC" and "the NPC" are the dialog state record's handles (DCC1 note); the transfer's arguments are source handle, destination handle, count
and two unused zeros.

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| GM1 | gold getter 0x464780, condition case 10 (0x415335) | The getter takes an object: an item of type 8 (gold) answers its own quantity, a critter (types 15 and 16) its gold stack's quantity (field 0xE8 names the stack, 0xA5 is the quantity, AM7), a container (type 2) goes to a stack lookup by prototype (0x462540, not traced); no stack is 0. The condition adds the getter over the PC and then over every direct follower of the PC (0x4411C0, the follower array in order, a snapshot) as a signed 32-bit sum that wraps, then tests it against the first operand: n ≥ 0 passes at sum ≥ n, n < 0 at sum ≤ −n, both inclusive and signed (n = 0 passes at sum ≥ 0; the operand −2³¹ keeps its sign under the negation and so passes only at sum = −2³¹). A waiting NPC is outside the follower array and counts nothing. | Original |
| GM2 | transfer 0x464830 (source, destination, count), the action's credit | A count of 0 returns 1 at once. A global mode byte (0x62AC6C: bit 0 set without bit 1 returns 0) is checked next; what sets it was not read. **With no source object** the debit part is skipped: the destination's gold stack is found (0x4647D0) and its quantity raised by the count with no overflow test (0x4EFDD0, 32-bit), or, without a stack, a new gold object is made (0x406DA0) and moved into the destination's list (0x4617F0); then a UI refresh (0x4605D0) and the result 1. A source that **has** objects: its gold stack (0x4647D0, or the one passed) must hold at least the count, else the call returns 0 and changes nothing; an event 0x15 is fired on the destination for the stack (0x441980, presumably the insert-item script event, not traced), the stack is destroyed when its quantity equals the count (0x43CCA0) and otherwise lowered by the count; then the same credit. A source without a stack still credits the destination (the debit part is skipped when the stack is null), as in AMA6. | Original |
| GM3 | action case 0 (0x415CC6) | n = 0 does nothing. n > 0: the transfer with no source and the PC as destination, so the PC gets n coins from nothing, with no weight or insert check. n < 0 (d = −n): the PC's gold g is read first (0x464780); the transfer PC to NPC with count d runs (its result is ignored); **then d falls by g whether or not the transfer moved anything**; if d > 0 the PC's direct followers are listed (0x4411C0, after the PC's call) and, in order while d > 0, each follower's gold h is read, the transfer follower to NPC with count d runs and d falls by h. Because the transfer is all or nothing (GM2), a payer that holds less than d at its turn pays nothing at all and yet is counted as having paid everything it held: a PC with 50 gold and a debt of 100 keeps its 50, d becomes 50, and a follower with 60 then pays 50, so the NPC is paid 50 of 100; a payer that holds a stack with less than what is left pays nothing. A payer with **no gold stack at all** (an NPC that never held coins, or whose stack was destroyed by paying its last coin, AM5) is not refused: its transfer skips the debit and credits the NPC the whole amount owed from nothing (GM2), and the owed amount falls by its 0 gold, so in the Original setting every goldless follower asked while something is owed makes money for the NPC (a PC with no gold and a follower with 200 on `$$ -100` pays the NPC 200). The result of the action string is untouched and the string goes on. | **Bug of the original game** (Proposed switch below) |
| GM4 | the talking NPC among the followers | When the NPC is itself a follower the transfer from it to itself moves its coins out (destroying its stack when the count equals it) and back in, a net no-op, while its gold still lowers d and still counts in the condition's sum. | Original |
| GM5 | the installed dialogs, `$$` fields | 1,576 Player condition fields in 81 files (no NPC condition is a string); 1,388 action fields (1,133 Player, 255 NPC line) in 139 files. Operands are written as `$$ n`, `$$n` and `$$-n`. Three action fields are forms the project's strict operand grammar leaves out and which stay stopped at a form: `$$ - 500` (twice, a space between the sign and the digits, which `atoi` reads as 0, so the source pays nothing and goes on to the next code) and `$$-500qu1042 2` (the operand is run into the next code with no separator; the source reads 500 and then the quest operation). | Original data |
| GM6 | the census after the slice (2026-11-07, 709 files) | The money code stops no installed field at a code outside the subset. Player condition fields 15,512: 9,982 whole, 5,530 stop (5,494 first at a code outside the subset). Player action fields 16,644: 14,246 whole, 2,398 stop (1,943 outside). NPC action fields 5,211: 5,037 whole, 174 stop (104 outside). 7,574 fields (of 10,431 before) still hold a code outside the subset. | Original data |

GM3's verdict: **a bug of the original game, not a reconstruction slip**: the author clearly meant "the PC pays what it has, the followers pay the rest" (the
amount owed falls by the PC's whole gold), but the refused all-or-nothing transfer (GM2) leaves the PC's gold untouched. Under the owner's rule modes
(D8): **Approved by the owner on 2026-11-07** (proposed in ARC-DEV-179, confirmed when the owner accepted the coordinator's table before ARC-DEV-180): the Original setting keeps it (`DialogRuleProfile.ShortPayerPaysNothing`), Steamwoven makes
each payer pay the lesser of its gold and what is owed, in the same order, and a payer without gold nothing, so the NPC gets at most what the condition `$$ n` promised (the talking NPC's own gold, when it is a follower, counts toward the condition but pays nobody, GM4). The two settings differ
only when the PC holds less than the debt (or a follower less than what is left), which needs a follower with gold: both cases are rare in play, because
a payment is normally gated by the same amount in `$$ n`, and the PC pays alone.

## Dialog quest log conditions (ARC-DEV-180)

Source: slice C2 of the condition codes plan (ARC-RES-091, deleted) (ARC-RES-091). Question: how exactly do the conditions `qu`, `qa` and `qb`
(cases 8, 24 and 21 of the condition interpreter 0x4150D0) read the PC's quest state, what does the state getter answer, and does anything differ between the
two rule settings? Read on 2026-11-07 with `llvm-objdump` (the three cases, the second-operand reader 0x4167C0, the quest state getter 0x4C4CB0, the global state
getter 0x4C51A0, the global table reset 0x4C4620 and the typed field getters 0x407900 / 0x408BB0 / 0x4E4180 it calls), F2 for QL1 and QL2 (a fresh reviewer repeated the reading of the three cases, the getter and 0x4167C0 independently and agreed; he noted that 0x4167C0 skips the C library's whole space set, which the grammar narrows to space and tab, and that a signed first operand would be re-read as the second, both unreachable in the subset), F1 for QL3; `Arcanum.exe` SHA-256
`9bd80511…3678d52`. Every direct branch stays inside `.text`. "The PC" is the dialog state record's handle (DCC1 note). Earlier rows: DCC4, SG1, XP8, LB6.

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| QL1 | conditions `qu` 8 (0x4152BB), `qb` 21 (0x415720), `qa` 24 (0x415831) | Each reads the second operand with 0x4167C0 (DS3: spaces before it are skipped, a missing one is 0), calls the quest state getter 0x4C4CB0 with the PC's handle and the first operand (the quest number, as `atoi` left it, no range test), and compares the answer with the second operand as **signed** 32-bit integers: `qu` fails on any difference (so passes at equal), `qb` fails when the state is greater (passes at state ≤ v), `qa` fails when the state is lower (passes at state ≥ v). A failing case returns 0 at once; a passing one goes on to the next operation of the field (DS1). A negative operand is legal: `qb q −1` never passes, `qa q −1` always does. | Original |
| QL2 | quest state getter (0x4C4CB0) | The object type (field 0x14F) of the handle decides. A PC (type 15): the PC's quest array (object field 0x103, element = quest number) is read into a local record by the typed field getter and the record's state word is returned, **6 (Botched) when its bit 0x100 is set** (the botched mark kept over the stored state; XP8, SS2, LB6 describe the writer). Any other type: the quest's global state (0x4C51A0), the word at index quest − 1000 of a 1000-entry table with no range test, every entry reset to 2 (Accepted) at 0x4C4620. The dialog's PC is always a PC in a one-player session, so the first branch is the one the conditions use. | Original |
| QL3 | the quest array element of a PC that never changed the quest (0x407900 → 0x408BB0 → 0x4E4180) | The typed getter copies the element out of the object's array field; the library's null-slot cases (0x4E41C3 and its siblings) zero-fill the out record, so a quest the PC never changed reads state 0 (Unknown), which agrees with the story model of `QueryQuest` (no record = Unknown) and with LB6. The quest array's own element size and null-slot case were not traced, so the "absent reads 0" step stays F1. The same getter indexes a number outside 1000..1999 with no test (a sparse array slot beyond the module's quests; for a non-PC the table read runs off its end): such a number is valid data with no state to answer, left outside the subset. | Original (absent element F1) |
| QL4 | the rule settings | Nothing in QL1–QL3 is a bug of the game: the compares, the botched mark and the Unknown default are the designed behaviour, so Original and Steamwoven are the same and there is no switch. | Original |
| QL5 | the installed dialogs (opt-in census `DialogCodeCensusRealDataTests`, 2026-11-07, 709 DLG files) | Player condition fields: `qu` 1,303 (139 files), `qa` 528 (96), `qb` 468 (84); every quest number is in 1000..1999. After admitting them the Player condition fields that parse whole rise from 9,982 to 11,630 and the fields holding a code outside the subset fall from 7,574 to 5,921 (1,653 freed, the plan's number); no field stops on `qu`, `qa` or `qb` itself. Two fields with a quest code stop at a form that has nothing to do with the quest: `qa1043, 1lf3 0` (a stray number where an operation should start) and `lf81 , lf9 1, lf10 1, qb1046 1` (an `lf` with its second operand missing before a comma). No NPC condition holds a quest code (NPC conditions are numbers). | Original data |

## Dialog combat action (ARC-DEV-190)

Source: slice C3 of the condition codes plan (ARC-RES-091, deleted) (ARC-RES-091). Question: when exactly does the action `co` (case 4 of the
action interpreter 0x415BA0) start a fight, between which objects, and what runs before it? Read on 2026-11-07 with `llvm-objdump` (the interpreter's
prologue 0x415BA0–0x415C32, the `co` case 0x415ECC, the string loop 0x4166EE and the tail 0x4166FB–0x41672A; the `ret` check of the attack event's prologue
0x4A9650 for the argument order), F1; `Arcanum.exe` SHA-256 `9bd80511…3678d52`. Every direct branch stays inside `.text`. "The PC" and "the NPC" are the dialog state
record's handles (DCC1 note). Earlier rows: SU13, DAC1, DAC4, DS1, SU10, SU12.

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| CA1 | action interpreter, local flag (0x415BB5, case `co` 0x415ECC) | The interpreter keeps a local flag, set to 0 at its entry; the `co` case only stores 1 in it and returns to the string loop (0x4166EE). The first operand, read with `atoi` before the case (DS1), is not used, so `co`, `co 0` and `co 5` are the same; nothing else is done at `co` itself. The string goes on after it: later operations still run, a second `co` changes nothing. | Original |
| CA2 | action interpreter, end of the string (0x4166FB–0x416717) | When the loop reaches the string's terminator (the next character is 0) and the flag is set, the interpreter calls the attack event (0x4A9650, SU13) once with these arguments: attacker = the PC handle (state record +0x08/+0x0C), victim = the NPC handle (+0x38/+0x3C), loudness 1, flags 0. The returned result (1, or 0 when an earlier case cleared it, as `fl` does) is not changed by the event. So the event runs **after** everything else in the string: after a `fl n` has spoken its node and run that node's own action string (0x413A30), and after the operations written behind the `co` (`co, lf 3 1` and `lf 3 1, co` do the same). Three exits skip the event, flag or not: an empty string (0x415BDC), a code with only one character left (0x415C50) and a code that is not in the table (0x415CB9, DS1: the string ends there), so `co` followed by a damaged operation never starts the fight. | Original |
| CA3 | what the event does, and what is not read here | The attack event of the PC on the NPC at loudness 1 with no flags is the hero's ordinary attack event (SU13, HostilityRules.AttackEvent, [hostility ledger](npc-ai.md#npc-ai-hostility-and-targeting-arc-res-057)): the NPC's who-hit-me-last, the reaction toward the PC, the tell-the-followers walks, the noise and the victim's reaction to the attack (fight or flee) are that function's. What the reply step (0x413130) does *after* the action string, when the string returned 1 (a `co` that is not followed by a final line or another transfer): by SU10 it runs the hostile check (kill-on-sight toward the PC) before the next node, and an NPC the attack made hostile would then speak a generated line (group 1000) instead of the reply's target node; that step was read in ARC-DEV-193 (HR1–HR3): the check follows every string that returns 1, whatever its codes, and the conversation runtime now models it behind a port. | Original (SU10, HR1–HR3) |
| CA4 | the rule settings | Nothing in CA1–CA2 is a bug of the game: the flag, the deferred event and the three exits are the designed behaviour, so Original and Steamwoven are the same and there is no switch. | Original |
| CA5 | what the installed scripts do now (`AreaScriptAttackAdmissionTests`, `AreaStoryScriptAdmissionTests`, `DAT_ROOT`, measured 2026-11-13 with and without the six actions in the talk sets) | The census of the scripts that carry a cast action or a spell command (over a hundred) showed 18 whose only command the skill talk set refused was a cast action (16 with cast spell on, among them 2729, 2799, 28006 and 28019, and 2 with cast unresistable spell on, 1571 and 2191): the talk sets now admit them whole. Of the installed scripts that carry an attack the talk sets admit 11 (10 before), of those with a story or clock command 31 (30 before); the five spell commands' own count (46 scripts, talk 1) does not move. Most scripts with casts still hold a command of another slice under the talk set (get stat 33, actions 7, 18, 19, 34, 41, 91, 120 and others). | Data |

## Dialog story state and area codes (ARC-DEV-191)

Source: slice C4 of the condition codes plan (ARC-RES-091, deleted) (ARC-RES-091). Question: how exactly do the conditions `ss`, `ar` and `ia`
(cases 27, 33 and 35 of the condition interpreter 0x4150D0) and the action `ss` (case 18 of 0x415BA0) read and write the state they name, and does anything differ
between the rule settings? Read on 2026-11-07 with `llvm-objdump` (the three condition cases and their shared pass/fail tails 0x415AD8 / 0x415AED, the negative branch
0x415711, the action case 0x4162D9, the story state getter 0x445090 and setter 0x4450A0, the known-area reader 0x4CAF50 and the area-of-object function 0x4CB6A0 with its
callee tail), F1; `Arcanum.exe` SHA-256 `9bd80511…3678d52`. Every direct branch stays inside `.text`. "The PC" is the dialog state record's handle (DCC1 note). Earlier rows:
DCC7, DAC7, SS1, WT2, GA2.

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| SV1 | condition `ss` (0x4158FF) | Reads the story state (0x445090: the global at 0x5E2FA4) and applies the signed rule of DS2 to the first operand n: n ≥ 0 fails when the state is below n (passes at state ≥ n); n < 0 takes the shared negative branch (0x415711), which negates n and fails when the state is greater (passes at state ≤ −n). No second operand. | Original |
| SV2 | action `ss` (0x4162D9 → setter 0x4450A0) | Calls the setter with the first operand. The setter (after its network test, SS1) stores the value in the global only when it is **greater** than the old one (a signed compare, 0x4450DD), so a lower or equal value changes nothing and the story state never falls; there is no error result. | Original |
| SV3 | condition `ar` (0x4159FE → 0x4CAF50) | n > 0: the case calls the known-area reader with the PC handle and n and fails when it answers 0; n ≤ 0: it negates n and fails when the answer is not 0 (so `ar −7` passes when area 7 is **not** known, and `ar 0` asks area 0). The reader answers 0 for a null handle, for any object whose type (field 0x14F) is not 15 (a PC) and, in a single-player game, for any handle other than the local PC (0x40DA50); otherwise bit 0 of the byte for the area in the table at 0x5FF5E8 (WT2: no range test on the area number). | Original |
| SV4 | condition `ia` (0x415A78 → 0x4CB6A0) | n > 0: passes when the area of the PC (GA2: the current map's map-list area, and on the start map without one the area at the object's location) equals n; n ≤ 0: passes when it differs from −n (so `ia 0` passes on any map that has an area, and `ia −3` also passes on a map without one). | Original |
| SV5 | the rule settings | Nothing in SV1–SV4 is a bug of the game: the signed rule, the keep-the-larger setter and the sign conventions are the designed behaviour, so Original and Steamwoven are the same and there is no switch. | Original |
| SV6 | the installed dialogs (opt-in census `DialogCodeCensusRealDataTests`, 2026-11-07, 709 DLG files) | Player condition fields: `ss` 703 (40 files), `ar` 37 (10), `ia` 238 (19); actions: `ss` 102 fields (23 files; 61 in NPC lines, 41 in Player replies). Every `ar` operand is within 1..4095 in magnitude. After admitting them the Player condition fields that parse whole rise from 11,630 to 12,568, the Player actions from 15,522 to 15,563 and the NPC actions from 5,037 to 5,095, and the fields holding a code outside the subset fall from 4,596 to 3,545 (1,051 freed, the plan's number); no field stops on these codes themselves except two `ss` fields at a form unrelated to the story state (a stray number where an operation should start and an `lf` with its second operand missing before a comma); the other Player condition fields that stopped first on one of these codes now go on to a later damaged character or form in the same field. The dialog `mm` action of the same area family wrote only a memory set of the viewer's dialog state before this slice; it now marks the area known in the session (WT2), the set `ar` reads. | Original data |

## Dialog PC stat conditions (ARC-DEV-192)

Source: slice C5 of the condition codes plan (ARC-RES-091, deleted) (ARC-RES-091). Question: which stat does each of `ch`, `pe`, `le`, `al`, `ma`, `ta` and `na`
(cases 1, 2, 20, 3, 4, 5 and 32 of the condition interpreter 0x4150D0) test, with which compare, and is any of them a bug of the game? Read again on 2026-11-07 with
`llvm-objdump` (the seven cases 0x4151D3, 0x4151E2, 0x4156DE, 0x415202, 0x415222, 0x415231 and 0x4159BC with their shared tails 0x4156E4, 0x4156EC, 0x415701, 0x415709,
0x415711 and the pass/fail tails 0x415AD8 / 0x415AED), F1, on top of the earlier row DCC2–DCC3; `Arcanum.exe` SHA-256 `9bd80511…3678d52`. Every direct branch stays inside
`.text`. "The PC" is the dialog state record's handle (DCC1 note); every case calls the stat getter 0x4B0490 with that handle and a stat number.

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| PQ1 | conditions `ch` (stat 7), `pe` (5), `le` (17), `al` (19), `ma` (16) | The first operand n is the threshold; a negative n takes the shared negative branch (0x415701/0x415709 → 0x415711), which negates it. Compare of the getter's value v with n, signed: n ≥ 0 fails when v < n (passes at v ≥ n); n < 0 fails when v > −n (passes at v ≤ −n). The stat numbers are the getter's: 5 perception, 7 charisma, 16 the magick–technology aptitude (positive is magick), 17 level, 19 alignment (the same numbers get stat of the SCR actions uses, GS1). | Original |
| PQ2 | condition `ta` (0x415231, stat 16) | The same stat as `ma`, negated before the compare (0x415244, 0x415263), so a positive operand asks for technology: n ≥ 0 passes at −v ≥ n; n < 0 passes at −v ≤ −n, that is v ≥ n. So `ta n` (n ≥ 0) asks for a technology aptitude of at least n and `ta −n` for at most n, the mirror of `ma` read from the technology side. | Original |
| PQ3 | condition `na` (0x4159BC, stat 19) | n ≥ 0 negates n and fails when the alignment is below it (passes at alignment ≥ −n); n < 0 fails when the alignment is above n (passes at alignment ≤ n). It is not the signed rule (`na 5` passes from −5 up, `na −5` passes at −5 and below), but the installed dialogs use it in complementary pairs (`na 799` and `na −800`, 18 and 17 fields; `na 49`, `na 300`, `na −50`, `na −100`, `na −200`, `na −500`): "alignment at least −799" against "at most −800", a clean split of the scale. So the form is the designed one, not a slip. | Original |
| PQ4 | the rule settings, and what the getter returns | Nothing in PQ1–PQ3 is a bug of the game: Original and Steamwoven are the same and there is no switch. The value is the stat getter's (0x4B0490, as GS1 recorded it for get stat); the session's `QueryHeroScriptStat` gives strength to charisma and the aptitude with the life's poison and crippled legs, and the level and alignment as the stored value with the hero's effect list laid over it (GS3, ARC-AI-2750) (Tempus Fugit, the follower bonus and the background adjustments are not modelled; ledger row 0x4B0490), so a condition can differ from the original where those effects matter. | Original (stat effects partial) |
| PQ5 | the installed dialogs (opt-in census `DialogCodeCensusRealDataTests`, 2026-11-07, 709 DLG files) | Player condition fields: `ch` 478 (115 files), `ma` 418 (40), `ta` 88 (14), `na` 69 (9), `le` 44 (2), `al` 31 (7), `pe` 11 (6); no NPC condition holds one. After admitting them the Player condition fields that parse whole rise from 12,568 to 13,593 and the fields holding a code outside the subset fall from 3,545 to 2,519 (1,026 freed, the plan's number; one field of them goes on to a later damaged character, so 1,025 more parse whole); no field stops on these codes. The `al` action (129 fields) is unchanged. | Original data |

## Dialog hostile check after a reply (ARC-DEV-193)

Source: the SU10 follow-up of the condition codes plan (ARC-RES-091, deleted) (ARC-RES-091), asked of slice C3: what the reply step (0x413130) does after the
reply's action string, and for which strings. Read on 2026-11-07 with `llvm-objdump` by the first reader (the reply step 0x413130–0x41326E, the player-object tests 0x45D8D0 and
the top of kill-on-sight 0x4AE120, and every store to the action interpreter's result local at [esp+0x10] in 0x415BA0, with the code table 0x5A0750 and the jump table 0x41672C
for the case of each store), F1, on top of the earlier rows SU10, DR2 and DR5 and the kill-on-sight rows HS6; `Arcanum.exe` SHA-256 `9bd80511…3678d52`. Every direct branch stays
inside `.text`. The dialog state record is the one of DCC1: +0x08/+0x0C the PC handle, +0x38/+0x3C the NPC handle, +0x17E8 the state.

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| HR1 | reply step (0x413130) | In this order: states 4 to 9 return at once; the can-speak test (0x4AD800, flags 0) of the NPC toward the PC refuses with state 1; state 3 decodes the stored response (0x417590) and skips the string, otherwise the picked reply's action string runs (0x415BA0, ledger SU13 and the rows of this section); a result of 0 returns (no check, no transition from here); the next line is taken from the reply tables. Then the **hostile check**: 0x45D8D0 on the NPC (a null object or hit points at 0 or below count as dead) nonzero skips it; otherwise kill-on-sight toward the PC (0x4AE120 with the NPC and the PC handles, HS6 and the will-kos script) nonzero makes the NPC speak a generated line (0x4185F0, race line group 1000, into the NPC's line buffer), sets state 4 and returns. When the check passes, the transition switch runs (0x414810, a 30-way switch on the reply's kind). | Original |
| HR2 | action interpreter result (0x415BAD, the stores 0x415EBF, 0x4162A4, 0x41635C, 0x4163B1, 0x4164B9, 0x41651B, 0x41656B, 0x4165A5, 0x4166EA) | The result local starts at 1 (0x415BAD; a null string pointer returns 1 at once, 0x415BC2, and an empty string leaves the local as it is, 0x415BDC → 0x41671F). Nine stores change it and each is in the case of a code that moves the dialog itself: `fl` (state 4) stores 0; `jo` and `uw` (state 0, both of their paths) store 0; `et` (its success tail, 0x4166DC, clears the state; the result store at 0x4166EA is 0 on every path) stores 0; `ce` (state 5), `su` (state 7), `ii` (state 8) and `ri` (state 9) store 0. No case stores 1 again and the loop goes on after a store, so the string returns 0 when any of these codes ran, wherever it stands, and 1 otherwise: every other code (`co`, `re`, `lf`, `gf`, `qu`, `mm`, `al`, `in`, `rp`, `$$` and the rest) leaves it at 1. An unknown code ends the string with the result so far (DS1). | Original |
| HR3 | what the check covers | So the hostile check runs for every reply whose string ran to the end without `fl`, `jo`, `uw`, `et`, `ce`, `su`, `ii` or `ri`, whatever the reply leads to: a plain reply to a node, an exit, a return to the caller's script line, a barter option (in the original it is checked a second time in the state-3 pass, below), a generated insult option (the insult, DR5, comes after it) and the single fallback exit. It also runs for a reply with no action at all, so an NPC that is hostile toward the PC at the pick (an attack from `co`, a change of the reaction or alignment by the string, the NPC's own state) ends the talk with the generated line before the next node, the exit, the barter window or the insult. `co` runs its attack event at the string's end (CA2) and the string still returns 1 unless it holds one of the codes above, so a `co` reply is checked after the event: the 76 installed Player fields that are a bare `co` end this way when the attack made the NPC kill-on-sight toward the PC; a `co` followed by `fl` returns 0 and is not checked. | Original |
| HR4 | the rule settings, and what is not modelled | Nothing in HR1–HR3 is a bug of the game: Original and Steamwoven are the same and there is no switch. Not read or not built in this task: the can-speak refusal of the pick (SU9), the stored-response state 3 (the barter option's second check: transition case 3 of 0x414810 stores state 3 and 0x567E8B enters this step again, whose state-3 path reaches the same check, 0x4131AB → 0x413204 — the port is asked once here), the same check at the dialog start (DR2, the start of a talk, still builds no refusal), the text of the generated group-1000 line (the host does not load the race-line files for a floating line; the conversation ends as `HostileRefusal` with the NPC's previous line, as it does for an insult that ends the talk) and the will-kos script (the viewer's AI host answers kill-on-sight without it, its `AllowsKillOnSight` is true). | Original (partly modelled) |

## Dialog item conditions (ARC-DEV-194)

Source: slice C6 of the condition codes plan (ARC-RES-091, deleted) (ARC-RES-091), the condition half (the non-negative `in` action is the next slice). Question: which holdings do `in` and
`ni` (cases 11 and 23 of the condition interpreter 0x4150D0) test, in which order, and what does the item search by name (0x462480) count as holding? Read again on 2026-11-07 with `llvm-objdump`
(the cases 0x4153A6–0x415457 and 0x415775–0x41582C, the pass and fail tails 0x415AD8 and 0x415AED, the search 0x462480–0x462533, the substitute call 0x45F650 by its uses), F1, on top of the earlier
rows DCC6, SG2 and BT11; `Arcanum.exe` SHA-256 `9bd80511…3678d52`. Every direct branch stays inside `.text`. "The PC" and "the NPC" are the dialog state record's handles (DCC1 note).

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| IH1 | conditions `in` (0x4153A6) and `ni` (0x415775) | The first operand n: n ≥ 0 is the party branch, n < 0 the NPC branch with the name −n (the negation keeps `int.MinValue`, which no item has). Party: the search on the PC (0x4153B3) passes at once when it finds an item; otherwise the PC's follower list (0x4411C0, freed after at 0x4410E0) is walked in order and the search runs on each follower until one finds it; the condition passes when one did. NPC: the search on the NPC (0x415420) passes when it finds the item; otherwise the substitute container is asked (0x45F650 on the NPC): none fails, else the search runs on the container and its answer decides. `ni` has the same two branches with the pass and the fail tails exchanged (0x415AED on a find, 0x415AD8 otherwise; `ni −n` with no substitute passes when the NPC does not hold the item). A pass goes on to the next operation of the field (0x415AD8), a fail ends the field with 0 (0x415AED). | Original |
| IH2 | item search by name (0x462480) | A null handle answers none. The object type (field 0x14F) selects the list: 2 (a container) the fields 0x3B/0x3C, 15 (the PC) and 16 (an NPC) the fields 0xED/0xEE; any other type answers none. The loop takes each entry of the list by its index (0x407610) and compares the entry's name field (object field 0x16) with the number; the first equal entry is the answer. Nothing is skipped: a worn item and the entries of a stack count like any other, a nested container's contents do not. | Original |
| IH3 | the rule settings, and what is not modelled | Nothing in IH1–IH2 is a bug of the game: Original and Steamwoven are the same and there is no switch. The session lists the hero's holdings as the starting kit, the items taken from the world and the made items, and an NPC's or container's as its current list (`QueryHolderItems`); the hero's coins are a count there, not a listed item, so a name asked for that only a gold pile carries is not found for the hero. The follower list is the session's direct followers in its array order, and the substitute container is the viewer's rule (BT11: no leader at T0 and not led by the hero now, the container within 20 tiles of the NPC's live cell, where the shown map's AI host has it, else its map row; ARC-WORLD-2357). | Original (holdings partly modelled) |
| IH4 | the installed dialogs (opt-in census `DialogCodeCensusRealDataTests`, 2026-11-07, 709 DLG files) | Player condition fields: `in` 671 (112 files), `ni` 410 (87); no NPC condition holds either. After admitting them the Player condition fields that parse whole rise from 13,593 to 14,649 and the fields holding a code outside the subset fall from 2,519 to 1,463 (1,056 freed, the plan's number); no field stops on these codes. The `in` action (606 fields) is unchanged: its negative form is the world or inventory port's, its non-negative form stays outside the subset. | Original data |

## Dialog item hand-over (ARC-DEV-199)

Source: slice C6b of the condition codes plan (ARC-RES-091, deleted) (ARC-RES-091), the non-negative `in` action (case 9 of the action interpreter 0x415BA0, 0x415FED–0x4161AD). Question: what exactly
does the action do when the operand is not negative, in which order are the holders searched, and what happens when the item does not end with the NPC? Read again on 2026-11-07 with `llvm-objdump` (the
case, the item search 0x462480, the follower list 0x4411C0 and its release 0x4410E0, the transfer 0x4617F0 and the holder re-read 0x4612A0 by their calls), F1, on top of DAC5; `Arcanum.exe` SHA-256
`9bd80511…3678d52`. Every direct branch stays inside `.text`. "The PC" and "the NPC" are the dialog state record's handles (DCC1 note).

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| TK1 | action `in` n ≥ 0 (0x415FED) | The first operand n (read with `atoi` before the case, DS1): a negative one takes the other branch (DAC5). The item search by name (IH2, 0x462480) runs on the PC; when it finds nothing the PC's follower list (0x4411C0, released at 0x4410E0) is walked in its order and the search runs on each follower until one finds the item; when no one holds it the action does nothing and the string goes on (0x4166EE). The holder found, the item is transferred to the NPC (0x4617F0: the item and the NPC handle), and the item's holder is read again (0x4612A0). | Original |
| TK2 | the transfer did not end with the NPC (0x416091–0x4160F7) | When the holder read after the transfer is the NPC the action is over. Otherwise 0x45DDA0 answers the NPC's leader (the PC at the top of the NPC's chain of leaders, 0 for none; field 0x119 climbed through leaders that are not the PC): with none the item is destroyed (0x43CCA0); with a leader the item is detached (0x466E00) and placed at the NPC's location (0x466E50 with the location of the NPC's handle, 0x406DA0). So a refused transfer to a leaderless NPC destroys the item, and the string goes on in both cases; the action does not clear the string's result, so the reply's hostile check (HR1–HR3) still follows it. | Original; plausibly intended (an item with no owner to leave it with is destroyed); built for the PC's party as the leader by ARC-DEV-238 (TK3) |
| TK3 | the rule settings, and what is not modelled | **ARC-DEV-238 builds the TK2 fallback.** A move the host's insert check refused (`InsertRefused`; the owner's decision 2026-11-07: as the original in both modes), or the item's remove check refused (`ItemNotDroppable`, the no-drop flag, ARC-WORLD-1283: 0x461CA0 tests it first), is settled by `GameSessionOwner.SettleRefusedHandover`: an NPC the PC leads has the item taken out of the hero's list and laid on its cell (`PlaceHeroItem`: a junk pile or a new one, the decay timer, the hero's placed items), any other NPC destroys it (`DestroyObject`); both are `Applied` and the string goes on. An NPC that follows another NPC, which the original counts as led when the chain ends at the PC, counts as having no leader here (the session models the PC's party only), and the NPC's cell is its map row's in the viewer (where the AI walked it, or a carried companion's home map, is not read: a known limit of a path the viewer does not reach). The fallback runs no remove-item or wield-off event, so an item with such a script or an item effect is `Rejected` as before. The viewer's insert check for an NPC accepts every item, so its talks never reach the fallback; a host with a refusing check does. The session moves only the hero's own item (starting kit, then taken items, then made items, in that order, the first of that name), through the same move as a barter sale or a gift (`GiveItemToNpc`, the host's insert check for the NPC, which the viewer lets accept everything); a refusal of the move that is not the host's (a version change, an unknown NPC, a script or item effect the session cannot run, a full list) is `Rejected` and fails the conversation, and no item is placed or destroyed. The hero's single list in the original is three lists here (the order is an approximation). An item held by a follower and not by the PC is also `Rejected` (the session has no move from one NPC's list to another's), except when the NPC itself is the first follower holding it: the item is with it already, nothing moves. Nobody holding the item is `Applied` with no change, as in TK1. The Original and Steamwoven settings are the same here; the TK2 fallback is not a setting because the original's rule is the same in both. | Original (partly modelled) |
| TK4 | the installed dialogs (opt-in census `DialogCodeCensusRealDataTests`, 2026-11-07, 709 DLG files) | `in` action fields: 383 in Player replies and 223 in NPC lines. After admitting the non-negative form 328 more Player action fields and 42 more NPC action fields parse whole; no field stops on `in` at a form or outside the subset (the fields that still stop do so at later codes outside it). | Original data |

## Dialog companion state and actions (ARC-DEV-195)

Source: slice C7 of the condition codes plan (ARC-RES-091, deleted) (ARC-RES-091), its first part: the conditions `fo` and `wt` and the actions `wa`, `lv`, `sc` and `so` (the condition `pa` and
the action `nk` are the next part). Question: which party and NPC-flag facts do the conditions test, and what do the actions change? Read again on 2026-11-07 with `llvm-objdump` (the condition cases 0x415698
and 0x415934 and their pass and fail tails 0x415AD8 and 0x415AED, the action cases 0x4162AD–0x416311, the wait 0x4AA7A0, the stop-waiting 0x4AA8C0, the leave 0x45DFC0 and its tail 0x45E040, the leader getter
0x45DE00, the flag setters 0x45F5C0 and 0x45F600), F1, on top of DCC8, DAC4 and DAC8; `Arcanum.exe` SHA-256 `9bd80511…3678d52`. Every direct branch stays inside `.text`. "The PC" and "the NPC" are the dialog
state record's handles (DCC1 note); "the flag word" is the NPC object's field 0x118.

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| CF1 | conditions `fo` (0x415698) and `wt` (0x415934) | `fo v`: the NPC's leader (0x45DE00: for an object of type 0x10 the field 0x119, else none) is compared with the PC's handle. `wt v`: bit 0x20 of the flag word (jilted). For both, v 1 passes when the fact holds, v 0 when it does not, and any other v passes without a test (the pass tail 0x415AD8; the fail tail 0x415AED returns 0). A leader that is the PC counts while the NPC waits too (a waiting NPC keeps the PC as leader, CF2). | Original |
| CF2 | action `wa` (0x4162AD → 0x4AA7A0) | The NPC and its operand-less call: nothing happens unless the object is of type 0x10 (an NPC) and a game-mode byte (0x62AC6C) does not forbid it (bit 0 set with bit 1 clear returns); an NPC whose WAIT bit 0x8 is already set returns with no change. Otherwise the leader is read (0x45DE00) and the wait timer is decided: no timer when the leader's stat 7 test (0x4B0EE0) is nonzero (the extraordinary-charisma case) or the NPC's spell flags (field 0x14) hold the mind-control bit 0x8000000; else the NPC's wait index (field 0x12C) is raised by one when the leader has Persuasion training (0x4C60C0, skill 11) and, below 6, a timer event of kind 0x11 is queued from the table at 0x5B5088 (0x45B800). Then the NPC is forced out of the follower list (0x45DFC0 with the flag 1), the PC is set as its leader again (0x45DE50) and bit 0x8 of the flag word is set. The ledger rows OL23 and OL25 hold the timer rules the session runs; the dialog action is the same wait as the wait order. An NPC with no leader reaches the same steps with a null leader (the null-leader case is read in CF8: the end state sets the WAIT bit, CF6). | Original |
| CF3 | action `lv` (0x4162C2 → 0x45DFC0 with the flag 1) | With no leader nothing happens (the function returns 1 at 0x45E029). The flag 1 skips both refusal tests of the unforced path (the leader's stat 7 test 0x4B0EE0 and the mind-control test 0x459040), then the stop-waiting 0x4AA8C0 runs (for a waiting NPC it clears bit 0x8, clears the leader, removes the wait timer event and joins it again as the unwait does, 0x45DE90) and the follower removal 0x45E040 follows (the NPC leaves the leader's follower array, its leader is cleared, bit 0x400 of the flag word is cleared). The jilted bit 0x20 is not touched: a dialog leave does not jilt (the loyalty and wait-timeout paths do). The session's forced leave does the same. | Original |
| CF4 | actions `sc` (0x4162E7 → 0x45F5C0) and `so` (0x4162FC → 0x45F600) | `sc` clears bit 0x10 of the NPC's flag word when it is set, `so` sets it when it is clear (the spread-out flag, OL8); both read no operand and act in any standing. `so` also starts the spread-out goal (goal 69, the walk away from the leader) right after it sets the bit, through the starter 0x436960 with the NPC and its leader (the earlier rows OL8 and the AI notes); `sc` starts nothing. Read again in the exe export for ARC-SCRIPT-1135: the start is inside the `if` of the clear bit, so a flag already set starts nothing; the leader is read by 0x45DE00 (null for an NPC with none, not followed). Modelled: the dialog port's callback starts goal 69 (toward the hero) on a follower the AI host runs, only when the flag was newly set and the hero leads the NPC. | Original |
| CF5 | the result of the string | None of the four actions stores 0 in the string's result (HR2 lists the codes that do), so the reply's hostile check still follows a string that holds them. | Original |
| CF6 | the rule settings, and what is not modelled | Nothing in CF1–CF5 is a bug of the game: Original and Steamwoven are the same and there is no switch. Modelled since ARC-SCRIPT-1136: `wa` on an NPC that is not in the party (the original sets the WAIT bit on an NPC without a leader, CF8; what the bit does is CF9; the session holds it as a wait flag on the free record, CF10). Not modelled: the wait timer of an NPC the host has no wait index for (a speaker that is no roster companion cannot be in the party anyway), the mode byte of CF2 (read as allowing it). The goal 69 that `so` starts (CF4) is started by the dialog action for a follower the AI host runs (ARC-SCRIPT-1135); not modelled: an NPC that is not led by the hero (the original reads its leader, the null-leader case is not followed), a speaker no AI host runs (the flag only), and the viewer's own spread-out order, which sets the flag only and does not start the goal yet. The starter's own branch for an equal goal already queued is left to the table's duplicate rule (the original, 0x436960, refuses at once when the queued goal is 56, or is not 8 or 61, and when it is 8 or 61 refuses only while the leader is under 8 tiles away, else interrupts and starts): a queued walk-near run of a leader 8 or more tiles away refuses here and not there. A follower that joined on this map after the host was built is not run by it, so `jo` and `so` in one talk set the flag and start no goal (#1423). The conditions accept the operands 0 and 1; another operand (which the source passes untested) and a `+` sign stay outside the subset or are damage, as for `me`. A number after `wa`, `lv`, `so` or `sc` is read and ignored by the source; the grammar keeps the stray-number rule of `fp` and stops at it. | Original (partly modelled) |
| CF7 | the installed dialogs (opt-in census `DialogCodeCensusRealDataTests`, 2026-11-07, 709 DLG files) | `fo` appears in 2 Player condition fields (1 file), `wt` in none; the actions `wa` 49, `lv` 83 (80 in replies, 3 in NPC lines), `so` 47 and `sc` 49 fields. After admitting them no field stops on these codes outside the subset; two `sc` action fields stop at a form of the strict grammar (the free text "script-walk away" in a reply's action field, which the source's scanner reads for the codes `sc`, `ri` and `wa`). The fields held back by a code outside the subset fall from 1,463 to 1,237. The per-code counts are the census's raw code scan, which also reads codes out of free text, so the two free-text fields count under `sc` and probably under `wa` as well. | Original data |
| CF8 | `wa` on an NPC with no leader (0x4AA7A0 → 0x4B0EE0, 0x4C60C0, 0x45B800, 0x45DFC0, 0x45DE50; read again for ARC-SCRIPT-1136, 2026-11-13) | The guards are the NPC's own (type 0x10, the mode byte, bit 0x8 clear) and do not test the leader. A null leader (0x45DE00 returns the field 0x119, null for an NPC that follows nobody) goes to the leader's stat 7 test (0x4B0EE0) and persuasion training (0x4C60C0, evaluated only when the stat test is 0 and the mind-control bit is clear), which read the type field of the null handle's pool slot with no check (OM1: turning a handle into an object pointer checks nothing); what slot 0 holds was not traced, so the two answers are an inference (both 0: not a PC or an NPC). On that inference a timer event of kind 0x11 is queued at the table entry of the NPC's wait index (no persuasion step) while the index is below 6, unless the NPC's spell flags hold the mind-control bit. Then the forced leave 0x45DFC0 returns 1 and does nothing (a null leader, CF3), the leader field is written null (it already was) and bit 0x8 is set. End state: the WAIT bit set, no leader, a wait timer queued by the wait index; no step in this set touches the follower array, so the NPC is outside it as it was (derived). | Original (F1; the null-slot read is an inference) |
| CF9 | what the WAIT bit does on an NPC with no leader (0x4150D0 condition case 0x1c, 0x4AA990, 0x4AA8C0, 0x45DE90, 0x4A9F10, 0x4AC380, 0x4AD4D0) | The dialog `wa` condition reads bit 0x8 of the NPC's own flag word and nothing else, so after the action `wa 1` passes and `wa 0` fails until the NPC is led again and unwaited. The timer's handler 0x4AA990 runs only for a non-null leader, so at expiry nothing happens at all (no jilting, no reaction change, the bit stays). The stop-waiting 0x4AA8C0 (the `uw` action, case 0x15) returns at once for a null leader, so neither `uw` nor `lv` nor `jo` clears the bit (the other dialog actions were not enumerated); `lv` does nothing without a leader (CF3). The join 0x45DE90 neither tests nor clears bit 0x8: the NPC that is then joined stands in the follower array with the PC as leader and the bit still set (the loyalty step 0x4AC380 returns early for a set bit; 0x4AD4D0, a gate on a leader's follower, returns 0 for one). The AI reads the leader of a waiting NPC as none (0x4A9F10), which a leaderless NPC has anyway, so nothing else moves. | Original (F1) |
| CF10 | the session, and what is modelled (ARC-SCRIPT-1136, issue #1136, schema 96) | **The census** (opt-in `DialogWaCensusRealDataTests`, 2026-11-13): the installed dialogs hold 47 Player replies with the `wa` action in 32 files, every one operand-less with the condition `wa 0` and none with `fo 1`. A static walk from the NPC lines no reply targets, through replies not gated by `fo 1`, `wa 1` or `pa`, reaches 20 of them (an upper bound that ignores which line a talk starts at). Starting at the NPC's own script (the file of the same name; a talk starts at line 0) and taking the false branch of the "attachee follows the triggerer" test (condition 18) for a free NPC, with every other condition free, 5 replies in 3 files are reachable: the two of the Surly Virgil dialog (the script is attached to Virgil while he follows, and the dialog's own `lv` leaves him with the script still on him), the two of Franklin Payne's follower hub (reachable again once the hub's local flag is set and the leader is lost without his `lv`) and the one of the mind-control dialog (that NPC is led by the PC, so it is no free case). The other 42 need a leader (the script sends a free NPC to a different line), so only a leaderless NPC that still holds a follower's dialog script is offered one. **Built:** the party record carries a wait flag (`SessionCompanion.WaitFlag`, the WAIT bit of an NPC the PC does not hold as waiting; schema 96, upgrade step 95 adds `false` to every record). `wa` on a free NPC sets it (the NPC stays free and outside the array, the Tempus Fugit flag is not touched) and queues the timer of its wait index with no leader read (no Persuasion step, no charisma test; the mind-control test stands, CF8); a set bit makes `wa` a no-change (CF2). The `wa` condition reads the bit alone (a waiting standing, or the flag, CF9). The timer's expiry does nothing for a free NPC (the flag stays, no jilting, no reaction change, CF9). `uw` on a free NPC with the flag does nothing and the flag stays; `lv` on a free NPC does nothing (CF3). A join of an NPC with no leader keeps the flag (0x45DE90 never writes bit 0x8 and calls the leave 0x45DFC0 only for a non-null leader): the NPC then stands in the array with the bit set, where the loyalty step returns early (0x4AC380), the AI reads it as waiting, `wa` changes nothing and `uw` clears the bit and joins again at the end of the array; a leave, a forced unwait or another join on the follower clears it (a join of an NPC the PC leads runs the leave 0x45DFC0 first, which runs the forced unwait 0x4AA8C0; so the join of a waiting NPC clears the bit, as the session already did). The follow drive of the viewer also treats a follower with the flag as waiting. **Not modelled:** the original keeps the pending timer of a free NPC that then joins, and its expiry would jilt the follower in the array (the leader cleared with the NPC still in the array): the join removes the timer here; the other readers of the bit for a follower that carries it (0x4AD4D0, the order paths); an unforced leave of a flag carrier or waiting NPC whose follow check refuses (the original's unwait stays refused, so the NPC leaves with the bit still set; the session clears it) (issue [#1492](https://github.com/Simarglok/arcanum-steamwoven/issues/1492)). | Original (F1; the null-slot read of CF8 is an inference) |

## Dialog NPC counters (ARC-DEV-196)

Source: slice C8 of the condition codes plan (ARC-RES-091, deleted) (ARC-RES-091): the condition and the action `lc`. Question: which counter does `lc i v` read and write, how wide is
it, and what happens to an index beyond the four counters? Read again on 2026-11-07 with `llvm-objdump` (the condition case 0x4154CA, the action case 0x4161CC, the counter query 0x444FD0, the flag query
0x444F10 beside it, the counter setter 0x445020, the attachment reader 0x407840), F1, on top of DCC9 and DAC6 (which left the counter's range unread); `Arcanum.exe` SHA-256 `9bd80511…3678d52`. Every
direct branch stays inside `.text`. "The NPC" is the dialog state record's handle (DCC1 note).

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| KC1 | condition `lc` (0x4154CA → 0x444FD0) | The first operand i and the second operand v (the second is read after the first, 0x4167C0, a missing one reads 0). The NPC's script attachment at point 9 (the dialog point) is read through 0x407840 (the object's field 32, the script field of SA1); with none the query answers 0. Otherwise it answers one byte of the attachment's counter word: the word shifted right by 8·i (the shift count is taken modulo 32, so the index wraps every four) and masked with 0xFF. The condition passes when the byte equals v as a 32-bit signed number (the pass tail 0x415AD8, the fail tail 0x415AED), so a v outside 0..255 never matches. `lf` beside it tests the bit i modulo 32 of the flag word the same way. | Original |
| KC2 | action `lc` (0x4161CC → 0x445020) | The second operand v is read after the first (i). When the attachment at point 9 exists, the byte of the counter word at 8·i modulo 32 bits is replaced by the low eight bits of v (the other three bytes stay) and the attachment is written back (0x4F01D0); with no attachment nothing happens. The action does not clear the string's result (HR2). | Original |
| KC3 | the rule settings, and what is not modelled | Nothing in KC1–KC2 is a bug of the game: Original and Steamwoven are the same and there is no switch. ARC-DEV-228 built what ARC-DEV-196 left out: an index outside 0..3 wraps onto counter i modulo 4 (the shift count 8·i is taken modulo 32), also for a negative i, and a second operand left out reads 0 (as for `sk`). Not built: a speaker with no dialog attachment (the viewer's state keeps a counter word for it all the same, as it does for its flags). A DLG that a script started at another point (not the dialog point) and that holds `lc` is refused at the start, as one holding `lf` is: which attachment such a start reads is unread in the executable (ARC-SCRIPT-046, X11), and the counters are the flags' attachment. | Original (partly modelled) |
| KC4 | the installed dialogs (opt-in census `DialogCodeCensusRealDataTests`, 2026-11-07, 709 DLG files) | `lc` appears in 315 Player condition fields (18 files), 195 Player action fields and 18 NPC action fields. After admitting it no field stops on it outside the subset; 22 fields stopped at a form until ARC-DEV-228: 21 name the index 4 ("lc4 3", "lc4 2", "lc4 1": 14 conditions and 7 NPC line actions) which the source wraps onto counter 0, and one reply action ("lc21", index 21 wrapping onto counter 1) has no second operand and reads 0. Since ARC-DEV-228 all 528 fields parse. The fields held back by a code outside the subset fall from 1,237 to 751. | Original data |

## Dialog spell college rank (ARC-DEV-197)

Source: slice C9 of the condition codes plan (ARC-RES-091, deleted) (ARC-RES-091): the condition `sc`. Question: which rank does `sc n v` read, and which rule does it apply? Read again on
2026-11-07 with `llvm-objdump` (the condition case 0x415AAA, the rank getter 0x4B1AB0, the neighbouring `le` case 0x4156DE for comparison), F1, on top of DCC10 (which left the college's range unread);
`Arcanum.exe` SHA-256 `9bd80511…3678d52`. Every direct branch stays inside `.text`. "The PC" is the dialog state record's handle (DCC1 note). `sc` as an action is another code (CF4).

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| CK1 | condition `sc` (0x415AAA → 0x4B1AB0) | The first operand n is the college, the second operand v is read after it (a missing one reads 0, as for `sk`). The getter answers 0 for an object that is neither a PC (type 15) nor an NPC (type 16); otherwise it reads element n of the object's college-rank array (field 0xDF) through the generic array getter, which this task did not read for an index beyond the sixteen colleges. The rule: with v above 0 the rank must be at least v; with v 0 or below, v is negated and the rank must not exceed it (the fail tail 0x415AED, the pass tail 0x415AD8). So `sc n 0` asks for rank 0, where the stat conditions' threshold 0 (`le`, `ch`) is always satisfied. | Original |
| CK2 | the rule settings, and what is not modelled | Nothing in CK1 is a bug of the game: Original and Steamwoven are the same and there is no switch. The session answers from the hero's spell book (`QueryHeroSpellBook`, the ranks 0..5 of the sixteen colleges); an NPC speaker is not asked (the condition reads the PC), and the diagnostic PC without a hero has no spell book, so the code stays outside the subset there. A college outside 0..15 stays outside the subset too; a negative college, a `+` sign and a second operand of the most negative number are damage, as for `sk`. | Original (partly modelled) |
| CK3 | the installed dialogs (opt-in census `DialogCodeCensusRealDataTests`, 2026-11-07, 709 DLG files) | `sc` appears in 158 Player condition fields (16 files), in no NPC condition. After admitting it every one parses; none stops at a form or outside the subset. The fields held back by a code outside the subset fall from 751 to 593. | Original data |

## Dialog follower name and kill (ARC-DEV-227)

Source: slice C7b of the condition codes plan (ARC-RES-091, deleted) (ARC-RES-091): the condition `pa` and the action `nk`. Question: which followers does `pa n` look at, which of them match, and what does `nk` do to whom? Read again on
2026-11-07 with `llvm-objdump` (the condition case 0x41587D, the follower list builder 0x4411C0, the action case 0x416430, the kill 0x45D900), F1, on top of DCC8 and DAC9; `Arcanum.exe` SHA-256 `9bd80511…3678d52`. Every direct branch
stays inside `.text`. "The PC" and "the NPC" are the dialog state record's handles (DCC1 note).

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| PN1 | condition `pa` (0x41587D → 0x4411C0) | The first operand n is the only one. A negative n is negated first and remembers the inversion (n ≥ 0 and a `-0` are not inverted). The list builder copies the PC's follower array (object field 0xF1, the direct followers: a waiting NPC is outside it, OL16) into a linked list. The walk takes each follower in order: one whose spell flags (field 0x14) lack the mind-control bit 0x8000000 and whose name field (0x16) equals the magnitude ends the walk with a match; the rest are skipped. The list is freed. A non-inverted condition passes when there was a match, an inverted one when there was none (the pass tail 0x415AD8, the fail tail 0x415AED). The most negative number stays negative after the negation and is damage here. | Original |
| PN2 | the owner read | The followers come from the session party (`QueryParty().Followers`, the same array); a follower's name number and mind-control bit come from its stored record by identity (the inventory resolver, a travelling companion's home map read on demand; the stored spell flags and name field of its row, as the script query SG3 reads them). A follower whose record cannot be read fails the conversation (a query failure), because the answer would be a guess. | Original (partly modelled) |
| PN3 | the rule settings, and what is not modelled | Nothing in PN1 is a bug of the game: Original and Steamwoven are the same and there is no switch. Not modelled: a mind control spell cast in play (the stored flag is read; the party consent facts hold it false too), a name number of the PC itself, and a follower with no stored record (a script-created or summoned one would make every `pa` fail with a query failure while it is in the party). A `pa` in the NPC's condition column does not exist in the installed data. A `+` sign and a missing operand are damage; the most negative operand is damage. | Original (partly modelled) |
| PN4 | action `nk` (0x416430 → 0x45D900) | No operand. The NPC's handle (the dialog state record's NPC) is passed to the kill, which is a death with no killer (the same routine as the script action kill, [SU6](npc-ai.md): no experience, no killer notification). The action string goes on and the dialog is not ended, so later fields still run; it does not clear the string's result (HR2). The viewer kills the speaker through the session's life domain, like a script's kill, and draws the plain fall; an NPC whose combat values cannot be read refuses (the action then fails the conversation). | Original (partly modelled) |
| PN5 | the installed dialogs (opt-in census `DialogCodeCensusRealDataTests`, 2026-11-07, 709 DLG files) | `pa` appears in 366 Player condition fields (35 files), `nk` in 7 Player reply actions and 2 NPC line actions (4 files). After admitting them every one parses; none stops at a form or outside the subset. The fields held back by a code outside the subset fall from 593 to 218. | Original data |

## Dialog window actions (ARC-DEV-229)

Source: slice C10 of the condition codes plan (ARC-RES-091, deleted) (ARC-RES-091): the actions `ii` and `ri` and the action spelled c-e (the guard test refuses that token). Question: what do they set,
what does the reply UI do with the pending code, and which part of it can the runtime take? Read again on 2026-11-07 with `llvm-objdump` (the action cases 0x41648C, 0x41653E and 0x416578, the generated line 0x413BE0, the reply
handler 0x413130, the reply UI 0x567E30 and its jump table 0x568078), F1, on top of DAC11 and NC1; `Arcanum.exe` SHA-256 `9bd80511…3678d52`. Every direct branch stays inside `.text`. The window contents past the dispatch are unread (F0).
The reply UI record holds the dialog record at offset 8 (0x567E86 passes it to 0x413130), so a UI offset is the dialog offset plus 8: UI +0x10 is the PC (dialog +0x08) and UI +0x40 the NPC (dialog +0x38).

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
