| WN1 | actions c-e 28, `ii` 32, `ri` 33 (0x41648C, 0x41653E, 0x416578 → 0x413BE0) | Each composes a generated NPC line (0x413BE0, the same call as `su`), sets the dialog's pending code to 5, 8 and 9 and makes the action interpreter return 0, so the later fields of the string still run (0x4166EE) and the reply handler returns before the hostile check, the insult and the target node, as for `su` (NC1, HR2). The first operand is read and ignored. The last window action of a string decides the code. The reply handler returns at once for a dialog already in a pending code 4..9 (0x41313E..0x41316E). | Original |
| WN2 | reply UI (0x567E30, jump table 0x568078 by the pending code) | The code selects the case. For 5, 8 and 9, and only when the dialog's PC is the local player (0x40D9F0 on the PC), the UI clears the dialog text (0x553370) and opens a window: 5 the character sheet and editor of the dialog's NPC (0x5597C0 with the NPC's handle and mode 2: CS2 and CP1; the first argument is the critter the sheet shows, so a follower's "show me your abilities" reply shows the follower's sheet, not the PC's), 8 the item **identify** window (the interface callback 0x4EE550 → 0x460DE0 → the slot at +0x160 of the callback table built by 0x57C6E0, which is 0x57CBC0: the item inventory window in mode 5, [IS1](items.md#item-identify-and-repair-windows-arc-ui-1731), read again on 2026-11-13: the first reading followed the table's other slots), 9 the item **repair** window (the item inventory opener 0x572240 with the PC, the NPC and mode 6). After the window branch of 8 and 9 the UI also closes the dialog (0x5678D0) when the network flag of 0x62AC6C is set. The composed line is never shown. A non-local PC opens nothing. | Original (all three windows open) |
| WN3 | the owner read, and what is not modelled | Nothing in WN1–WN2 is a bug of the game: Original and Steamwoven are the same and there is no switch. The runtime ends the talk for the window as it does for `su` (end kinds `NpcSheetWindow`, `ItemIdentifyWindow`, `ItemRepairWindow`; the last window action decides; a dialog transfer or an insult in the same reply is outside the subset), and the grammar parses the three codes with the optional window port. The viewer builds the NPC sheet since ARC-UI-088 (a read-only sheet of the speaker, mode 2, ledger CHW24; `AreaConversationHost` passes a window port that offers only it); since ARC-UI-1731 the `ii` and `ri` windows are built as a diagnostic list ([IS1–IS7](items.md#item-identify-and-repair-windows-arc-ui-1731), the window port offers all three) and the replies end the talk for them. The names identify and repair are by use. The generated line is not composed (it is never shown). | Original (partly modelled) |
| WN4 | the installed dialogs (opt-in census `DialogCodeCensusRealDataTests`, 2026-11-07, 709 DLG files) | The c-e action appears in 47 Player reply fields (27 files), `ii` in 26 (2 files) and `ri` in 61 (8 files), 134 occurrences in 132 fields; none in an NPC line. With every port the grammar parses all 132 fields; the fields held back by a code outside the subset fall from 180 to 48. Since ARC-UI-1731 the viewer offers all three. | Original data |

## Dialog newspaper queue and NPC origin (ARC-DEV-230)

Source: slice C10 of the condition codes plan (ARC-RES-091, deleted) (ARC-RES-091), the last two action codes of the tables, `np` and `or`. Question: what does each set, where is it
kept, and what does a save hold? Read on 2026-11-07 with `llvm-objdump` (the action cases 0x416477 and 0x416528, the queue module 0x4BF060–0x4BF340, the setter 0x45D2D0), F1, on top of DAC10;
`Arcanum.exe` SHA-256 `9bd80511…3678d52`. Every direct branch stays inside `.text`. The reading window that prints an issue and the daily pop are not modelled here (F0 past the addresses named); ARC-UI-1739 models them, see [NP1–NP8](#newspaper-vendor-and-daily-event-arc-ui-1739).

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| NQ1 | action `np` 27 (0x416477 → 0x4BF140) | The scanner's first number is the issue and the second operand is read by the same reader as for `sk` and `tr` (0x4167C0; a missing one is 0, so is a missing first operand: an installed dialog writes a bare `np`). The queue function is called with both and the string goes on: the result is not cleared and nothing else is set. | Original |
| NQ2 | queue module init (0x4BF060, 0x4BF080), save (0x4BF100) and load (0x4BF0C0) | The queue is a table of 25 words (0x6876D0, zero-filled) and a count (0x6876CC). A new game sets words 0–3 to −1, word 4 (the head, the issue shown now) to 5000 and the count to 5. The save writes the 100 bytes of the table and then the count, and the load reads them back, so the queue is part of the save file. | Original |
| NQ3 | queue add (0x4BF140), look-up (0x4BF1D0) | An issue number below 5000 is first looked up in words 4 to 24 and, if found, nothing happens (the words past the count are 0 and count too: `np 0` finds them while the table has room). With a second operand that is not 0 the words 4 to 23 are copied up one place (24 is lost) and the issue then overwrites word 5, so it goes in behind the head, the head stays, and the count rises by one unless it is 25 already. Without it the issue takes the word at the count and the count rises; once the count is 25 the issue takes word 24. An issue from 5000 on is never looked up, so it queues again every time. | Original |
| NQ4 | daily newspaper event (0x4BF270), pop (0x4BF2C0), default issue (0x4BF330) | An event of kind 27 with a delay of one day (86,400,000 ms) runs the pop (a network flag byte at 0x62AC6C can skip it) and sets itself up again. The pop drops the head: a head below 5000 is removed by moving the whole table down one word from word 0 (the head moves to word 3, the reserved words shift out), any other head by moving the words from word 4 down; when nothing is left behind the head, a default issue (a marker from 5000 on, drawn at random among the numbered texts of the newspaper message file) becomes the head and the count is 5. The shown issue is word 4 (the readers at 0x41A55D, 0x41A625 and 0x4494FB ask for it; 0x41A713 counts the words from word 0 that are not −1). | Original (modelled by ARC-UI-1739: [NP1–NP8](#newspaper-vendor-and-daily-event-arc-ui-1739)) |
| NQ5 | what this build keeps | The session holds the table as the original does (25 words and the count; the `newspaper` section, schema 74; a save from an older build has a new game's queue). A build that cannot pop never writes the state a pop leaves, so the load checks only the table's shape (25 words, a count from 5 to 25); a pop leaves any words behind (NP7). The rule of NQ3 is `GameSessionOwner.QueueNewspaper`. The daily event, the default issue, the vendor's talk and the printed issue came with ARC-UI-1739 ([NP1–NP8](#newspaper-vendor-and-daily-event-arc-ui-1739)); the script side of the queue (a85) with ARC-SCRIPT-1764 ([NP9](#script-actions-queue-newspaper-and-float-newspaper-headline-arc-script-1764)). | Original (modelled) |
| NQ6 | action `or` 31 (0x416528 → 0x45D2D0) | The NPC (the dialog's second participant) and the first operand go to the setter: with no object, or one whose type field (0x14F) is not 16 (an NPC), nothing happens; otherwise the object's field 0x123, the origin, takes the value as read (any 32-bit number, no range test) and the string goes on (result not cleared). The origin picks the reputation effects of the NPC's reaction (RP5). | Original |
| NQ7 | what this build keeps | The origin is the mobile object field `npc.origin` of the NPC's directory entry (schema 74), kept only while it differs from the map data's, as `npc.faction` is. The viewer reads the set origin where it reads the NPC's origin and faction (`AreaConversationHost.Scope`), and the talk in progress follows. A speaker that is not an NPC is left alone, as in the original. | Original |
| NQ8 | the installed dialogs (opt-in census `DialogCodeCensusRealDataTests`, 2026-11-07, 709 DLG files) | `np` appears in 17 Player reply fields and 4 NPC-line fields (6 files), `or` in 4 NPC-line fields (1 file); all 25 parse with the new ports. After this slice no code of the tables is outside the subset: the 23 fields that still stop on a code hold codes the tables do not have (`$2`, `$5`, `BE`, `MM`, `ST` in conditions, `MA`, `TA` in a Player action, `AD` in an NPC line: damaged data the original fails or ends on, DS1). | Original data |

## Newspaper vendor and daily event (ARC-UI-1739)

Source: issue #1739 (the queue of NQ1–NQ5 was written and saved but nothing read it). Question: who consumes the queue, what does the player see, and what does the daily event do? Read on 2026-11-13 in the exports of 1.0.7.4 (the dialog generated states 0x19 to 0x1d of 0x414810 with 0x41A520, 0x41A620, 0x41A700, 0x41A880 and 0x41A8C0; the item maker 0x4BF210; the daily event 0x4BF270, the pop 0x4BF2C0 and the default issue 0x4BF330; the message fetch 0x56CCA0), F1, on top of NQ1–NQ8; `Arcanum.exe` SHA-256 `9bd80511…3678d52`. There is no newspaper window of its own: the issue is a written item read through the reading window of [WR1](../../world/reading-window-runtime.md), and the vendor is a dialog reply.

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| NP1 | the Player reply marker `p:` (state 0x19, 0x41A520) | Picking a reply with the marker runs its action, then the vendor's greeting replaces the NPC line and the replies without leaving the node: the NPC line 4200..4299 and four ways on: buy the issue shown now (PC line 2200..2299), hear its headline (2000..2099), see the back issues (2100..2199) and leave (800..899). | Original |
| NP2 | headline (state 0x1a, 0x41A620; fetch 0x56CCA0) | The shown issue is the queue's word 4. Its headline is the message of that number in the newspaper file with the markup taken off and every line feed made a space (the hook returns the text). The NPC says the headline (a closing full stop is added unless it ends in a full stop, a question mark or an exclamation mark, then a space) followed by a line of 4300..4399; the options are yes (PC line 1..99, to the price) and no (100..199, leave). | Original |
| NP3 | back issues (state 0x1b, 0x41A700) | The words 0 to 3 that are not −1, in table order, each become an option whose text is the issue's headline and whose way on is the price; with some the NPC line is 4500..4599 and the way out the PC's 800..899, with none the line is 4400..4499 and the single answer 600..699. | Original |
| NP4 | price (state 0x1c, 0x41A880 → 0x418A40) | The price screen of the other talks (reaction scale) with the base price 2; yes goes to the payment, no leaves. | Original |
| NP5 | payment (0x418B30) | Too little gold of the PC is the notice of the other talks; otherwise the price goes to the NPC. This build makes the paper first and then takes the gold, so a refused item costs nothing (not visible in the original's screens). | Original |
| NP6 | issue given (state 0x1d, 0x41A8C0; item maker 0x4BF210) | A new newspaper item (prototype 0x36F0 = 14064 of the executable, the project's 14084) is made with the issue as the item's written start line (field 0xCB) and goes into the PC's pack; the NPC's class line 15000 and the PC's class line 1000 close the screen. The printed issue is read by the use path of the reading window (WR1), whose newspaper text starts at the written start line. | Original |
| NP7 | daily event (kind 27, 0x4BF270), pop (0x4BF2C0), default issue (0x4BF330) | The first event is set up when a new game starts, to the next midnight (86,400,000 ms minus the milliseconds of the day), and sets itself up again one day on from its firing time. It pops the queue as NQ4 says; when nothing is left behind the head the default issue is 5000 + 10 × a draw of 0..n, n the count of consecutive headlines 5010, 5020, ... that have text, and the count becomes 5. | Original |
| NP8 | what this build keeps | The pop is `SessionNewspaperState.Popped`; the event is the session's event kind `newspaper.daily` (saved with the other events, restore checks its fields). The chain is armed by the first event pass of a host that has the newspaper texts (a host without them, such as most tests, arms nothing, so the existing event counts are unchanged), to the next midnight of the game clock. The draw is the session's random stream. The vendor is `DialogConversation.OpenNewspaper` with the host's `IDialogNewspaperStandPort` (the queue, the headline and the printed item through the session's item maker and `SetItemWrittenStart`). A pending event that fires before the viewer's reading data has loaded has no headline texts, so a draw it needs gives 5000 (the dialog reads its own copy of the texts). The script actions `queue_newspaper` (a85) and `float_newspaper_headline` (a86) came with ARC-SCRIPT-1764 ([NP9–NP12](#script-actions-queue-newspaper-and-float-newspaper-headline-arc-script-1764)). | Original (partly modelled) |

## Script actions queue newspaper and float newspaper headline (ARC-SCRIPT-1764)

Source: issue #1764 (the leftover of #1739: the queue and the vendor were built, the two script actions that write and read the queue were outside the SCR subset). Read on 2026-11-14 in the exports of 1.0.7.4 (the action executor 0x447220, cases 0x55 and 0x56, with the queue add 0x4BF140, the queue read 0x4BF200, the message hook 0x460800 → 0x56CCA0, the PC getter 0x40DA50, the voice lookup 0x4141E0 and the float callback slot 0x5E2FE8), F1; `Arcanum.exe` SHA-256 `9bd80511…3678d52`. The number of the actions is the executor's case number (85 and 86), as in [the opcode census](../scr-census.md).

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| NP9 | action `queue_newspaper` 85 (0x447220 case 0x55 → 0x4BF140) | The two operands are read as numbers by the number reader of the other actions (0x44BA70) and handed to the queue add with nothing else done; the run goes on. The first is the issue and the second the front flag: the rule is [NQ3](#dialog-newspaper-queue-and-npc-origin-arc-dev-230), the same function the dialog code `np` calls (NQ1), so a script and a dialog queue identically (the second operand is a flag by its value: 0 queues after the count, any other number at the front). No condition on the host: the executor has no per-point restriction. | Original |
| NP10 | action `float_newspaper_headline` 86 (case 0x56, census anomaly X9) | The object is operand slot 1 (kind byte and value at +5 and +0x10 of the clause; read by 0x44B960, a group's first object), slot 0 is never read: the editor's signature puts the object in slot 0, the one installed clause has a number kind there and the triggerer in slot 1. The text is the head issue's headline (the queue's word 4 through 0x4BF200, the message hook, the markup and line feeds as [NP2](#newspaper-vendor-and-daily-event-arc-ui-1739)); when it has a length and does not end in a full stop, a question mark or an exclamation mark a full stop is added, then one space is added (an empty headline is left as it is). The text is handed with the object, the PC getter's handle (0x40DA50: the player character, refetched when the cached one is stale), the voice lookup's output (0x4141E0) and the run's first object to the float callback (0x5E2FE8), the one the float line (a23, [FL5](#talk-commands-arc-script-050)) uses. The headline is the same punctuated text as the vendor's headline line. | Original |
| NP11 | what the admission and the interpreter keep | S59 is S58 plus the two actions ([`ScrAdmissionSet.S59`](../../scripting/scr-dispatcher-runtime.md)). The admission checks operand slot 1 of action 86 as an object and leaves slot 0 unread (X9; the catalogue keeps the editor's signature). The interpreter hands both on as `ScrNewspaperRequest` effects in action order: for a85 the two operands as read, for a86 the object (none for an absent one: nothing is requested, as the callback gets no object to float over). The queue's head cannot change inside a run (the add never touches word 4), so the effects need no order between kinds. | Original |
| NP12 | what the hosts do | The dialog host (`AreaScriptHost`, all four of its sets) and the use host (`AreaUseScriptHost`: the use set and so the tile, sector, dying and combat-point sets, and the heartbeat set) apply a85 through `GameSessionOwner.QueueNewspaper` (a request the session refuses for a limit, the revision budget, stops the later steps of the apply as the other session requests do) and float the a86 headline through their float list (`TakeFloats`), the text read before anything applies from the module's newspaper file through the host's content (opened the first time a run needs it). The dialog host commits the headline floats before the rest of the apply, as it does the float line's (a run it refuses later still floats); the use host commits them after its apply succeeds. The newspaper file is read through a content stack of its own (as the vendor's talk reads it), so the content's changed-file check does not cover it. A host with no newspaper file, or a head issue without a message, floats nothing (the original's display is handed an empty text; the display itself is F0, so an empty float is taken as drawing nothing) and the run goes on. Not admitted: the thrown-item host (S33 subset of its own), the item script host and the slide scripts (none holds a newspaper action in the installed data). The PC's gender and name tokens are not read: the headline is plain text, as the callback gets it. | Original (display F0) |

## Script print line in the message window and the wards gate of script 1643 (ARC-DEV-309, B21)

Source: bug B21 (the entrance to the elves' town gives no reaction to a click), [triage T6](../open-bugs.md). Question: why does script 1643 end at its line 2, and what does the original show for the line it prints? Read on 2026-11-08 with `llvm-objdump` (the condition evaluator 0x4451B0 with its jump table 0x44714C, cases 4 (0x445355) and 43 (0x446B93); the value reader 0x44BA70 with its jump table 0x44BB34; the global flag reader 0x444CF0; the print line action 0x44B390; the callback caller 0x460630; the interface table builder 0x57C6E0; the message post 0x550750 → 0x552770), F1, on top of IS1–IS4 and ML1–ML2; `Arcanum.exe` SHA-256 `9bd80511…3678d52`. Every direct branch stays inside `.text`. The installed scripts and dialogs were read with the project's own reader (identifiers and numbers only; no text is recorded here).

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| PW1 | print line (0x44B390 → 0x460630 → table slot 0x5E86D8 = 0x550750 → 0x552770) | The action's second number is stored as the first field of the entry it builds on the stack (the entry's type) and the built text is its second field; that entry goes through the interface callback, which is the message window's post (the table built at 0x57C6E0 holds 0x550750 at offset 0x80, and 0x45FD40 copies it to 0x5E86D8). So the second number is the window's entry type of ML1–ML2: 4 and 5 stored (4 plays sound 3004), 6 to 12 never stored, drawn only in the message mode and only when more than 3,000 ms of real time passed since the last stored entry. This closes ARC-RES-055 U1. | Original |
| PW2 | the installed scripts (project reader, 2026-11-08) | 189 print line actions in the installed scripts: 186 name type 6, one type 0, one type 1, one reads a counter. The viewer posts types 4, 5 and 6; the others (0 to 3, 7 to 13) need data a print line does not carry or drawings the viewer lacks (ML5) and stay NOT READY. Superseded in part by [PY1–PY4](#print-line-types-beyond-4-to-6-arc-script-1118-pw2-follow-up): types 1, 2, 3 and 12 are posted too. | Original (partly modelled) |
| PW3 | condition 4 (0x445355), value reader (0x44BA70, kind 4 at 0x44BB1C), flag reader (0x444CF0) | Condition 4 reads both operands with the value reader and takes the true clause only when they are equal; operand kind 4 returns the global flag as 0 or 1. Script 1643 (the entrance to the elves' town) therefore takes its teleport only while global flag 2365 is 1; otherwise its line 1 prints a one-line refusal about wards (type 6) and line 2 ends the run with skip default, as the log of the bug report shows. The refusal is the original's rule, not a lost condition. | Original |
| PW4 | writers of flag 2365 (project scan of all installed scripts and dialogs, 2026-11-08) | Only the first line of the death scripts 1805, 2137 and 2173 assigns it (1); no dialog action and no other script names it, and the guard's dialog does not. In the original the flag therefore opens with one of those three deaths; the dying point (12) has no host in Steamwoven, so the flag can not be set in play yet (ARC-DEV-312). | Original (open: dying scripts) |
| PW5 | what this build keeps | A scripted use or tile or sector run that applied hands its printed lines with their types to the viewer (`AreaScriptedUse.Printed`), which posts types 4, 5 and 6 to the message window by `AreaScriptMessages`; the status line keeps showing the text as before. Before ARC-DEV-309 the text appeared only in the diagnostic status line, so the click looked like no reaction. | Original (partly modelled) |

## Print line types beyond 4 to 6 (ARC-SCRIPT-1118, PW2 follow-up)

Source: issue #1118 (PW2 left types 0 to 3 and 7 to 13 NOT READY). Question: what does the original's entry hold for a print line, so which of those types draw something the viewer can reproduce? Read on 2026-11-12 in the exports of 1.0.7.4 (print line 0x44B390 with its listing, the draw 0x5529C0, the post 0x552770, the body lookups 0x4C3CC0 and 0x4C4170, the schematic reader 0x56DBD0) and by the project reader over the installed scripts.

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| PY1 | print line entry (0x44B390, the listing from 0x44B453 to 0x44B4BE) | The entry the call hands to the message window (0x460630) has four fields on the caller's stack: the type (the second operand through the value reader 0x44BA70), the text (the buffer the line step built), the number at +8 written as 0, and the number at +0xC never written by this function. So a print line carries no number of its own: a type that draws a number draws 0 and one that reads +0xC reads whatever the stack held. With the network flag (0x62AC6C bit 0) on, the attachee's player slot is looked up (0x4A2B10): no slot posts nothing, slot 0 posts locally, another slot sends the entry to 0x4EDA60; single player never takes that branch. | Original |
| PY2 | the draw's cases (0x5529C0), post filter (0x552770) | By type with the entry above: 1 the title (the line's text) then `intgame.mes` 13 ("You have absorbed %d unit(s) of poison.") formatted with 0 in the red text font, icon 439, sound 3006, stored; 2 the title then the curse body of number 0, 3 the title then the blessing body of number 0 (the body lookups 0x4C3CC0 and 0x4C4170 give an empty text for a number below 50), icons 440 and 441, sounds 3003 and 3002, stored; 12 the transient text's layout (title and body at a line break, else the whole) with icon 444, never stored, shown only in the message mode and more than 3 s after the last stored entry, and without the second layout of types 4 to 6 when the text does not fit; 0 the title `intgame.mes` 21, message 11 with 0 and, unless +0xC is −1, message 12 formatted with +0xC (junk; with −1 the entry's own text in the scheme block); 7 the entry's skill or stat name and description for number 0 and, when +0xC is not 0, a right-aligned value line from it (junk); 8 reads fields +0x10 and +0x14 beyond the entry (junk); 9, 10 and 11 the name and description of number 0 of the interface's own tables; 13 the title of schematic number 0 (0x56E9D0) over the entry's own text as the body (the print line names no other number). | Original |
| PY3 | the installed scripts (project reader `ScrPrintLineTypesRealDataTests`, `DAT_ROOT`, 2026-11-12) | 189 print line actions: 186 name type 6 outright, script 1021 (selling stolen goods) line 8 type 0, script 1143 (the Pillar of Truth examine) line 2 type 1, and script 1711 (room number examine) line 0 reads its type from counter 1. No installed script names 2, 3, 12 or a type above 6 outright. | Original |
| PY4 | what this build keeps | `AreaScriptMessages` posts types 1, 2, 3 and 12 with PY1's zero (new entry kinds `PoisonAbsorbed`, `Curse`, `Blessing`, `ScriptHover` with the log's sounds, icons, stored rule and layouts of PY2) in addition to 4 to 6. The type 1 line of script 1143 therefore shows the poison notice for 0 units: the original's own result. Types 0 and 7 to 11 and 13 are NOT READY: 0, 7 and 8 draw junk from the unset field, 9 to 11 and 13 need the interface's per-number description tables for number 0 only, which no installed script reaches (script 1021's type 0 and script 1711's counter-read type are the two live cases: the first stays unposted, the second posts whatever its counter holds when it is 1 to 3, 4 to 6 or 12). | Original (partly modelled) |

## Creature dying script, point 12 (ARC-DEV-312, B21 follow-up)

Source: bug B21 and [PW4](#script-print-line-in-the-message-window-and-the-wards-gate-of-script-1643-arc-dev-309-b21): global flag 2365, which opens the elves' town entrance (script 1643), is set only by the death scripts 1805, 2137 and 2173. Question: when and with which triggerer and victim does the original run a creature's dying script, what does skip default stop, and what do the three scripts need? Read on 2026-11-10 with `llvm-objdump` (one reader, F1) in the retail 1.0.7.4 `Arcanum.exe` (SHA-256 `9bd80511…78d52`, [method](../original-executable-check.md)): the kill function 0x45DA20, its one caller and the dispatcher call 0x441980; the three scripts through the project reader (no text, no bytes kept).

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| DY1 | the callers of 0x45DA20 (a scan of every call in `.text`) | One caller only, inside the damage apply 0x4B4390 (call site 0x4B5344, after the hit points are written and the dead test 0x45D8D0 holds). So a kill by damage runs the dying script there, whatever the damage's source (attack, spell, burn, trap, a script's damage); how a script's kill (action 58) and the poison tick end a creature was not read here (F0: this build asks the script for them too, with no killer). It passes the victim, the killer (the damage context's source, +0x30, the null handle when the damage has none) and a cause code. | Original |
| DY2 | kill 0x45DA20 → dispatcher call at 0x45DA57 | With a null victim it ends at once. Otherwise it first calls 0x4D5620 on the victim (not followed, GT34 step 1), then runs the dispatcher with attachment point 12 (dying): the **killer is the triggerer**, the **victim the attachee**, no extra object, line 0. A zero answer (skip default) returns from the whole function at 0x45DA61: nothing after it runs. A non-zero answer goes on; a destroyed victim (object flag 0x1, which a dying script can set by destroying its attachee) returns next, also with no death processing. The hit points stay as the damage left them. | Original |
| DY3 | what the death processing is (0x45DA20 after the script) | In order: the victim leaves combat mode (0x4B3F20), its death time is stored (field 0xF4), the spell runs it holds end (0x459740), the round's reaction categories refresh (0x4B80E0); for an NPC the decay event is queued, its combat focus is set to the killer and the kill notification runs (0x4AA1B0); then the killer's PC leader gets the kill award (a fifth of the victim's worth) and the kill log (0x4F5330), and the LEADER_KILLING point (20) runs for each follower of that leader (a list walk, 0x4411C0); a victim that is no NPC takes another branch (0x467520); the common tail then runs the party leave (0x45DE00, 0x45DFC0, 0x45DE50, 0x4BAB30), the art reset (0x504100), the dying goal (0x435080, goal 31), the one-day body event (0x45EBE0), the hit-point damage set to 32000 (0x43D530) and 0x4CBC60 with the killer (GT34 holds the same order; its reading of 0x45ED70 and the call list after the kill log was not followed; settled by ARC-MAGIC-1146 [RM7](spell-runs.md#spell-component-remainders-arc-magic-1146): 0x45ED70 is the Combat-Focus Wipe event, 0x45EBE0 the one-day body event). Skip default at point 12 therefore leaves a dead-by-hit-points creature with no death time, still in combat mode, with its spell runs, its worth, its kill notice, its dying goal (no fall animation) and its party membership untouched. | Original |
| DY4 | the installed scripts 1805, 2137, 2173 (project reader, 2026-11-10) | 13, 3 and 4 lines. The first line of each assigns global flag 2365 := 1. None has a skip default action (1); each ends in run default (2). 1805 goes on to ask the killer's object type, adds reputation (72) and loops over everyone in the vicinity (focus 8); 2137 and 2173 test a quest state (condition 6) and set one (action 16). | Original |
| DY5 | what this build keeps | The session's life step asks the host's dying script (`GameSessionOwner.DyingScript`) after the hit points are written and before the death time, the combat mode exit, the spell runs' ending and the wait timer; skip default (or a victim the script destroyed, GT34 step 3) stops them, the dying goal that the step's critter states would start, and the callers' kill award and kill log (`SessionLifeResult.DeathStopped`); a host that throws counts as run default. The order after the script is the original's: combat mode exit, death time, spell runs' ending, wait timer. The killer is named by the caller: the hero for the hero's attack and throw, the attacker for an NPC attack, the spell's damage source for a spell, the trap's killer for a trap, none for a burn, a script's damage or kill. The viewer runs the victim's point-12 script through the shown map's use-script host (`AreaUseScriptHost.RunDying`, set `DyingAdmission`, which holds the quest, reputation, vicinity and dialog commands of the tile set and the kill and toggle of the heartbeat set; 1805, 2137 and 2173 are admitted whole); the slot is kept as map changes under `scr12.*` (no save schema step). A run that is refused or not applied stops nothing. | Original (partly modelled) |
| DY6 | not modelled | The Combat-Focus Wipe event (0x45ED70, queued for a victim that is an NPC: event kind 26 after 600,000 ms, unless 0x405BC0 holds for the object; built by ARC-MAGIC-1146, [RM7](spell-runs.md#spell-component-remainders-arc-magic-1146); the one-day body event is 0x45EBE0, ARC-WORLD-1158), the PC leader's kill-log text for a follower's kill, LEADER_KILLING (point 20, no installed attachment), the dying script of a creature on a map other than the shown one, the hero's death (the PC has no script), and what the viewer does from the life step's died event for a stopped death (its `_deathAnimations` pose, the death sound cue, the "killed" combat text: the attack and AI results do not carry `DeathStopped` yet), the party leave, the 32000 hit-point set and the nested-run guard (a dying script's own kill of another NPC runs that one's script on the same host). A dialog a dying script asks for is not started. | NOT READY |
| DY7 | cause code, focus step and kill notification (0x45DA20 steps after the death time; 0x4B4390 call site; 0x406F20; 0x4AA1B0), read in the exports on 2026-11-12 | The cause code is the kill's fifth parameter and goes to the dying goal's starter 0x435080 as its animation: 7 when the damage flag 4 is set or the damage is at most 20, else 0x11, 0x13 or 0x12 for the damage context's hit location 1, 3 or other (head, leg, body); it is the dying animation of [FA10](combat.md) and the goal's frame (`AiExecutorRules.DyingFrame`), which Steamwoven already starts with it, so nothing more is needed for it. For a victim that is an NPC (object type 0x10) the death processing then sets the victim's combat focus (field 0x11B, whatever its danger state) to the killer, or to none when the damage has none, and runs the kill notification 0x4AA1B0: stop fleeing (0x4AF170: the setter's NONE for FLEE and the flee goals' interrupt), the victim's stop waiting (0x4AA8C0), and, for a killer of object type 0xF (the PC, never a follower), every NPC of the 7 × 7 square around the killer that is alive, has no NO_FLEE, shares the victim's faction and is in COMBAT_FOCUS on the killer gets BACKING_OFF (field 0x118 bit 0x10000000). A victim that is no NPC takes another branch (0x467520) and has no focus step and no notification. The exe reading agrees with [MR](npc-ai.md#npc-ai-morale-arc-res-058) and OL25. | Original |
| DY8 | what this build keeps (ARC-AI-1134) | The life step writes the dead NPC's focus := the killer in the same revision as its death (the hero's id is the hero handle, a killer-less damage clears it; at the danger record budget a victim with no record gets none), after the dying script allowed the death and the death time, the combat mode exit, the spell runs' ending and the wait timer (OL25) are written, and then tells the host (`GameSessionOwner.KillNotice`, which the viewer sets with each AI host it builds for the shown map): `AreaAiHost.NoticeKill` runs `MoraleRules.KillNotification` over its world, applies it with `ApplyKillNotification` (the victim's stop fleeing, BACKING_OFF on the plan's NPCs) and ends the victim's flee goals at level 6 before the dying goal starts. The host runs it for an NPC it runs on the shown map only; elsewhere only the focus write stands. A host that throws counts as no notification; a stopped death (skip default) has neither step. | Original (partly modelled: the decay event and the kill-log text stay DY6) |

## Spell conditions and the spell mastery action (ARC-MAGIC-029, MT21a)

Source: slice MT21a of the [magic plan](../magic-implementation-plan.md) and the [magic audit](../magic-audit-res-103.md): which SCR conditions ask about spells, what the action "give spell mastery in college" does, and what each needs from the host. Read on 2026-11-11 by one reader (level F1) with `llvm-objdump` in the retail 1.0.7.4 `Arcanum.exe` (SHA-256 `9bd80511…78d52`, verified; nothing copied or run): the condition evaluator 0x4451B0 with its jump table 0x44714C (cases 14 at 0x4458D5, 35 at 0x446799, 48 at 0x446DF2, 49 at 0x446E65), the script action interpreter 0x447220 with its jump table 0x44ADBC (type 112 at 0x44A0EE), the operand getters 0x44B4E0 (object list), 0x44B960 (first object) and 0x44BA70 (value), the mastery setter 0x4EF080 and its callees 0x4B1CF0, 0x4B1C70, 0x4B1CB0, 0x4B1AB0, the known test 0x4B1950 and the influence test 0x459380 with its iterators 0x458CF0 and 0x458D90. Behaviour in prose; no bytes.

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| SC1 | obj has body spell, condition 14 (0x4458D5) | The operand's object list is built (0x44B4E0) and every object counts when its object field 20 (`obj_f_spell_flags`, read through the field getter 0x406CA0) has any of the bits 0x3C: body of air (0x4), earth (0x8), fire (0x10) and water (0x20). Invisible (0x1), floating (0x2) and every higher bit do not count. No value operand is read. Every object type has the field, so any object may be asked. The list rule of SG6 then decides (an empty list is false; every object, or one of an "any" focus kind, makes it true). | Original |
| SC2 | obj is influenced by spell, condition 35 (0x446799) → 0x459380 | The value of operand 1 is read once (0x44BA70) and then asked of every object of operand 0: the influence test answers 1 when some active spell run (the 512 slots in order) has the object as its target or holds it in one of its two object lists and its spell is the value; a null handle answers 0, no range test on the spell. Agrees with ledger [AC1](npc-ai.md#npc-ai-casting-host-arc-world-102). The list rule decides. | Original |
| SC3 | knows spell, condition 48 (0x446DF2) → 0x4B1950 | The value of operand 1 is the spell, asked of every object: 0 for a null handle or a spell outside 0..79; otherwise whether the spell's rank (spell % 5 + 1) is at most the rank the object has in the college spell / 5 (0x4B1AB0: a PC's or NPC's stored rank, 0 for every other object). Agrees with ledger [SB5](magic.md#spell-book-arc-magic-004). The list rule decides. | Original |
| SC4 | mastered spell college, condition 49 (0x446E65) → 0x4B1CB0 | The value of operand 1 is compared for equality with the college the object mastered (field 0xDF index 16 of a PC or NPC, −1 when none; −1 for every object that is neither a PC nor an NPC). A value of −1 therefore matches an object with no mastery and every non-critter. The list rule decides. | Original |
| SC5 | give spell mastery in college, action 112 (0x44A0EE) → 0x44B960, 0x44BA70, 0x4EF080 → 0x4B1CF0 | The first object of operand 0 and the college of operand 1 (a number, not range-checked) go to the mastery setter, which runs the local setter 0x4B1CF0 (a PC or NPC with no mastered college and at least five ranks in the college gets it written, SB7; any other object nothing) and, in a network game, sends message 0x6D. The run goes on with the default result. An empty operand reads an uninitialised first slot (the same reading as ledger [GA1](#talk-commands-arc-script-050)). | Original |
| SC6 | what this build keeps | Set S31 holds the four conditions and the action. The interpreter reads the value of operand 1 once before the list is walked (the original builds the list first; nothing observable differs, as building the list cannot stop a run and the value is read in both) and keeps the SG6 list rule; the host answers the four questions through `IScrSpellQueries` (spell flags, influence, known, mastered college) and receives the mastery as one `GiveSpellMastery` object-state request (object and college as read). An object the host does not know stops the run (as for dead); an absent object makes the conditions false and the action does nothing (the project choice for an uninitialised handle). The multiplayer broadcast is not modelled. The host wiring must give request 112 its own branch: the area use host's request chains treat any unhandled change as a toggle of state. **No host answered the questions when this row was written** (ARC-MAGIC-029); the host wiring (spell book and flags for the PC and NPCs, the spell runs, the mastery write through `MasterHeroCollege`) came with ARC-MAGIC-030 (SC7–SC9). The census counts 22 installed scripts with condition 14, 6 with 35, 16 with 48, 16 with 49 and 2 with action 112, and S31 admits 20 more installed scripts whole than S30 (1,660 against 1,640). | Original (host wired by SC7–SC8) |
| SC7 | host side (ARC-MAGIC-030, MT21b; project reader, 2026-11-11) | The use host (`AreaUseScriptHost`, set under use, examine, item use, catching thief, tile and sector scripts, heartbeat, taking damage, bust and dying) answers the four questions and the talk host (`AreaScriptHost`, while the shown map's use host answers its queries) forwards them. Condition 14 reads the object's spell flags as the original's field getter does: the row's effective field 20 for any object, and for an NPC the flags the session's spell components wrote over it (`QueryObjectFlags`); the PC's are the written ones. **The body-of-element bits (0x4, 0x8, 0x10, 0x20) were written by no spell component yet when this row was read (superseded: ARC-DEV-358 made the ObjFlag component write them, see CA4)** (the ObjFlag component takes only the hold bits and spoken-with-dead), so a running Body of Fire does not make condition 14 true in the viewer; a bit in an NPC's map data or a written one does. Condition 35 is the influence test over the session's spell runs (`SpellRunRules.UnderInfluence`: the run's target only, as AC1). Conditions 48 and 49 read the object's spell book: the hero's, an NPC's as the session records it, else as its map data stores it (`SpellBookRules.FromFields`); any other object type has rank 0 and no mastery. An object of no loaded row, a book outside its ranges and a session without a hero are unknown and stop the run (as for an absent object). | Original (partly modelled) |
| SC8 | give spell mastery, the write (ARC-MAGIC-030) | Request 112 has its own branch in the use host's refusal pass and apply pass (the generic branch toggles an NPC's state and refuses any other object). The PC gets `MasterHeroCollege` and an NPC of the shown map `MasterNpcCollege` over its map data's advancement; both go through `SpellBookRules.Master` (no mastery yet and five ranks), so a refusal by the rule, a college outside 0..15 or a session without a hero changes nothing and the run goes on, and only a session that cannot take a change (record budget, revision) stops the later steps. A scenery, item or any other object type does nothing, as in the original. Not modelled and left unapplied with the reason named: an NPC of another map, a PC-type row, and an NPC whose map data gives no admissible sheet. The talk host applies request 112 for the PC and for an NPC whose advancement the viewer reads (the same source as its scheme change); any other object is ignored. The multiplayer message 0x6D is not sent. | Original (partly modelled) |
| SC9 | what the installed scripts do now (`AreaScriptSpellTests` real-data check, `DAT_ROOT`) | 46 installed scripts use one of the five commands. Admitted whole by the host sets: use 1, tile and sector (placed) 3, heartbeat 1, talk 1. The rest stop on another command first (first refusal under the talk set: get stat (33) 17 scripts, action 91 in 16, condition 12 in 4, actions 18 and 34 in 2 each, action 44, action 60, condition 1 and condition 31 in 1 each), so this slice makes few scripts newly run in the viewer; those commands belong to later slices. | Data |

## Story and time commands (ARC-SCRIPT-1165, E5-c)

Source: slice E5-c of the [missing-systems plan](../../project/missing-systems-plan.md#e5-script-and-dialog-coverage) and the [script refusal census](../script-refusal-census.md) (ARC-RES-1101): the six conditions and thirteen actions that tell a script about the game's story and clock. Read on 2026-11-12 by one reader (level F1) in the exports of the retail 1.0.7.4 `Arcanum.exe` (SHA-256 `9bd80511…78d52`): the condition evaluator 0x4451B0 (jump table 0x44714C), the script action interpreter 0x447220 (jump table 0x44ADBC), the operand getters 0x44B4E0 (object list), 0x44B960 (first object), 0x44BA70 (value) and 0x44BB50 (store), the quest globals 0x4C51A0, 0x4C51C0 and 0x4C5070, the rumor functions 0x4C58D0, 0x4C5650 and 0x4C5690, the blessing and curse functions 0x4C4280, 0x4C42F0, 0x4C4450, 0x4C3DD0, 0x4C3E40 and 0x4C3FA0 (BC3 to BC5), the clock helpers 0x45A7C0, 0x45A870, 0x45A890, 0x45A8B0, 0x45A8D0 and 0x45ACF0, and the experience calls 0x4C53C0 and 0x45F110. Behaviour in prose; no bytes.

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| SQ1 | daytime, condition 1 (0x4451FD) → 0x45ACF0, 0x45AAA0 | No operands. The game clock is a pair {day, milliseconds into the day} (TL15); the hour is milliseconds / 3,600,000 modulo 24 and it is day when the hour is 6 to 17, night otherwise. | Original |
| SQ2 | global quest state, condition 7 (0x4454EF) → 0x4C51A0 | Two values, the quest and the state; true when the quest's game-wide state (an array of one dword per quest, index quest − 1000, no range test) equals the state. The array holds 2 (accepted) until a PC completes (4) or botches (6) the quest. | Original; a quest number outside 1000..1999 reads 0 here (the session holds no state for it) |
| SQ3 | rumor known, condition 40 (0x446A06) → 0x4C58D0 | The first object of operand 0 and the rumor of operand 1; true only for a PC whose learned-time entry for the rumor (index rumor − 1000) is not 0. Any other object, and a null handle, give false. | Original; a rumor outside 1000..2999 reads as not known here; a null handle (no triggerer) takes the false branch, no missing-object stop |
| SQ4 | rumor quelled, condition 41 (0x446A62) → 0x4C5650 | One value, the rumor; the bit of the game-wide quelled set (a bit array, index rumor − 1000), whatever the PC knows. | Original; a rumor outside 1000..2999 reads as not quelled here |
| SQ5 | clock actions, 96 (0x4498FB), 97 (0x449921), 98 (0x449959), 105 (0x449EC6) → 0x45A8D0, 0x45A870, 0x45A8B0, 0x45A890 | Each stores one number into the number store of operand 0 (0x44BB50): the day number (the day word plus one), the hour of the day (0 to 23), the minute (0 to 59) and the hours since startup (day × 24 plus the hour). | Original |
| SQ6 | obj has blessing, condition 8 (0x445549) → 0x4C4280, and obj has curse, condition 9 (0x44564A) → 0x4C3DD0 | The object list of operand 0 and the number of operand 1 (read once); an object counts when it is a PC (object type 15) whose blessing list (field 0x104) or curse list (field 0x106) holds the number. Then the list rule of SG6: none counted (an empty list included) false, all counted true, some only for the "any" focus kinds. | Original (BC3) |
| SQ7 | set quest global state, action 17 (0x4475F3) → 0x4C51C0 | The quest of operand 0 and the state of operand 1. Single player: nothing changes when the quest's state is already 4 or 6 (final); otherwise only a request for 4 or 6 is stored, and any other value returns 2 and stores nothing. A client of a network game changes nothing and a host sends a message first (not run in single player). | Original; the message is not sent |
| SQ8 | add blessing 25 (0x44783A) → 0x4C42F0, remove blessing 26 (0x4478C2) → 0x4C4450, add curse 27 (0x44794A) → 0x4C3E40, remove curse 28 (0x4479D2) → 0x4C3FA0 | The number of operand 0 is read, then the function is called for every object of the list of operand 1, in list order; a PC only, as BC4 and BC5 say (add nothing when held, the effect and the received message for the local PC; remove the first entry and the effect). | Original (BC4, BC5) |
| SQ9 | quell rumor, action 51 (0x4484D8) → 0x4C5690 | The rumor of operand 0 and the first object of operand 1; the rumor is quelled only when that object is the local player (the handle of 0x40DA50); any other object, and an absent one, change nothing. Quelling is a game-wide bit that never clears (LB11). | Original |
| SQ10 | give quest XP, action 63 (0x448B57) → 0x4C53C0, 0x45F110 | The first object of operand 0 and the value of operand 1, which is a quest experience level (not a quest number): 0x4C53C0 looks the level up in the table of `xp_quest.mes` (0 without an entry), and 0x45F110 gives that experience to a PC (type 15): 0 changes nothing, the difficulty adjusts the amount (XP11), and the hero's levels follow. The quest state's completion (0x4C4D20) gives the experience of the quest's own level the same way. | Original (XP11) |
| SQ11 | grant one fate point, action 117 (0x44A2B3) | The first object of operand 0; for a PC its fate points (stat 20) are raised by one through the stat setter, which clamps at 100 (FT7, FT8); any other object changes nothing. | Original (FT8) |
| SQ12 | set PC quest unbotched, action 119 (0x44A39F) → 0x4C5070 | The object list of operand 0 and the quest of operand 1; for each object: when the quest's game-wide state is not 6 nothing changes; for a PC the game-wide state goes back to 2, the PC's record loses its botched mark (bit 0x100) and is stamped with the game time, and the local PC hears the completed sound when the record's state is 4 and gets the logbook mark. An object that is not a PC leaves the state as it is. | Original; sound and logbook mark are the host's |
| SQ13 | what this build keeps (ARC-SCRIPT-1165) | Set S34 holds the six conditions and thirteen actions (S33 plus them: `ScrAdmissionSet.StoryConditions`, `StoryActions`); a host that holds them implements `IScrStoryQueries` (the clock, the quest's game-wide state, a PC's rumors, the quelled set, a PC's blessings and curses). The run's own changes to the game-wide quest states, quelled rumors, learned rumors and the PC's blessings and curses are read back by its later conditions (the original changes the game at once); a PC quest state changed with set PC quest state (16) is not (limit). The effects are `ScrStateRequest` entries in action order (kinds `GlobalQuestState`, `QuellRumor`, `QuestExperience`, `FatePoint`, `QuestUnbotched`, `AddBlessing`, `RemoveBlessing`, `AddCurse`, `RemoveCurse`), which `AreaScriptStory.Apply` gives to the session: `SetGlobalQuestState`, `QuellRumor`, `AwardQuestExperience`, `GrantHeroFatePoint`, `UnbotchQuest`, `AddHeroBlessing`, `RemoveHeroBlessing`. A blessing or curse needs the host's rule of its effect (`BlessingEffects`: the module's blessing and curse table, then the effect table); a host without it refuses the action. The session snapshot no longer requires a PC quest record's shared state to match when the record is not completed or botched. The "received" message and the logbook mark of a blessing or curse, and the sound of an unbotched quest, are not shown. The item-event host (wield on and off, hit) does not run these commands yet: a blessing item's script stays refused there. | Project |
| SQ14 | what the installed scripts do now (`AreaStoryScriptAdmissionTests` real-data check, `DAT_ROOT`) | 96 installed scripts use one of the 19 commands. S34 admits 52 of them whole (S33: none); the host sets admit them whole: use 23, tile and sector (placed) 35, heartbeat 24, dying 47, talk 26. The rest hold a command of another planned slice (NPC state, world objects, critter queries, vitals, loops). | Project |
| SQ15 | in-run read-back limits (review of ARC-SCRIPT-1165) | The run overlay records an add blessing for a PC object and an out-of-range rumor or quest number as the script wrote them; the host then skips a blessing for a session with no hero and refuses an out-of-range number. A later condition of the same run reads the overlay, not the committed state. A missing blessing effect rule is found before anything of the run applies (`AreaScriptStory.BlessingRulesResolve`), so such a run is refused whole. | Steamwoven limit (original has no such split: it writes as it goes); not reachable with the installed scripts and a hero session |
| SQ16 | quest request that is not above the quest, and what lights the Logbook button (ARC-WORLD-1554; read on 2026-11-13 in the text export of 0x4C4D20, 0x4C4E60, 0x4C5070, 0x4C5400 and the listing, one reader) | The public quest setter 0x4C4D20 reads the stored state (0x4C4CB0, the PC branch: the state word, 6 when the botched bit is set) and calls the internal setter 0x4C4E60 only when that state is not 4, 5 or 6 and is below the request, so a repeated or lower request writes nothing: no state, no time stamp, no light (single player; a multiplayer host repeats the call for each party member on a request of 2 or 4 and for each player slot on a request of 6, a client calls nothing). The internal setter (a PC only) stores the request (a botched request keeps the old state word and adds the bit 0x100), rewrites the time with the game clock, gives the completion experience and then, for the local PC, calls the light callback for the Logbook button (slot 1, flag 1, 0x460790) only when the stored state word less the botched bit (mask 0xFFFFFEFF) is not 0. The unbotch function 0x4C5070 does the same test on the state it keeps. The other callers of the internal setter are network code (0x49D690 packet dispatcher, 0x4A2070 network events) and 0x4C5400, reached through 0x4BA080 and 0x4C31A0 (it needs two PC objects and has its own state test; its name is not read, F1); none is a player's request. The session's quest rule (a request not above the effective state is NoChange) is therefore the original's, and nothing lights for it. A botched mark on a quest the PC never held (stored state 0) lights nothing in the original, nor does its unbotch; the Logbook stamp skips such a record (`QueryHeroInterfaceStamp`). | Original |

## NPC state commands (ARC-SCRIPT-1163, E5-a)

Source: slice E5-a of the [missing-systems plan](../../project/missing-systems-plan.md#e5-script-and-dialog-coverage) and the [script refusal census](../script-refusal-census.md) (ARC-RES-1101): the nine actions that change an NPC's own state (critter disband, set and adjust reaction, the four standpoint actions, change art number, change script). Read on 2026-11-12 by one reader (level F1) in the exports of the retail 1.0.7.4 `Arcanum.exe` (SHA-256 `9bd80511…78d52`): the script action interpreter 0x447220 (cases 22, 30, 31, 45, 46, 59, 99, 140 and 141), the operand getters 0x44B960 (first object), 0x44B4E0 (object list) and 0x44BA70 (value), the party functions 0x45DFC0, 0x45E040 and 0x4AA8C0, the reaction functions 0x4C0CC0 and 0x4C0DE0 (R2), the object field accessors 0x406CA0, 0x4EFE50, 0x4EFDD0, 0x407840 and 0x4F01D0, and the art id helpers 0x502700, 0x502780, 0x502C40, 0x502220 and 0x4F02F0. Behaviour in prose; no bytes.

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| NS1 | critter disband, action 22 → 0x45DFC0 (force 1), 0x4AA8C0, 0x45E040 | The first object of operand 0. When it has a leader the follow check is skipped (force 1): the NPC is unwaited (0x4AA8C0) and taken out of its leader's follower array (0x45E040, order kept, leader cleared, animations interrupted at priority 5, forced bit cleared); an object with no leader changes nothing. The same function is the session's leave (`GameSessionOwner.LeaveParty`, ledger C6, OL23), which the dialog's leave and the leave order already use. | Original |
| NS2 | set reaction 30 and adjust reaction 31 → 0x4C0CC0, 0x4C0DE0 | Operand 0 the NPC, operand 1 the PC, operand 2 the number. 31 calls the reaction change with the number as the delta; 30 first reads the standard reaction (0x4C0CC0) and calls it with the number less that reading. The change is the one of R2: nothing for a delta of 0, for objects that are not an NPC and a PC, for an NPC the PC mind-controls, and for a negative delta from a PC of extraordinary charisma who leads the NPC; otherwise it passes through the PC's reaction effects and is added to the stored value. | Original |
| NS3 | day and night standpoints, actions 45 and 46 → 0x4EFE50 (fields 0x121, 0x122); extended 140 and 141 → 0x4EFE50, 0x4EFDD0 (field 0xF3) | Operand 0 the object, operands 1 and 2 the x and y of the tile. Only an NPC (type 16) acts: its standpoint location field takes x as the low word and y OR x's sign extension (a negative x gives −1) as the high word. The extended actions read a third number and also write it less 4,999 into field 0xF3, the NPC's teleport map (the same field as WS3), unconditionally. Any other object type does nothing. The standpoint step then reads the fields (WS3). | Original |
| NS4 | change script, action 99 → 0x407840, 0x4F01D0 (field 0x20 of the object, the attachment array) | Operand 0 the object, operand 1 the attachment point (index), operand 2 the new script number. When the object is the run's own attachee and the point is the run's own, the number goes into the run's pending script result (the same place as action 6, change this script), taking effect when the run ends; otherwise the point's element is read, its script word (the third) replaced and the element written back, so the flags and counters stay. The point is not range-checked. An element that does not exist is read into an uninitialised stack buffer before the write (the original's accessor only logs it). | Original; project choices in NS7 |
| NS5 | change art number, action 59 → 0x502780, 0x502C40, 0x502220, 0x4F02F0 (field 1, the object's art) | The object list of operand 0 and the number of operand 1. For each object: the art id's number bits are replaced (their position depends on the art type, 0x502780: types 0, 1, 2 and 12 give 0, scenery-like types take 9 bits at bit 19, a number beyond the type's range logs an error and keeps the id), the frame is set to 0 (0x502C40) and the new id is written (0x4F02F0) only when the art file exists (0x502220). | Original |
| NS6 | what this build keeps (ARC-SCRIPT-1163) | Set S36 is S35 plus the nine actions (`ScrAdmissionSet.NpcStateActions`); the interpreter hands one `ScrObjectStateRequest` per object of disband, the reactions, the standpoints and change art number, and handles change script itself. `AreaUseScriptHost` (use, placed, heartbeat and dying sets: `NpcStateHostActions`, all but 59 until ARC-SCRIPT-1735, which gave 59 its host: [AN1–AN9](#change-art-number-arc-script-1735-e5-a-tail)) applies: disband through the session's leave with force, the reactions through the AI host (`AreaAiHost.ScriptReaction`: the session's adjust and set with the NPC's and hero's facts), the standpoints as the session's object fields `npc.standpoint.day` and `npc.standpoint.night` (`SessionNpcStandpoint`, saved with the object, no schema step) that the AI host's route facts read over the row's, and the extended actions' map as the NPC's teleport map (`SetTeleportMap`); change script as the slot write call-ex already makes. The run's later following tests read a disband. | Project |
| NS7 | project limits | (1) Only an NPC of the shown map takes a reaction change or a standpoint; any other object is left as it is and the run's other effects apply. (2) The reactions need the AI host (the set is refused without the delegate) and its facts: for an NPC the AI host does not run, or one without reaction facts, the change is not made, `AreaUseScriptHost.LastReaction` names why and the run goes on; a session refusal of the amount is traced the same way. (3) Change script writes only the points the host keeps as map changes and only where the object's row has the element; any other run is left unapplied before anything applies (the original writes its sparse array). The run's own slot is the run's result at any call depth (a call-ex callee's is written back by call-ex; a called script's is dropped, as in the original). A map number below 4,000 for the extended standpoints (teleport map below 1) is left unapplied before anything applies. A point beyond 35 ends the dispatch. A new element (none read) starts from flags and counters 0. (4) Change art number was **not applied by any host** (the viewer kept no per-object art overlay), so every host set left it out; ARC-SCRIPT-1735 gave it the overlay and a host, limits in [AN8](#change-art-number-arc-script-1735-e5-a-tail). (5) The dialog host (`AreaScriptHost`) does not hold the nine actions: a dialog script that uses one stays refused. | Project |
| NS8 | what the installed scripts do now (`AreaNpcStateScriptAdmissionTests` real-data check, `DAT_ROOT`) | 270 installed scripts use one of the nine actions (disband 34, set reaction 11, adjust reaction 13, day standpoint 73, night standpoint 62, extended day and night 1 each, change art number 51, change script 109). S36 admits 208 of them whole (S35: none); the host sets admit them whole: use 56, tile and sector (placed) 117, heartbeat 89, dying 143. The rest hold change art number or a command of another planned slice. The story census of SQ14 moves with it: of the 96 story scripts the placed set admits 36 (was 35), heartbeat 25 (24) and dying 51 (47). On the 79 maps map mode opens, 189 more tile scripts run without a teleport and 189 fewer are refused (`AreaUseScriptsOriginalTests`). | Project |

## Change art number (ARC-SCRIPT-1735, E5-a tail)

Source: issue #1735, the part of slice E5-a that [NS7](#npc-state-commands-arc-script-1163-e5-a) (4) left out. Read on 2026-11-13 by one reader (level F1) in the exports of the retail 1.0.7.4 `Arcanum.exe` (SHA-256 `9bd80511…78d52`): the script action interpreter 0x447220 (case 0x3B, action 59), the operand readers 0x44B4E0 and 0x44BA70, the object field accessors 0x406CA0, 0x406D40 and the art writer 0x4F02F0 with its worker 0x43EB30, and the art id helpers 0x502700, 0x502710, 0x502780, 0x502830, 0x502C40, 0x502220 and 0x51AE50 (the ART name builder; the callback behind it is [NA1](data.md#art-names-and-missing-art-arc-res-050)). Behaviour in prose; no bytes. The independent check is the review of this task. AN13–AN15 (ARC-RES-1782, issue #1782, read on 2026-11-14 by one reader, level F1, in the same exports) close the F0 point of AN4: the art name callback 0x41DAE0 for a tile id with its normaliser 0x41D510, the tile file name builder 0x4EAE90 with its field readers (0x5036B0, 0x5036E0, 0x503700, 0x5037B0, 0x5038C0, 0x503950, 0x503990, 0x504FD0), the name table lookup 0x4EB0C0, the name file builder 0x4EAF70, the tile name loader 0x4EB2E0 and the file test 0x52FB80; the data facts are read in the owner's installation (the base DATs, the Arcanum and Vormantown modules), and the census by a local scan of the installed scripts (not committed).

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| AN1 | change art number, action 59 → 0x447220 case 0x3B | The object list of operand 0 (0x44B4E0) and the number of operand 1 (0x44BA70, read once). For each object, in list order: the object's art id (field 1, 0x406CA0) goes through the number setter (0x502780), then the frame setter with frame 0 (0x502C40), then the file test (0x502220); only a test that answers 0 (the file exists) writes the new id (0x4F02F0). The run goes on whatever happens. An absent operand makes an empty list. | Original |
| AN2 | number setter 0x502780 (class 0x502700 = bits 28–31; number reader 0x502710) | The classes **0, 1, 2 and 12** (tile, wall, critter, monster) answer the id **0**, whatever the id was. A unique NPC (13) holds its number in 8 bits at bit 20 (range 256); an interface art (5) in 12 bits at bit 16 (range 4,096); an item (6) in 11 bits at bit 17 with a range that depends on its type and subtype (0x502830: 20 for the first three types with no coverage, 1,000 otherwise); every other class (portal 3, scenery 4, container 7, misc 8, light 9, roof 10, facade 11, eye candy 14) in 9 bits at bit 19 (range 512). A number at or past the range logs "Range exceeded in art set" and returns the id as it was (the rest of the action still runs on it: frame 0, the file test, the write). The other bits (for scenery the kind bits 6–10, the frame bits 14–18, the palette) are kept. | Original |
| AN3 | frame setter 0x502C40 with frame 0 | Tile, wall and item ids are returned as they are. An interface or misc art (5, 8) clears its frame in bits 8–15, a facade (11) in bits 1–10, a light and an eye candy (9, 14) in bits 12–18, every other class in bits 14–18. The art header is read (0x5030B0) only for a frame other than 0, which this action never asks for. | Original |
| AN4 | file test 0x502220 (name 0x51AE50, file 0x52FB80) | The ART name builder (the callback of NA1, called for every class but 8) names the file of the id; the test answers 0 when it exists and 0x10 when the name cannot be built or the file is missing. The action writes only on 0. So a number with no ART file leaves the object as it was (the run goes on), and an id of the classes 0, 1, 2 and 12 (the id 0 the setter made) is written only if the file of art id 0 exists. It does in the installed data ([AN13](#change-art-number-arc-script-1735-e5-a-tail)), so the original writes the id 0 on a critter, a monster or a wall; this build keeps them unchanged ([AN15](#change-art-number-arc-script-1735-e5-a-tail)). | Original (the id 0 file: AN13, F1) |
| AN5 | art writer 0x4F02F0 → 0x43EB30 | The worker acts only when the object's flags (field 19, `obj_f_flags`) have bit 0 clear, i.e. an object destroyed in its map data or by a script is left as it is. It then reads the object's rectangle (0x43DFD0), writes field 1, clears bit 27 of the render flags (field 327, `obj_f_render_flags`), reads the rectangle again, hands both to the dirty-rectangle callback and refreshes the object's light/fog state (0x43F710). The wrapper then records the change for a replay when a global bit (0x62AC6C bit 0) is set (a debug or network feature; not read further). | Original (the replay record F0) |
| AN6 | what the art decides on screen | The object rectangle, the hover and the pick shape read the current art's frame (NA1, 0x43DFD0), so the changed object is drawn, outlined and picked as its new art; the frame is 0, so a scenery that animates (fires, torches) runs its loop on the new art (its goal reads the art each step). A scenery whose new art has one frame is still. | Original |
| AN7 | which objects the census names | The 51 installed scripts that use the action have 63 uses: 39 on the current looped object (28 in loops over the scenery of the vicinity, focus kinds 15 and 16, and 11 over its containers, kind 17: [AN14](#change-art-number-arc-script-1735-e5-a-tail)), 17 on the attachee, 5 on the extra object, 2 on the triggerer (the holder of an insert-item event, a display case and never the PC: [AN14](#change-art-number-arc-script-1735-e5-a-tail)); the numbers are 2–120. Of the 122 placed sites of the scripts that use change art number or touch art (123) 53 are tile scripts, 29 stand on NPCs, 20 on generic items, 13 on scenery, 3 on containers and the rest are sectors and other items. Which object a run reaches is decided at run time (the vicinity loop, the attachee's type), so the census cannot name the targets by type; the host refuses a target the viewer cannot draw (AN8). | Original data (census, ARC-SCRIPT-1735) |
| AN8 | what this build keeps (ARC-SCRIPT-1735) | `ScrAdmissionSet.NpcStateHostActions` is all nine actions now; `AreaUseScriptHost` plans each request (`ArtPlan`) and applies it as the session's object field `obj.art` (`SessionObjectArt`: the new art id with frame 0, by object identity, saved in the snapshot's objects section, **no schema step**: an unknown-before property of a routed field, old saves load as they were, a mobile object's record travels with it and a static object's stays on its map). The plan: the PC and an NPC of a critter class change nothing (AN2, AN4; the original writes a tile's art there, an exe bug no installed use was found to reach: AN15); an object destroyed (AN5) changes nothing; a number whose id names no ART file (`AreaExtraArt.Exists`, the module's content) changes nothing; the data's own id clears the field; **only a scenery of a loaded map took a new art** in ARC-SCRIPT-1735; items followed in ARC-SCRIPT-1779 and ARC-SCRIPT-1826 ([AN10–AN18](#change-art-number-arc-script-1735-e5-a-tail)) and doors, windows and containers of the shown map in ARC-SCRIPT-1778 ([AD1–AD5](#change-art-number-arc-script-1735-e5-a-tail)). Any other target (a unique NPC, a trap, an object with no loaded row) is **refused**: the run is left unapplied before anything applies, as for the other host actions. The viewer draws the new art in place of the scenery's T0 body, frame 0, and since ARC-RENDER-1781 animates it over the new art's frames (the scenery bodies of `TerrainViewerSceneryArt.cs`; a busted scenery's ground body wins). Out of scope here: items (ARC-SCRIPT-1779, [AN10–AN12](#change-art-number-arc-script-1735-e5-a-tail)), a unique NPC's body; the new art's own animation loop is ARC-RENDER-1781 ([AAR1–AAR6](world.md#an-animated-scenery-whose-art-changed-arc-render-1781)). Issues: a unique NPC #1780 (no installed run reaches one: [AN16–AN17](#change-art-number-arc-script-1735-e5-a-tail)), the id 0 read for critters #1782. | Project |
| AN9 | tests and census | `ObjectArtNumberTests` (AN2, AN3), `ObjectArtStateTests` (the session field, snapshot round trip, budgets), `AreaNpcStateScriptTests` (the host: write, frame 0, clear by the data's id, the refusals), `AreaNpcStateScriptAdmissionTests` (real data: the sets now admit the use 85, placed 168, heartbeat 98 and dying 214 of the 270 scripts), the smoke stage `object-art` (`./arcanum smoke pr --stages object-art`: the shown map's scenery takes another scenery's art number, is drawn from it, saved and restored by the data's id, on the main map and on a second real map; the stage drives the session's write directly, the host path is covered by `AreaNpcStateScriptTests`). | Project |
| AN10 | item number range 0x502830 (item type 0x504550 = low four bits of an item art id, coverage 0x504570 = bits 14..16) | The number setter's range for an item art id (class 6) is 20 for the item types 0 and 1 and for type 2 (armour) with coverage 0, and 1,000 for every other type; an art id of another class has no range (0). The number takes 11 bits at bit 17; the type, subtype (bits 6..9), place (12..13), coverage, destroyed (bit 10) and palette bits stay. A number at or past the range logs "Range exceeded in art set" and keeps the id; the frame setter keeps an item id as it is. | Original (read 2026-11-13 for ARC-SCRIPT-1779, F1; the independent check is that task's review) |
| AN11 | which art field an item's change art number writes (0x4F02F0 → 0x43EB30; the field readers of IW22, CU7, IW28) | The action writes **object field 1** (the current art, the item's ground art) only: the worker 0x43EB30 sets field 1 and clears bit 27 of the render flags (AN5). An item's **inventory art is field 0x5D** (set through 0x4FFFE0, drawn in the inventory grid, on the cursor and as the item's size in cells, IW22, IW28, CU7); the paper-doll art of a worn weapon or armour is the weapon's field 0x71 or the armour's field 0x97 (IW22). None of them is written, so after the action an item shown in a holder's list, on the cursor or worn looks as before; only the item as an object on the ground (its draw, hover and pick shape, AN6) and in flight (a thrown item flies with its current art, PF2) takes the new art. The issue that opened this slice assumed the inventory art follows: the executable says it does not. | Original (read 2026-11-13, F1) |
| AN12 | what this build keeps for items (ARC-SCRIPT-1779) | `ArtPlan` takes an item (types 5..14) of a loaded row: a destroyed item (the session's deletion, or object flag 0x1) changes nothing; the new id is `ObjectArtNumber.Changed` on the item's current art (`ItemRange`, `ItemType`) and is written as the same session object field `obj.art` only when an ART file names it (`AreaExtraArt.Exists`, the ground place). An item is a **mobile** object: its record is one by its identity (`oid:`), found from any map (`QueryObjectArt`), saved, carried with the item between holders and maps, and cleared by the data's id from whatever map the item stands on (`SetObjectArt`). The viewer draws a ground item (an NPC's item dropped, a pile's item, an item a T0 body draws where it lies) and a thrown item's flight from the written art; the inventory grid, cursor, paper doll and every other holder view keep the inventory art (AN11). Project limits: the host finds an item's row on the map the request names (the shown map, the clicked item, that map's rows); an item with no row there is reached as [AN18](#change-art-number-arc-script-1735-e5-a-tail) says (an item of another map's data carried here, a made or script-created item), and an item the session knows neither as carried nor as made (a starting-kit item `kit:n`, whose art the viewer draws from the kit load, F0; an item of an NPC's own list carried to another map) still refuses the run; a deleted item is left as it is (AN5); an item row whose art is not of the item class is refused; an id equal to the current one writes nothing; the destroyed bit of the art (a broken item, bit 10) is kept and not drawn differently (F0). Tests: `ObjectArtNumberTests` (range, setter), `ObjectArtStateTests` (the mobile record), `AreaNpcStateScriptTests` (the host's item plan), the smoke stage `object-art` (its item part: an NPC's item dropped next to the hero and an item a T0 body draws, with the saved field and the clear; it writes the session field directly, as the scenery part does, so the host's plan and the range are the unit tests', and the unchanged inventory art holds by construction: no inventory, cursor or paper-doll code reads `obj.art`). | Project |
| AN13 | the file of art id 0 (0x502220 → 0x51AE50 → 0x41DAE0 → 0x4EAE90 → 0x4EB0C0, 0x4EAF70; names 0x4EB2E0; file 0x52FB80) | The id 0 the setter answers for the classes 0, 1, 2 and 12 is an id of class 0, a **tile**, with every field 0. The normaliser keeps it 0 (for a tile it only clears the palette bits 4–5 and the bits 0–3). The tile name builder reads its two name indexes (bits 22–27 and 16–21), its edge (bits 12–15), its variation (bits 9–11), its outdoor bit (8) and its two flip bits (7, 6): all 0. The lookup takes the name of index 0 from the table of the entries 300 and up of `art\tile\tilename.mes` (the indoor, not flippable set; the loader puts entry 300 at index 0, and an entry with no `/` keeps its first three letters as the name). The file builder sees the two names equal and names the base tile file `art\tile\<name>bse0a.art` (edge character 0, variation letter a). In the owner's installation entry 300 names a dungeon floor tile whose base file is present in `arcanum2.dat`; the file test walks the mounted DATs and folders and answers 0. So the action **writes the id 0**: the critter, monster or wall takes the art of an indoor floor tile, frame 0. What the screen then shows (a critter's draw, animation and hover read critter fields of a tile id) was not read (F0). | Original (F1; the screen effect F0) |
| AN14 | which installed uses reach a critter | The two triggerer uses are the insert-item scripts of the Finger of Mannox and the Strange Glass Device (02181, 02182, on the items). The names 1201 and 1203 are those of two container prototypes, the full display cases of those two items (the container range 1200–1599 of the object names in `oemes\oname.mes`; a PC or an NPC carries the name of a critter prototype, outside that range), so the triggerer use reaches a **container**, never the PC; when the item goes anywhere else the script loops over the containers of the vicinity with that name and changes their art to 4 (the case left empty). Of the other 61 uses, the 39 looped ones run in loops over the scenery (28) or the containers (11) of the vicinity, never its critters; the 5 extra-object uses (shovel, sound gears, glass key) each run only after a test that the extra object is named 1216, 1219 or 1671 (a grave and a container of the container range, a pedestal of the scenery range); the 17 attachee uses reach the object that holds the script: use, transfer and teleport scripts named for containers, sarcophagi, crates, manholes and devices (where each is placed was checked in [AN16](#change-art-number-arc-script-1735-e5-a-tail)).
