| AN15 | classification and what this build keeps (ARC-RES-1782) | An **exe bug** (bug rule of 2026-10-28): the setter's answer 0 for the classes it does not number is the id of an unrelated floor tile, so a script that sets a critter's art number would turn the critter into a floor tile instead of doing nothing or logging the range. No installed use was found to reach it (AN14; the placement of the 17 attachee uses: AN18), so this build fixes it in both settings, as for the other unreached script bugs of the Scripts row of the [rule modes](../../world/rule-modes.md) ("both fixed"): a critter, a monster and the PC change nothing and a wall is refused (AN8), with no rule mode, since the Original setting has no installed case to reproduce. The two triggerer uses act on display cases, containers, which are the scope of #1778. The build already behaves as decided, so no implementation issue follows (#1782 AC2). | Project |
| AN16 | which installed runs reach a unique NPC (ARC-SCRIPT-1780, census of the installed data, 2026-11-14) | **None.** Unique NPC art (class 13) is common: 81 of the 497 NPC prototypes (art classes 2, 12 and 13: 232, 184 and 81) and 787 of the 3,792 NPC rows placed on the 81 maps (classes 2, 12, 13 and 4: 1,009, 1,975, 787 and 21), so the question is open to the data. The action's 63 uses (51 scripts) name their object by four kinds. **Looped (39):** the nearest loop that starts above each use runs over the scenery (15, any scenery 16) or the containers (17) of the vicinity, never over critters; the two scripts that also hold a critter loop (8) start it after the use (1269 line 11 against its uses at 5 and 9; 1596 line 10 against its use at 6, read, not asserted); NPCs hold 10 of these scripts (heartbeat, dialog and dying points, pinned: 1067, 1269, 1299, 1388, 1393, 1636, 2083, 2108, 2782, 2983; 1388 stands on a class 13 NPC once), all of whose uses are looped, so the object they write is the looped scenery or container. **Attachee (17):** the scripts stand on containers (type 2) and scenery (3) only (use, transfer and teleport points); 1337 and 2206 are called by scenery, container and item scripts with their own attachee or a looped scenery (pinned callers: the scenery scripts 1314, 1332, 1333 and 2854–2856 call 1337, passing their triggerer and attachee; 2208 and 2978 call 2206, passing the current looped object; 2205 calls it too and stands on no object; no NPC-held script calls any of them), and 1193 and 2205 stand on no object of the installed maps (1193 is called by none). **Extra object (5):** each use follows a test that the extra object is named 1216, 1219 or 1671 (2942, 2978 and 3047, the item scripts of the shovel, the sound gears and the glass key); the objects with these names are containers (2) and a scenery (3), never an NPC (script 2205 also tests extra objects named 5650–5656, items, but stands on no object). **Triggerer (2):** the display-case containers of [AN14](#change-art-number-arc-script-1735-e5-a-tail). No other script changes the script of an object to one that changes art (no action 99 with a constant script number names one), so the table of holders is complete for the installed content; an object created at run time has the script of its prototype, which the census read with the placed rows. A mod that adds a script or places one on a unique NPC is outside the installed data. | Original data (census, ARC-SCRIPT-1780) |
| AN17 | classification and what this build keeps (ARC-SCRIPT-1780) | The original's setter does take a number for a unique NPC (AN2: 8 bits at bit 20, range 256), so a script that names a class 13 NPC would change its unique number (its body and portrait files come from it); the viewer draws an NPC from its body and animation set, not from one art id, so it would need the changed body, the walk and stand animations and the hover shape. The census (AN16) finds **no installed run that reaches one**, so that model is **not built**: the host keeps refusing a unique NPC target (AN8: the run is left unapplied before anything applies, the refusal says so), and the issue is closed with this answer rather than with unreachable code. The census is pinned by `ObjectArtCensusRealDataTests` (opt-in `DAT_ROOT`, category `RealData`: the NPC art classes of the prototypes and placed rows, and the uses' object kinds, loop kinds, holders and tested names), so a data change that makes a use reach an NPC fails it. | Project (F1 for the census, no executable read beyond AN2) |
| AN18 | an item with no row on the shown map (ARC-SCRIPT-1826; the same field and worker as AN11, AN12; the object model: a made object is a copy of its prototype, OM rows) | The original's action writes object field 1 of whatever object the script names, wherever it is held; it needs no map row. This build reaches the baseline art of such an item in two ways. (1) A **map-data item carried from another map** (the hero's taken item, one of the hero's lying on the ground, one the hero gave away): `ArtPlan` asks the session for the origin map it names (`QueryPcInventory`, `QueryPlacedItems`, `QueryGivenItems`) and reads the item's row there by identity through `AreaUseScriptHost.OtherMapRow` (the inventory host's `RowOn`, the lookup the item's use script already makes, ARC-DEV-316); the row's art and object flags decide as for a row of a held map (an item destroyed in its map data is left as it is). (2) A **made or script-created item** (`made:n`: a schematic product, a script's create object, an item the hero dropped again): its art is its prototype's current art, read through the world's prototype reader (`AreaScriptWorld.Item`, the same reader the viewer draws a created item from), so the first change works from the prototype's art number and the prototype's own art clears the record. The record is the same `obj.art` field; `made:n` joins the mobile entities **for this field only** (`ObjectStateVocabulary.Mobile`), so the record is one whatever map it names, travels with the item from holder to holder and map to map, is saved in the snapshot's objects section and is cleared by the baseline from any map; **no schema step** (the field and the section are the same; an older save has no such record). The viewer needs no change: the ground and flight draw already read `QueryObjectArt` by identity and prefer it to the row's or the prototype's art. Project limits: an item the session does not know as carried, made or created (an item of an NPC's own list carried to another map with its map data, no origin named) is still refused as an object with no loaded row; a starting-kit item (`kit:n`) is refused (F0: its art comes from the hero load, not read for this action); the viewer draws a placed assembly product from the schematic data's art before the prototype's (ARC-WORLD-070), while the baseline here is always the prototype's art, so for a product whose two arts differ the first change starts from the prototype's number (F0: whether they differ for any installed product was not checked); an ammunition or gold stack split or merged by the session changes its `made:n` entity, so its record stays with the old entity (the record of a deleted item is dropped); a made item that only a junk pile holds is refused unless the session lists it as placed (F0). Tests: `ObjectArtStateTests` (the made item's record), `AreaNpcStateScriptTests` (a made item and a carried item as the extra object of a use script: write, same-number no-op, baseline clear, refusals, destroyed carried item). | Project |
| AD1 | what the action does to a door, a window and a container (ARC-SCRIPT-1778) | Read on 2026-11-13 in the exports of the retail 1.0.7.4 executable (level F1, with the [LK10](world.md), [LK13](world.md) and [LW2](items.md) rows already read): the open state of a door or window and of a container **is the frame of its art id** (`portal_is_open` 0x4F08C0 reads the frame of field 1 through 0x502B50 and answers whether it is not 0; a container's open and close step the frame, LK13, LW2). The art writer (0x43EB30, AN5) writes field 1 and the render flags and nothing else: no lock field (locked, jammed, held, busted bits of the portal or container flags) is read or written. So the new id the action builds (number setter, then frame 0, AN2, AN3) leaves the object **closed on frame 0 of the new art, with every lock field as it was**, whatever frame it stood on, and also when the number is the one it had (the setter and the frame setter run on every call). The object's busted ART bit (bit 9 of a portal's id), its door or window bit 10, its rotation (bits 11–13) and its mirror bit are other bits of the id and stay. | Original |
| AD2 | what this build keeps for a door, a window and a container (ARC-SCRIPT-1778) | `AreaUseScriptHost.ArtPlan` takes a portal (type 1, art class 3) and a container (type 2, class 7) of the shown map beside the scenery: the same checks (the PC and a critter class change nothing, a destroyed object changes nothing, a number whose id names no ART file changes nothing: `AreaExtraArt.Exists` now names a portal's file by the owner's portal rule, `AreaObjectArt.Resolve` class 3), and the object must be a door, window or container the viewer knows (`AreaScriptWorld.Openable`), else the run is refused before anything applies. The apply writes the object's art id as `obj.art` (frame 0, AN8) and the session's frame 0 (`SetOpenableFrame`), so the object is closed and the lock fields stay (AD1). An id equal to the data's art with frame 0 clears the record; the frame is reset all the same. | Project |
| AD3 | what a door and a window read from the new art | `AreaPortals.Retarget` gives the portal the new art's facts (door or window bit, rotation, the frame count of its group), its frame rate and every frame of its group, read from the module's `art/portal` files (`AreaExtraArt`), and returns the map's own entry when the record goes. What reads the portal afterwards reads the new art: the open steps and the closing (`PortalFrameRules` over the new frame count, so the open frame is the new art's), the passage rule (open is any frame but 0, the session's), the step delay (the new art's frame rate), the door host (`AreaDoorHost` finds the portal by entity at each call), a script's toggle and open state (`AreaScriptWorld.Openable`). The viewer syncs from the session's `obj.art` records whenever the session changes (`SyncPortalArt`), so a record that comes from a save is applied too. A door run in flight (the open or close steps) goes on with the new art's frame count; the original's runs read the art at each step too (LK11). | Project |
| AD4 | what the viewer draws for a door, a window and a container | A portal with a new art is drawn from the new art's frame the session holds (`AreaScenePlan.PortalArtFrame`: placed from the frame's size and hotspot about the anchor its T0 body was placed from), closed or open. A container is drawn from its art id (the written one, else the map's own) with the session's frame, off its T0 body (`ContainerArtBodies` in the ground bodies): the open lid was not drawn before (barter-runtime), so a container the hero opened shows frame 1 of its own art too, and a script-changed one its new art's frame 0 or 1. The container's open step reads the frame count of the art it has now (`ContainerFrameCount`: an ART of one frame keeps frame 0, LW2). An art id whose frame does not read leaves the T0 body (said once). | Project |
| AD5 | tests and smoke (ARC-SCRIPT-1778) | `AreaNpcStateScriptTests` (a door and a container: new id on frame 0, closed, the lock fields kept; the same number still closes an open door; no openable or no file; a wrong art class), `AreaObjectArtTests` (the portal rule), the smoke stage `object-art` (second part: a container and a door of a real map take another's art number: drawn closed and open from the new art, the portal's facts and open frame are the new art's, the data's id returns the T0 body; the stage drives the session's writes, the script path is the unit tests'). Limits: a door run in flight when the action runs (AD3) and the replay record (AN5) are not modelled; a door or container of another map than the shown one is refused. | Project |

## World-object commands (ARC-SCRIPT-1164, E5-b)

Source: slice E5-b of the [missing-systems plan](../../project/missing-systems-plan.md#e5-script-and-dialog-coverage) and the [script refusal census](../script-refusal-census.md) (ARC-RES-1101): the thirteen commands (three conditions, ten actions) that act on a single world object or on one game-wide table (create item, transfer item, set lock state, toggle open or closed, set written start, toggle sector blocked, toggle monster generator, touch art, destroy item named, toggle item inventory display, and the conditions obj is open, sector is blocked and monster generator disabled). Read from the exports in `research-private/` (the action executor 0x447220 and the condition evaluator 0x4451B0; behaviour in prose, no listings).

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| WO1 | create item, action 66 → 0x447220 case 0x42 | Operand 0 is a basic prototype number (the file is that number + 20, as create object) and operand 1 the first object of its list, the holder. The new object is made at the holder's location; when it is an item (type 5..14) it is put into the holder's inventory by the insert (0x4617F0, slot −1: the first free place). A prototype of another type stays where it was made. No object, nothing. | Original |
| WO2 | transfer item, action 41 → case 0x29 | Operand 0 is a name number, operands 1 and 2 the first objects of their lists (the holder and the new holder). The first direct item of the holder with that name number is found in the holder's own list (0x462480) and inserted into the other's by the same insert (0x4617F0). Nothing without both objects or without such an item. | Original |
| WO3 | set lock state, action 53 → case 0x35 → 0x441DD0 | Operand 0 the first object, operand 1 the state. A portal or container (the junk pile is neither) has its locked bit cleared and, for the value 1, set again; clearing a locked object schedules the Lock event an hour later, as a key does, but only for the value 0 (the event is scheduled when the object was locked and the value is 0); the value 1 sets the bit, any other value clears it and schedules nothing. An absent object, any other type and the PC change nothing. | Original |
| WO4 | toggle open or closed, action 88 → case 0x58; condition 36 → case 0x24 | The first object of operand 0. A portal is opened or closed at once to the end of its steps (0x4EEF80, 0x4F08F0); a container opens when its frame is 0 (the ART's next frame, 0x502BC0) and otherwise closes (the previous frame, 0x502C00, never below 0); a destroyed object and any other type do nothing. Condition 36 is true when the portal's or container's art frame is not 0 (0x4F08C0, 0x502B50); any other object and an absent one are false. | Original |
| WO5 | toggle sector blocked, action 106 → case 0x6A → 0x4EF010 → 0x4D0F00; condition 45 → case 0x2D; the list 0x4D2FC0, 0x4D3000, 0x4D3050, 0x4D0EE0; the world map's refusal 0x4207D0, 0x420900, 0x4209C0 | The sector of the two numbers (y ORed with x's sign word, packed 0x4CFC50) is put on the current map's blocked list when it is not on it and taken off when it is; the list is the map's `map.sbf`, loaded with the map and saved with it. The world-map route does not enter a blocked sector and a blocked destination is refused. Condition 45 is true when the sector is on the list. | Original |
| WO6 | set written start, action 94 → case 0x5E | The first object of operand 0 and the start of operand 1; the written-start field (0xCB) is written for a written item (type 13) only. | Original |
| WO7 | destroy item named, action 126 → case 0x7E → 0x43CCA0 | The name number of operand 0 and the first object of operand 1; the first item of that name in its own list is destroyed. Nothing for an absent object. | Original |
| WO8 | toggle item inventory display, action 127 → case 0x7F | The first object of operand 0 (an item of type 5..14 only) has bit 0x10 of its item flags (field 0x57) flipped. | Original |
| WO9 | toggle monster generator, action 110 → case 0x6E; condition 46 → case 0x2E; 0x4BABF0, 0x4BABD0 | Bit 0x80 of the slot's byte in the generators' counter table is set when clear and cleared when set; a slot outside 0..255 changes nothing (both table functions test the range). Condition 46 reads that bit; the table is game-wide. | Original |
| WO10 | touch art, action 123 → case 0x7B → 0x4EF190 → 0x502290 | The original asks the art cache whether the art number is known and uses nothing of the answer; the operand is read and nothing else happens. | Original |
| WO11 | what this build keeps (ARC-SCRIPT-1164) | Set S37 holds the three conditions and ten actions (S36 plus them: `ScrAdmissionSet.WorldObjectConditions`, `WorldObjectActions`); a host that holds them implements `IScrWorldObjectQueries` (open state, whether an object can open, the blocked sector list, the generator block bits). The run overlays the open state, the sector list and the generator bits, so its later conditions read its own toggles, and the effects carry `ScrObjectStateRequest` (create item, transfer, lock, toggle, written start, destroy item named, display toggle) and `ScrWorldRequest` (sector and generator toggles, each with the state it ends in). The use, tile, sector, heartbeat and dying hosts hold the three conditions and six actions they apply (`WorldObjectHostActions`: set lock state, create item, toggle open or closed, toggle sector blocked, toggle monster generator, touch art). The lock bit goes through `SetOpenableLocked` (value 1 or 0, with the Lock event) or a plain flag write; the open toggle writes the session's art frame; create item goes through `CreateItemForHero` or `AddItemToNpc` with the viewer's insert check; the blocked sector list is sector-scope map changes `map.blocked` (`SessionSectorBlock`, no schema step); the world map's route refuses a blocked sector. | Project |
| WO12 | project limits | (1) *Closed by ARC-SCRIPT-1279 (WO14–WO18): transfer item (41), set written start (94), destroy item named (126) and toggle item inventory display (127) are applied by every host that applies the other S37 actions; ARC-SCRIPT-1164 had left them out for want of a per-item field write and removal.* (2) Create item needs an item prototype (not Gold, Key, KeyRing or Ammo, whose creation is another path) and a holder that is the hero or an NPC with an inventory list; any other run is left unapplied before anything applies. (3) Lock state and the open toggle apply to a portal or container of the shown map; any other object type does nothing, as in the original. (4) A sector with a negative number is refused (the session's keys do not hold it). (5) The world-map screen's refusal when the hero's own sector is blocked (0x5607E0) and the picture's dimming of a blocked sector are not built; the route refuses the sector. (6) The monster generator bit table is the session's generator block state (`ToggleGeneratorBlock`) and answers slots 0..255 only. (7) Create item whose holder has a full list or whose insert check says no leaves the item not taken and the run goes on (the hero's path drops it to the ground); `LastCreatedItem` names the result. (8) The run's own open overlay assumes a two-frame container and does not know a destroyed object (the toggle then reads open in the same run, the session frame stays 0); a script that tests `obj is open` on an object of another loaded map stops the run (the host has no facts for it). (9) A sector with an x beyond the 26 packed bits is refused like a negative one. | Project |
| WO13 | what the installed scripts do now (`AreaWorldObjectScriptAdmissionTests` real-data check, `DAT_ROOT`) | 117 installed scripts use one of the 13 commands. S37 admits 84 of them whole (S36: none); the host sets admit them whole: use 25, tile and sector (placed) 43, heartbeat 30, dying 48. The rest hold one of the four commands no host applies (transfer item, set written start, destroy item named, toggle item inventory display) or a command of another planned slice. Beside the earlier censuses: the NPC state check (NS8) moves to placed 120, heartbeat 91, dying 147 (use 56); the story check (SQ14) to use 24, placed 37, heartbeat 26, dying 52; on the 79 maps map mode opens, 151 more tile scripts run without a teleport and 151 fewer are refused, and 2 more sector scripts run (`AreaUseScriptsOriginalTests`). | Project |
| WO14 | the four item commands in the exe (ARC-SCRIPT-1279, read again at 0x447220 cases 0x29, 0x5E, 0x7E, 0x7F) | The finder 0x462480(holder, name) returns the first entry of the holder's inventory list (container list 0x3B/0x3C for type 2, critter list 0xED/0xEE for types 15 and 16; any other type nothing, even a null handle) whose field 0x16 (the name class) equals the name; worn items are in that list. Transfer item runs the finder on the first object and then the insert 0x4617F0(item, new holder) = 0x461810 with slot −1: an item that already has a parent goes through the transfer 0x461CA0, which runs the new holder's transfer and the item's remove-item and insert-item scripts and the host's room and weight tests (0x466510); a refused insert returns 0 and the item stays. Destroy item named runs the finder on the first object of operand 1 and then the destroy 0x43CCA0 (the destroy event of point 2 first, which can stop the destroy; for an item with a parent the removal 0x466E00 with its remove-item event; then the object flag 0x1). Set written start writes field 0xCB through 0x4EFDD0 only when the object's type (field 0x14F) is 13. Toggle item inventory display flips bit 0x10 of the field 0x57 for the types 5 to 14. Every failure path of the four returns the same value as success: the run goes on. | Original |
| WO15 | what bit 0x10 of the item flags is | The inventory window's painter (0x576520, param 0) skips an item that has bit 0x10 and its other pane's painter (0x578330) does the same for gold and ammunition; a painter called with param 1 clears the bit (0x576520 from the window's opening, 0x5727B0, at its end) and the code that takes an item in hand sets it (0x4A50D0, 0x4A52C0) as does the written item's case of 0x551A80. So the bit is the window's "held, do not draw" mark that scripts can also flip; it is saved with the item (field 0x57). Not built here: the viewer's inventory painters do not read the session's flipped bit (see WO17). | Original |
| WO16 | what this build keeps for the four commands (ARC-SCRIPT-1279) | Session: `QueryNamedItem` (the finder), `QueryNamedItemDestroy` and `DestroyNamedItem` (refused before any effect for an item with a destroy or worn wield-off script, or with a remove-item script when the caller gives no script host (ARC-SCRIPT-2854: with one the removal runs point 28, IE3) (a hero's item counts the points of the seed row it was taken from and of its worn slot), a spell run that has it as its source or worn item spells, or a spent deleted-record budget), `QueryNamedItemTransfer` and `TransferNamedItem` (hero and NPC, hero and container; refused for two holders neither of which is the hero, a worn item leaving an NPC or container or carrying item spells, an item with an insert-item, remove-item or wield-off script (a hero's worn item with any), an item that is the source of a running spell, a new holder with a transfer script (point 32), a container off the actor's map or spilled, a holder no attached map lists, and, to the hero, a gold pile or a key the key ring cannot take; each plan carries the failure its move would answer), `SetItemWrittenStart`, `ToggleItemDisplay` (one exclusive-or parity per item), `QueryItemFieldWrites`; the fields are `inventory.itemFields` of the save, schema 94 (step 93 adds the empty list), dropped with a deleted item. Host: `AreaUseScriptHost` applies the four in its use, placed, heartbeat and dying sets (`WorldObjectHostActions` is now all ten actions); the item row must be a row of a loaded map (type 5 to 14, 13 for the written start), the PC and any other type do nothing; a transfer the holder's insert check or a full list declines leaves the item and the run goes on. The reading window's facts (`AreaInventoryHost.WrittenFacts`) take a written start over the prototype's. | Project |
| WO17 | limits of the four commands | (1) Transfer item between two holders neither of which is the hero (NPC to NPC, NPC to container) is refused: the session has no such move; the action stays unapplied for such a run. (2) The moves run no item or holder script (the original runs the transfer, insert-item, remove-item and wield-off scripts): an item with one is refused, a hero's item into an NPC with a transfer script (point 32) is refused by the session. (3) Destroy item named runs no destroy script (point 2) and no remove-item event: an item with one is refused; the destroy of a worn item of the hero goes through the session's one destroy path without the unwear effects it would have in the original (such an item with a running spell is refused). (4) The finder looks only at holders an attached map lists (a companion's carried home map included); a holder of an unattached map does nothing. (5) The session's insert check and list budgets answer the move (a refused insert or a full list leaves the item, as in the original); the host treats every budget refusal of the move (the session's own NPC item total, ammunition and durability records, a 32-bit unit sum, besides the list length) as the original's room test, so the item stays and the run goes on; key rings and gold are not moved by name. The pre-check is not a full dry run: it reads each limit against the state before the run, so two actions of different names that together pass a limit (a key ring with room for one more key, one deleted-item record left) leave the second unapplied after the first (the run stops there, as any refusal in an "applied as it goes" host does); a refusal the session could not foresee at the plan (none is known after the checks above) would leave earlier steps of the run applied, as every refusal in the "applied as it goes" hosts does; a run with two item actions (or a create item before one) on the same holder and item name is refused whole, because the second would be planned on the list before the first. (6) The flipped display bit is saved and queryable, but the viewer's inventory painters do not read it and the window's opening does not clear it (WO15). (7) The run's own later conditions do not read an item moved or destroyed earlier in the same run (no admitted condition asks for an item's holder; the dialog `IN` conditions are another host). (8) Set written start of a schematic document changes the schematic number it registers (the facts the reading window passes carry the start line); the written start of an item no loaded row names is refused. | Project |
| WO18 | what the installed scripts do now (`AreaWorldObjectScriptAdmissionTests`, `DAT_ROOT`) | Of the 117 installed scripts that use one of the 13 commands, S37 still admits 84 whole; the hosts' sets admit more of them since the four item commands have a host: use 39 (was 25), placed 69 (was 43), heartbeat 35 (was 30) and dying 77 (was 48). Beside the other censuses: the NPC state check (NS8) moves to placed 123 and dying 150 (use 56, heartbeat 91), the story check (SQ14) to use 33, placed 47 and dying 62 (heartbeat 26, talk 26). | Project |

## Virgil's altar talk and the world-map reply l: (ARC-SCRIPT-1358)

Source: issue #1358 (owner report 2026-11-12: the world-map reply of Virgil's altar talk, node 250 of DLG 1324, was not selectable in the tested package; in the original it ends the talk and opens the world map). Question: which reply goes to the world map, and what does the original do with it? Read on 2026-11-12 from the installed data through the content stack (`ScriptContent`, `Arcanum4.dat` copy of script 1324), private read, no text kept, and from the executable's exports (reply builder 0x416C10, state setter 0x414810, dialog interface 0x567E30, map open 0x560760 and 0x5607E0); F1.

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VA1 | node 250 of DLG 1324 and its replies | Node 250 is one NPC line (condition 41) in which Virgil hints that the map offers a faster way to the nearest town. Its three replies are records 251 (intelligence gate 1), 252 (5) and 253 (−4), each with response 0 and no action. Record 252 and 253 are ordinary closing lines; **record 251's text is the generated marker `l:`** (the 2300–2399 PC line, GP1), so the reply the player sees there is a generated one. | Original |
| VA2 | marker `l:`, option builder 0x416C10 and state setter 0x414810 (case 0x1E), dialog interface 0x567E30 (case 6) | A reply whose text is exactly `l:` (either case) gets a generated PC line (2300–2399) and, when picked, runs the reply's action, then sets the dialog state 30 (GP1). The setter turns that into the dialog's own state 6 and nothing else. The interface's state 6 is the world map: when the dialog's PC is the local PC (0x40D9F0) it runs a UI helper (0x4C9720, not read further) and the map open (0x560760, which clears four map words and calls 0x5607E0). The reply's response value is not followed and no script line is resumed. | Original |
| VA3 | map open 0x5607E0 (via 0x560760) | The same function the bar's Map button reaches. It first handles an already open map (0x560F40); then it returns silently for a missing PC or one of two PC states (0x45D8D0, 0x45D800), and refuses with game messages (0x25A, 0x25D, 600) for the cases it tests before the world-map interface mode is entered; the viewer's `OpenWorldMap` is this build's model of it. The talk is already over when it runs. Only these first branches were read here; the rest stays F0. | Original |
| VA4 | what this build did before | `l:` was grouped with the unsupported generated interactions (`DialogOptionBlockKind.UnsupportedInteraction`), so the reply was drawn but could not be picked (the tested package). Its closing replies 252 and 253 returned to script line 15, which returns and skips the default (tile script 1328 sets flag 2380 and calls Virgil's dialog point at line 14 of script 1324; nothing else is on that path). | Project |
| VA5 | what this build keeps now (ARC-SCRIPT-1358) | A port `IDialogWorldMapPort` (`OpenWorldMapCommand`) offers `l:` in a map-mode talk: the pick runs the reply's action, applies the port and ends the talk with `DialogEndKind.WorldMapWindow` (transition `DialogOptionTransition.WorldMap`, no script continuation, the response not followed, a dialog transfer or window in the same reply stays blocked). `AreaConversationHost.TakeWorldMapRequest` hands the request to the viewer, which opens the world map once the dialog window is gone through the Map button's own function (`OpenWorldMap`, so its refusals apply). Outside map mode the port is not offered and the reply stays blocked. Limit: the original's PC test is the local PC only; the UI helper call (0x4C9720) is not modelled. | Project |

## Get location and the Crash Site Molochean agent (ARC-SCRIPT-1359)

Source: issue #1359 (the owner's report of 2026-11-12: the heartbeat 1717 of the Crash Site's Molochean agent was refused at its line 9 with action 95 outside the heartbeat set, right after the altar talk) and the [script trigger audit](../script-trigger-audit.md). Read from the exports in `research-private/`: the action table 0x44ADBC (index type − 1) in the executor 0x447220, entries 73 (run to) and 94 (get location), and the object field reader 0x406DA0 (F1; the SHA-256 of `Arcanum.exe` is the one the exports were made from).

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| GL1 | get location, action 95 → action table entry 94 → 0x406DA0 (field 2, the object's location) | The first object of operand 0 (0x44B960) goes to the object field reader with field 2, which answers the 64-bit location (the low word the tile x, the high word the tile y); the low word is stored into operand 1 and the high word into operand 2 by the store routine 0x44BB50, each as 32 bits. Run to (74, table entry 73) reads the first object and the two numbers and calls the goal starter 0x434400 with mode 3 (running); it is the action set 69 and 74 already read in [GT154–GT161](../../architecture/goal-table.md). No test of the object: an absent object reaches the field reader as the null handle. | Original |
| GL2 | what this build keeps (ARC-SCRIPT-1359) | Set S38 is S37 plus get location (95); `IScrWorldQueries.Location` answers it, so a host that answers the world questions already answers it. The interpreter writes the tile's x and y into operands 1 and 2, cut to 32 bits like distance between (71); an absent object, or one without a place, stops the run as distance between does (a project choice: the original reads the null handle's location). A literal number in operand 1 or 2 is refused (`WriteToNumber`). Of the host sets the **heartbeat set** holds action 95 (`AreaUseScriptHost.HeartbeatAdmission`, and the dying set, which is built on it): run to (74) was already in it since ARC-DEV-231, so heartbeat 1717 (global flag 2323 set: set day and night standpoint to a literal tile, get location, run to) is admitted whole and starts the goal 74 run through the AI host. With DAT_ROOT, of the 270 installed scripts with an NPC state action (NS8) the heartbeat set now admits 94 whole (was 91) and the dying set 150 (was 147); the use and placed sets are unchanged (56, 120). The use, placed and talk sets leave 95 out: tile script 2080 (also uses 22, 45, 46, 95, 99) is a separate task. | Project |

## Vitals commands and the food use script (ARC-SCRIPT-1481)

Source: issue #1481 (the gap of the poison survey, [poison-sources](../poison-sources.md): the food use script 30282, 729 placed sites on 27 maps, was refused whole) and the [script refusal census](../script-refusal-census.md). Read on 2026-11-13 by the implementing session with the exports in `research-private/` (F1): the executor 0x447220 cases 107, 108 and 128, the vitals functions 0x43D5A0, 0x43D600, 0x43D510, 0x45D670, 0x45D700, 0x45D550, the stat getter 0x4B0490 with the range tables 0x5B5194 and 0x5B5204 (data dwords), the item use 0x462CC0 (rows IU1–IU5 above) and the installed script 30282 itself, read as data (its lines are paraphrased, none is quoted).

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VT1 | get hit points, action 107 (0x447220 case 107; 0x43D600, 0x43D5A0) | Operand 0 is the object (the first of its list, 0x44B960, no null test), operands 1 and 2 are stores (0x44BB50). Operand 1 receives the **current** hit points (0x43D600: the maximum 0x43D5A0 minus the hit-point damage 0x43D510), operand 2 the **maximum** (0x43D5A0: the base and adjustment sum, plus the effects, for a PC or NPC; a non-critter has its own branch). The run goes on. | Original |
| VT2 | get fatigue points, action 108 (case 108; 0x45D700, 0x45D670, 0x45D550) | The same shape for fatigue: operand 1 the current fatigue (0x45D700: the maximum 0x45D670 minus the fatigue damage 0x45D550), operand 2 the maximum (0x45D670: zero for a non-critter, else the constitution, willpower and level terms and the effects). | Original |
| VT3 | heal poison, action 128 (case 128; the stat setter 0x4B0980, getter 0x4B0740, ledger PZ1, PZ4) | Operand 0 the object list (0x44B4E0, with its count), operand 1 the amount: each object's poison level (stat 24) becomes the level minus the amount, floored at 0, through the setter (which schedules the poison event, TE10). No critter test is in the loop. A negative amount raises the level (the setter clamps to 0..1000). | Original |
| VT4 | magick-tech adjustment, action 91 (ledger WE2, EQ9), eye candy, action 120 (WE4), destroy, action 68 (TD1, TD2) | Unchanged: 91 stores `(ratio × value + 50) / 100` with the ratio of the item for its owner (100 for a neutral item), 120 plays a script effect on an object, 68 destroys the object (an item leaves its holder's list). | Original |
| VT5 | stat getter 0x4B0490 for stat 24, tables 0x5B5194 and 0x5B5204 | Stat 24 (the poison level) has no special branch: the value is the base level (0x4B0740) plus the object's effects (0x4EA690) clamped to the stat's range **0 to 1000** (the minimum table's entry 24 is 0, the maximum table's 1000). No installed effect changes it (PZ6), so it reads the level written by the setter. get stat (33) calls it as GS1 says. | Original |
| VT6 | the food use script 30282, "generic magic potion" (installed data; attached to 729 food items, use point 1; item use 0x462CC0, IU2 gives its roles: the user is the triggerer, the item the attachee, the target the **extra object**) | Counter 2 of the attachment selects the effect: 0 hit points, 1 fatigue, 2 poison, 3 all three; counter 0 is the base amount, counter 3 the item's threshold and counter 1 the eye candy. In order: the script reads the target's current and maximum hit points (or fatigue, or the poison level through get stat 24) and, **when the target is already whole (mode 3: all three whole), returns "skip default" at once**, so a potion is not wasted. Otherwise it destroys the item, reads the user's aptitude (get stat 16), plays eye candy 47 on the target and returns "skip default" when the aptitude is at or below the negative of counter 3 (a technologist's potion fizzles after the item is gone); else (an aptitude at or below 0 raises counter 1 by one, which picks a second eye candy) it takes the magick-tech adjustment of counter 0 for the item and its owner as the amount, plays eye candy, then heals by mode: hit points (75), fatigue (76), poison (128), mode 3 all three in that order. Every path ends in "skip default", so the item use charges 4 action points and stops (IU4): the script itself destroys the food. | Original (read as data) |
| VT7 | what this build keeps (ARC-SCRIPT-1481) | Set **S43** is S42 plus 107, 108 and 128. `IScrVitalQueries` answers 107 and 108 (a host whose set holds them implements it; a question the host cannot answer ends the dispatch not modelled, nothing applied). The area use host answers the PC from the session's life and an NPC of the shown map from the world (`AreaScriptWorld.Vitals`, the combat values' life status); any other object is not modelled. Heal poison is a `ScrObjectStateChange.HealPoison` request applied through the session's heal (`LifeHeal.Poison`, the heal applier's poison write, PZ3, which is the setter's write); the hero's heal fatigue (76) goes through the same life step (an NPC's through the older `HealFatigue`). A negative heal poison amount raises the level by its size, as in the original (ARC-SCRIPT-1593: the life step is asked with the negative amount and writes the level plus the amount through `GameSessionOwner.RaisePoisonLevel`, the setter's clamp and timers). The **use set** (`AreaUseScriptHost.Admission`, and the placed and dying sets built on it) now holds 107, 108, 128, 75, 76, 91, 120 and 68: heal and destroy act as the other hosts' (an NPC of the shown map), and destroy of the **item in use** takes the food out of the hero's list (`DestroyUsedItem`); the use host answers the magick-tech ratio for the PC (`EquipmentRules.EffectivePowerRatio` of the item's complexity read from its row and the PC's aptitude; a made or kit item has no row and answers by its prototype's complexity, ARC-SCRIPT-1594, VT8) and plays no script eye candy itself (the run only asks for it: since ARC-SCRIPT-1592 the hosts keep the requests for the viewer, VT9). The inventory use passes the hero as the use's **target** (IU2: a right click and Shift on the use icon), so the script's extra object is the hero, or the critter the item cursor picked (`RunItemUse(…, target)`). A destroy of the item in use is checked before any effect is applied (`UsedItemDestructionRefusal`: held, not worn, food or scroll, budget), and the run's slot write-back is skipped once the item is gone (no orphan `use.counters` row). Admitting script eye candy and the other item actions in the shared set also lets the 68 exit tiles of the void maps (scripts 2549-2558: eye candy, then the teleport) run to their own teleport instead of the ARC-WORLD-031 fallback, with the same result. | Project limits (eye candy, items without a row) |
| VT8 | made and kit items (ARC-SCRIPT-1594, issue [#1594](https://github.com/Simarglok/arcanum-steamwoven/issues/1594)) | **Built.** A food or potion the session made (`made:n`) or the starting kit holds owns no object row and no origin map, but the original makes every object as a copy of its prototype ([object model](../../architecture/original-object-model.md)), so it carries the prototype's script array and its complexity. The use host (`AreaUseScriptHost.IsPrototypeItem`: no row, a made or kit item of the session) names such an item by the shown map; reads its use slot through `QueryItemScriptSlot` (the slot the session kept, else the prototype's element); takes the magick-tech ratio (action 91) from the prototype's complexity (`PrototypeComplexity`, the inventory host's prototype fields); and keeps a slot the run wrote in the session (`StoreItemScriptSlot`: the first write copies every non-zero element of the prototype's array onto the item, SA3, then replaces the written point; a destroyed item keeps none). The destroy of the item in use is the session's used-item destruction as for a taken item. The viewer's hero use (`ItemUseScript`) no longer refuses an item that was not taken from a map; an NPC's made item runs through the same host. Tests (`AreaVitalScriptTests`) cover a made food; a kit item takes the same session reads (`QueryItemScriptSlot`, `QueryPrototypeItemKind`) and is not tested separately. Left: a made item whose prototype complexity an instance write changed (none is written by any built action), and the kit's own creation, which was not read separately (F0). Not built still: an NPC that is not of the shown map (a companion carried from another map) is refused as a user: its use goes on without the script. | Project (built)
| VT10 | an NPC that eats (ARC-SCRIPT-1590; IU2, IU4, VT6) | **Built in the viewer.** The AI host's item use (`AiExecutorRules.UseItemNow`, 0x462CC0) asks the scene's `ItemUseScript` for an item with a use script; the viewer answers it through `AreaUseScriptHost.RunItemUse` with the NPC as the user and triggerer, the item as the attachee and the use's target ("pc" for the hero, an NPC's entity) as the extra object, as IU2 gives the roles. A script that skips the default (the food script 30282 always does) stops the use, the 4 action points are charged in a round (IU4) and the script destroys the food itself: the destroy of the item in use leaves the **NPC's own list** (`GameSessionOwner.RemoveItemFromNpc`; refused before any effect, with the other checks of the run, for an item the NPC does not hold or wears, `NpcItemRemovalRefusal`). A script that ran default lets the use go on as before (a food is spent). The heal, cure, vitals and magick-tech answers read the NPC from the world as for any NPC of the shown map. A run that is refused or not applied goes on as without a script, logged once (`ARC-SCRIPT-1590 item-use-refused`). | Original |
| VT9 | script eye candy, action 120 (ARC-SCRIPT-1592; WE4) | **Built in the viewer.** `rules/ScriptEyeCandy.mes` is read as the script list (`SpellEyeCandyTable.OpenScript`, shape `EyeCandyListShape.Script`: one entry per message from message 0, no sound offsets), with the spell data. Each host that applies a run keeps its play requests, object and number, in action order (`AreaScriptHost.TakeEyeCandies` for the dialog host, `AreaUseScriptHost.TakeEyeCandies` for the use, tile, sector, dying, combat-point and item-use runs, taken once; a run the host refuses whole shows none) and the viewer plays each on the shown map every frame (`SyncScriptEyeCandies`): the PC is the hero, another object of the shown map is its cast id (`SessionSpellCaster.CastIdOf`); `AreaEyeCandyHost.PlayScript` is the add 0x44C7A0 (play flags 0, tag −1): the entry as an overlay run of its own goal beside the object's main run, drawn like a spell's eye candy. A goal's entry is now a reference that names its list (`EyeCandyEntryRef`: the list in the bits above 24, the index below; the spell list is list 0, so every earlier run and save reads as before; no schema step), because the original's run keeps its table in its slot 12 and the entry of a script number is not the spell list's of the same number. Installed use: 149 clauses in 115 scripts name entries 1 to 74 (the poison and plague item timers and hit scripts play 30 on the triggerer, the void maps' exit tile scripts play 27, the generic magic potion 30282 a number it reads from a variable, a few dialog scripts 58 or 31 to 33 on their NPC) and one clause (the void vendor device movie script 2862) a number 438 past the list, which plays nothing as in the original. **Built (ARC-SCRIPT-1829, read in the executable):** the add has no object-type test (it refuses only a null or destroyed object, an index past the list, a limit and a full part list), so any object of the shown map with a cell plays: `EyeCandyCell` now also gives a created critter (its mover's cell, else its created cell) and an item lying on the shown map (the placed item's cell), besides the hero, the rows of the map (scenery, portals, containers, ground items by their stored cell, named by `CastIdOf`), companions and created scenery. An item that is neither a map row nor on the ground has no cell and the request is dropped (the original's overlay is not drawn for it either); a map item is anchored where it is drawn (ARC-SCRIPT-2020: the viewer's `GroundItemEyeCandyCell` reads the session's ground inventory): at its row cell while it lies there, at its placed cell after a drop, a throw or a push, and nowhere (request dropped) while the hero or an NPC carries it, it lies in a container or a junk pile, or it is destroyed or switched off. The play hands the add the object's art rotation (0x44C7A0 writes the art modifier 0x502880 of the object's current art into the block; the add turns the entry's art to it with 0x502930 for every entry with art, no flag gates it, a rotation of 0 leaves the art as it is): the hero's facing, an NPC's mover facing, a static object's stored art's rotation (`ScriptEyeCandyRotation`; `EyeCandyArt.TurnTo`: bits 9..11 of the eye-candy art id, 8 or more as 0, a negative one as 7), fixed at the play. The entry's sound (`Sound:`, 22 installed entries) plays at the object's cell when the add succeeds: at the run's begin once, or for a looping entry (`anim_forever`, 33 installed) with loop count 0 until the run's end node stops the handle (`PlayScriptSounded`, `ScriptEyeCandyPlay`); a static or part-less entry or a full part list sounds once at once; the volume is only the positional size class (the viewer plays every object but scenery as large; the scenery flags 0x40/0x80/0x100 size classes are not applied, a recorded limit). A static (non-animating) entry installs nothing for a script's play (the +0x1C word is 1, which skips every overlay and light write of the add: an entry shows nothing, not even its light, and only sounds once), so the host's refusal of such an entry is the original's own; none is installed. An entry without art (art −1) only sets the object's translucent flag 0x40 (0x43EF10; the tinting and translucency entry flags 0x10 and 0x8 do the same, no colour or alpha value; never cleared); none is installed and the flag's draw was not read. **Not built:** a looping sound of a run that outlives a map change or a load (the run's eye candy keeps drawing, the voice is not re-attached, #2021); an item carried in an inventory (an item with no map cell: the original adds the effect but its overlay shows only where the item is drawn, and where its sound sits (field 2) is unsettled, needs the oracle); the object's own pixel offsets for the overlay's anchor; an object the same run destroys before the viewer plays the request one frame later (the original plays at the action, so a puff-and-vanish shows nothing here); stop script eye candy on an object nothing played on (it ends nothing, see WE12a); the NPCs' own use scripts (the AI host's `RunUseScript`, VT8) and the wield, unwield and item-event scripts of the item host (ARC-MAGIC-1745) show none; the exploding weapon's entry 55 (a weapon effect, not this action). Opt-in census: `ScrScriptEyeCandyCensusRealDataTests`; smoke stage `script-eye-candy` (the map's exit tile script 2549 on the hero, then the same entry on a scenery, container or portal row of the map; since ARC-SCRIPT-1828 a queued stop takes the hero's run off and the row's, WE12a). | Project limits (as stated) |

## Critter-state and social conditions (ARC-SCRIPT-1733, group CRQ of the script census)

Source: issue #1733 and the [script refusal census](../script-refusal-census.md) (ARC-RES-1101: the CRQ group, the largest group the plan did not have). Read on 2026-11-13 by the implementing session with the exports in `research-private/ghidra-export/c/funcs` (the condition dispatcher 0x4451B0 and the helpers below), one reader, F1; the field numbers are the object field table's (0x13 object flags, 0x14 spell flags, 0xDA critter flags, 0x118 NPC flags, 0x129 the NPC's reaction-PC array, 0x14F object type: 0xF PC, 0x10 NPC). The tail, issue #1771 (ARC-SCRIPT-1771: CS12 to CS16, set S54), was read the same day by the implementing session in the same exports, one reader, F1: cases 11, 19 and 34 of 0x4451B0, the bad-associates test 0x45F460 with the follower list builders 0x441260 and 0x4411C0, the art id helpers 0x502700 and 0x503F20, the field reader 0x406CA0 (field 0x18, obj_f_aid) and the tile distance 0x4B96F0 (with its call in case 34, read in the listing).

ARC-SCRIPT-1772 (issue #1772) adds the rows CQ1–CQ8 below: the getters 38, 39 and 89, toggle invulnerability (57) and rename (92), read from the same dispatcher (0x447220) on 2026-11-13 by that session, F1.

Every case reads its operand lists with the one routine of the other object tests (0x44B4E0 the objects, 0x44B8F0 frees them) and decides by the one shared tail: the count of objects that pass, true when all pass, or when one passes and the operand's focus kind is one of the any kinds (an empty list is false). Cases 10, 28 and 29 take the first object of each of two operands (0x44B960) instead.

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| CS1 | obj met PC before, condition 10 (case 10 → 0x4C0C40, 0x4C12F0) | Two objects, an NPC and a PC (the census names `oo`). The same handle twice is true; the second object not a PC (type 0xF) is false; the first not an NPC (0x10) is false; else whether the PC is found in the NPC's reaction slots 1..9 (slot 0, the conversation slot, is not looked at), so during the first talk it is false and after its end true (the ledger rows R5 and R6 of the [social ledger](social.md) say the same of the dialog's `ME`). | Original |
| CS2 | obj is invisible, condition 15 (case 15, field 0x14 through 0x406CA0) | The objects of operand 0 whose spell flags hold bit 0x1 (the invisibility spell); no type test, so any object that has the field counts. The detect-invisible bit is 0x200, not this one. | Original |
| CS3 | obj has surrendered, condition 25 (case 25 → 0x4AF210 → 0x4AAB00) | The objects of operand 0 that are NPCs (type 0x10, tested first) and whose danger state is 3. The state read tests the fleeing bit (0xDA 0x10) first (state 2), then the surrendered bit (0xDA 0x4000, state 3), then the NPC's fighting flag (0x118 bit 0x1, state 1), else 0; so a fleeing NPC that also has the surrender bit set is not surrendered. | Original |
| CS4 | obj is in dialog, condition 26 (case 26 → 0x4C1110 / 0x460460, 0x567400, 0x567420) | The objects of operand 0: an NPC (type 0x10) is in dialog when entry 0 of its reaction-PC array (field 0x129, the conversation slot) is not null; **every other object type**, the PC included, asks a callback the dialog layer registers (0x460460 → 0x567400 → 0x567420: the dialog slot of the PC in single player is always slot 0 (0x567420 picks the slot by the network flag 0x62AC6C bit 1 only), and the callback returns that slot's word at +0x1850 whichever object was named), so a scenery or item answers as the PC does. The word is **1 from the dialog start** (0x567460 writes it after the NPC's conversation slot was opened by 0x412FD0 and the first lines composed, then composes the replies with 0x567D60) **until the close** (0x5678D0, 0x567A20 and 0x5679C0 write 0; 0x567A60 sets it to 1 again), read again on 2026-11-13 (ARC-SCRIPT-1773). | Original |
| CS5 | obj can see obj, condition 28 (case 28 → 0x4AF260) | Two critters (operands 0 and 1, the first object of each, 0x44B960); the condition holds when the sight check of the first toward the second returns 0 (`if (result == 0)` in the case). The check is the [awareness ledger](npc-ai.md)'s AW1 to AW4 (1000 for an observer stunned, blinded, sleeping or unconscious, for an invisible target the observer cannot detect, for a target outside the facing, else the distance short of the perception range with the blocker and the prowling margin); the script asks it with the facing of the observer's current art, which for the hero is the rotation field it keeps (the viewer's drawn facing); the check reads the observer's critter flags 0xDA (0x10A0: stunned, blinded, sleeping), which the hero has as any critter has, and the dead-or-unconscious test of the observer (0x45D800): the same code runs for the PC. Steamwoven reads the hero's stunned, blinded (life state or written flag 0x80), sleeping (written flag 0x1000) and dead state, so the pick-pocket start (`AreaPickPocket`, which turns the hero toward the target and asks the same check) now also refuses for a blinded, stunned or sleeping hero. | Original (the check is the AI host's) |
| CS6 | obj can hear obj, condition 29 (case 29 → 0x4AF470 with loudness argument 1) | Two critters as CS5; the condition holds when the hearing check of the first toward the second returns 0. The check is AW5 of the [awareness ledger](npc-ai.md) (1000 for a stunned or unconscious listener or beyond 1000 tiles, sleeping halves Perception, a prowling source lowers it by the contest margin, the sound's blockers count); the script's loudness 1 is the normal one (`Loudness.Normal`). | Original (the check is the AI host's) |
| CS7 | obj is invulnerable, condition 30 (case 30, field 0x13 through 0x406CA0) | The objects of operand 0 whose object flags hold 0x400000 (a script's toggle invulnerability, action 57, flips it); no type test. | Original |
| CS8 | obj is undead, condition 38 (case 38, field 0xDA) | The objects of operand 0 that are NPCs (type 0x10, tested first, so the PC is never undead here) whose critter flags hold 0x4: the same list shape as obj is animal ([CP9](combat.md)), whose bit is 0x8. | Original |
| CS9 | prowling, condition 51 (case 51 → 0x45EDE0) | The objects of operand 0 that are a PC or an NPC (type 0xF or 0x10, a null handle is false) whose critter flags hold bit 0x1, the prowling bit the skill bar's button and the stealth code write (ledger ST1). | Original |
| CS10 | what Steamwoven builds | Set **S53** is S52 plus the nine conditions (10, 15, 25, 26, 28, 29, 30, 38, 51), and `IScrCritterStateQueries` answers them (a host whose set holds one implements it; null for an object it does not know ends the dispatch not modelled; an absent operand of a two-object case also stops the run, a deliberate divergence: the original passes a null handle, so two absent operands would read true for met before and one absent false). The shown map's use host answers: invisible from `SpellFlags` (the row's spell flags under the ones the session's spells wrote; the hero's written ones); invulnerable from the object flags (an NPC's and the hero's as the session has them, any other row's as stored); undead from the critter flags as the session has them; prowling from the session's concealment (the hero's, a follower's) or, for an NPC, its stored critter flag 0x1; surrendered from the session's danger record (`DangerState.Surrender`, the same read order as 0x4AAB00); met before and in dialog from the session's reaction record of the NPC (`SessionReaction.MetBefore`, `InConversation`); can see and can hear from the AI host's sight and hearing checks through `AreaScriptWorld.CanSee` / `CanHear` (`AreaAiHost.ScriptCanSee`, `ScriptCanHear`; excess 0, hearing at `Loudness.Normal`). The use, placed, tile, sector, heartbeat, dying and combat-point sets hold them, and so does the dialog host's talk set (`AreaScriptHost` hands each to the shown map's use host; it also gives obj is animal and obj is in combat there). Condition 31 and 37 are now in every one of those sets, not the combat-point set only. | Project |
| CS11 | what is not modelled (limits, each a null answer that stops the run) | **In dialog** for the PC and every non-NPC object: the dialog layer's callback (CS4) is not given to the use host, so the answer is not modelled (the viewer's conversation host holds the state; a follow-up issue wires it). **Can see and can hear** with the hero as the observer (its facing is not tracked, `AreaAiHost.SeeToward`) and for an observer or target that is not an NPC of the shown map or the hero, and while the scene has no line-of-fire map. The follow-up is issue #1773. **Invisible and invulnerable** of an object the host holds no row for. The dialog's own first talk: the dialog script runs before the conversation slot opens (GT120: the point 9 script runs first, then the dialog start 0x412FD0 opens the slot), so in a dialog script the NPC is not yet in dialog. | Project limits |
| CS12 | read, not built: obj has bad associates, condition 11 (case 11 → 0x45F460, 0x441260) | For a PC (type 0xF) the object itself, else the object's leader (0x45DDA0, none: false): over the leader's followers (the list 0x441260 builds, freed with 0x4410E0) the first follower that has spell flag 0x4000 (summoned), no NPC flag 0x8000 and no critter flag 0x8 (an animal) and whose object flags 0x102 are both clear makes it true; a follower with 0x102 set only logs a warning and the walk goes on. Left to its own issue with condition 34 (within range) and the getters 38, 39, 89 and actions 57, 92. | Original (read), not built |

## Cast actions in the dialog host (ARC-SCRIPT-1168, E4-g rest)

Source: issue #1168 (script hooks for spells, plan row E4-g) and the earlier reads GT183/GT184 of the [goal table](../../architecture/goal-table.md#the-s14d-read-of-the-executable) (the cast actions 48, 61, 118, 121, 122, 136 in the executor 0x447220, level F1). This slice needed no new read of the executable: the question was which hosts run the actions the interpreter already hands out. Audit of 2026-11-13 (project reader, against `origin/main` and the installed scripts, `DAT_ROOT`): of the issue's list the four spell conditions and give spell mastery were built by MT21a/MT21b (SC1–SC9), the PC as a script caster by ARC-DEV-244 (the hero's casting goal), and paid spellcasting is the healer reply `h:` (ARC-SCRIPT-1732, [HLR1–HLR12](social.md#healer-reply-arc-script-1732)); the "100 coins" the magic evidence names for services is the healer menu's price on the reaction scale (0x4B1740 returns the base 100, 0x419C30 hands it to the dialog state 0x11), not a separate spell-book rule. What was left was the dialog host.

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| CA1 | the cast cases in a dialog's script (0x447220, GT183) | The executor is one routine for every script point (the dialog point included): a cast action fills an invocation (the source or none, the spell, the target, flags 0x2 free and 0x4 unresistable) and calls the dispatcher 0x455AC0, with no starter, gate or type test of its own, so a talk script's cast acts like a use script's. The goal a cast starts (26 or 82, with 84 after 82) is GT184's; a cast with no caster starts none and is processed by a magic-tech event 2 ms later. | Original (GT183, GT184) |
| CA2 | what this build keeps (ARC-SCRIPT-1168) | The dialog host's talk sets (`TalkAdmission`, `SkillTalkAdmission`) hold the six cast actions (S29's) beside the damage, heal and attack actions, and only while the shown map's use host answers (`RunAdmission`). `AreaScriptHost.Apply` refuses the run whole, before anything applies, when the world has no script cast (`AreaUseScriptHost.CanApplyScriptCast`: the AI host), and applies each cast request in action order through the use host's own path (`ApplyScriptCast`, the code the use, tile, heartbeat and dying hosts already ran, now one method): the target an NPC of the shown map or the PC, the caster an NPC of the shown map, the PC or none; another map's object and a non-NPC caster are left as they are. The talk host's `LastCast` is the test and smoke seam. | Original (partly modelled): same limits as the use host's cast (GT183/GT184 rows of the goal-table ledger) |
| CA3 | timing | The dialog host applies a run's effects before the dialog opens (X11), so a talk's cast starts its goal as the window opens; the talk partner is held by the AI gate while the dialog is open (ARC-DEV-126), so an NPC caster that is the talk partner acts when the dialog closes. Not read in the executable whether the original's goal runs under an open dialog; the held-partner rule is the project's. | Project |
| CA4 | body-of-element bits (condition 14) | The earlier note SC7 that no spell component writes the four body bits (0x4, 0x8, 0x10, 0x20) is out of date: ARC-DEV-358 made the ObjFlag component take every bit of the spell flags (field 20) and the session keeps what it writes, which condition 14 reads for the PC and for an NPC (`AreaScriptSpellTests`, `SessionSpellCasterFlagTests` for Body of Fire and Body of Earth). | Original (modelled) |
| CA5 | what the installed scripts do now (`AreaScriptAttackAdmissionTests`, `AreaTalkQueryTests`, `DAT_ROOT`) | 80 installed scripts carry a cast action or one of the five spell commands. The talk sets admit one script with an attack more than before (11 of 79, not 10); a script that only casts (2729, 2799, 28006, 28019 and the item scripts under the use point) is admitted whole by the talk set, the rest still hold a command of another slice (get stat 33, action 91, actions 7, 18, 19, 34, 41 and others). | Data |
| CS10 | what Steamwoven builds | Set **S53** is S52 plus the nine conditions (10, 15, 25, 26, 28, 29, 30, 38, 51), and `IScrCritterStateQueries` answers them (a host whose set holds one implements it; null for an object it does not know ends the dispatch not modelled; an absent operand of a two-object case also stops the run, a deliberate divergence: the original passes a null handle, so two absent operands would read true for met before and one absent false). The shown map's use host answers: invisible from `SpellFlags` (the row's spell flags under the ones the session's spells wrote; the hero's written ones); invulnerable from the object flags (an NPC's and the hero's as the session has them, any other row's as stored); undead from the critter flags as the session has them; prowling from the session's concealment (the hero's, a follower's) or, for an NPC, its stored critter flag 0x1; surrendered from the session's danger record (`DangerState.Surrender`, the same read order as 0x4AAB00); met before and in dialog from the session's reaction record of the NPC (`SessionReaction.MetBefore`, `InConversation`); can see and can hear from the AI host's sight and hearing checks through `AreaScriptWorld.CanSee` / `CanHear` (`AreaAiHost.ScriptCanSee`, `ScriptCanHear`; excess 0, hearing at `Loudness.Normal`). The use, placed, tile, sector, heartbeat, dying and combat-point sets hold them, and so does the dialog host's talk set (`AreaScriptHost` hands each to the shown map's use host; it also gives obj is animal and obj is in combat there). Condition 31 and 37 are now in every one of those sets, not the combat-point set only. ARC-SCRIPT-1773: **in dialog** of the PC and of every object that is not an NPC of the shown map answers from the session's conversation slots (`GameSessionOwner.QueryDialogOpen`: true while any NPC's conversation slot is open, the span of the original's PC dialog slot word, CS4); **can see** and **can hear** with the hero as the observer ask the AI host, whose sight check takes the hero's drawn facing from the scene (`AreaAiScene.HeroRotation`) and the hero's stunned, blinded and sleeping state from its life state and written critter flags. | Project |
| CS11 | what is not modelled (limits, each a null answer that stops the run) | **Can see and can hear** for an observer or target that is not an NPC of the shown map or the hero, with a target that is concealed or prowls (the hero's check reads its prowling margin, AW4, which is built for a concealed hero toward an NPC and a concealed follower toward an NPC, not toward the hero: the answer is null, as is a hero observer whose facing the scene does not give), the hero toward itself, and while the scene has no line-of-fire map (the answer is null, not false: the viewer's map is null only while no area is ready, so a transfer's frames refuse and the run stops, ARC-SCRIPT-1773). The hero's step offset is not in the line (AW18: it stands at its cell centre), and its facing is the viewer's drawn octant, so a hero in an attack or fall pose is taken as the pose's facing. **Invisible and invulnerable** of an object the host holds no row for. The dialog's own first talk: the dialog script runs before the conversation slot opens (GT120: the point 9 script runs first, then the dialog start 0x412FD0 opens the slot), so in a dialog script the NPC and the PC are not yet in dialog; the first NPC line is composed inside 0x412FD0 before the PC's slot word is written, so a condition asked while it is composed would read the PC as not in dialog (no script asks it there). | Project limits |
| CS12 | obj has bad associates, condition 11 (case 11 → 0x45F460, 0x441260) | For a PC (type 0xF) the object itself, else the object's leader (0x45DDA0, none: false): over the leader's followers (the list 0x441260 builds: the direct followers in array order, then each one's own followers recursively, freed with 0x4410E0) the first follower that has spell flag 0x4000 (summoned), no NPC flag 0x8000 and no critter flag 0x8 (an animal) and whose object flags 0x102 are both clear makes it true; a follower with 0x102 set only logs a warning and the walk goes on. The usual list rule (0x44B4E0, the shared tail) runs over operand 0. Built by ARC-SCRIPT-1771 (CS15). | Original |
| CS13 | obj is monster of type, condition 19 (case 19 → 0x406CA0 field 0x18, 0x502700, 0x503F20) | Read on 2026-11-13 by ARC-SCRIPT-1771 (F1, one reader). Operand 0 is an object list, operand 1 a value (0x44BA70), the usual list rule. For each object the art id of object field 0x18 (obj_f_aid, the art the object was built with; a polymorph writes the current art, field 1, and leaves it) is read with the field reader whatever the object type (a type without the field reads 0); the art type is the top four bits (0x502700) and the monster number, for a type 0xC art, bits 23..27 (0x503F20; any other type reads 0). The object counts when the type is 0xC and the monster number equals the value. | Original |
| CS14 | obj is within range, condition 34 (case 34 → 0x44B960, 0x44BA70, 0x406DA0 field 2, 0x4B96F0) | Read on 2026-11-13 by ARC-SCRIPT-1771 (F1, one reader). Operand 0 is one object (the first of its list, 0x44B960: no list rule), operands 1, 2 and 3 are values: the range, x and y. The condition is true when the tile distance of the object's location (field 2, the 64-bit pair of tile x and y, no null test) and the point (x, y) is at most the range. The distance (0x4B96F0, the same routine as the action distance between, HW3) is the larger of the two axis differences, each the absolute difference of two unsigned 32-bit coordinates held as 64 bits; the comparison with the range is signed 64-bit, so a negative range is never met. The compiler builds the point's y word as operand 3 or-ed with the sign extension of operand 2, so a negative x makes y all ones (a distance of about 4.29e9 on y, never met by a range of a signed word); no installed script passes a negative coordinate. | Original |
| CS15 | what Steamwoven builds (ARC-SCRIPT-1771) | Set **S54** is S53 plus the three conditions (11, 19, 34). Obj is within range is the interpreter's own test over the world's `Location` (`IScrWorldQueries`, as distance between: a host without it is refused at the run options; an absent object or one without a place stops the run, as there). `IScrCritterStateQueries` gains `HasBadAssociates` and `IsMonsterOfType`. The shown map's use host answers: **bad associates** for the hero, or for an NPC the hero leads (the only leader the session holds: `Leader`), over the session's party followers on the shown map: a follower whose spell flags (`SpellFlags`), NPC flags (the data's under the session's NPC layer), animal critter flag and object flags (the data's under the session's layer) pass the four tests makes it true; a follower the host holds no row for is unknown, so the answer is null unless another follower makes it true (a follower on another map is not read); an NPC without the hero as leader, and any non-critter, is false; an object with no row is null; **monster of type** from the row's own field 0x18 (slot 23): an NPC whose field does not read is null, any other row without it false, the hero false (its base art is a human critter art built from its race and gender, ledger NW3). The use, placed, tile, sector, heartbeat, dying, combat-point and the dialog host's talk sets hold the three conditions. | Project |
| CS16 | what is not modelled (limits) | **Bad associates**: the followers of a follower (the recursion of 0x441260) and an NPC that leads other NPCs are not modelled (the session holds the hero's party only); a summon cast by an NPC is not walked. A hero follower that has no row on the shown map answers unknown. | Project limits |
| CS17 | installed use of in dialog, can see and can hear (ARC-SCRIPT-1863; opt-in `ScrHeroSightCensusRealDataTests`, `AreaHeroSightScriptRealDataTests`, `DAT_ROOT`) | Census (retail 1.0.7.4 install): 21 clauses in 16 scripts ask in dialog (26), 18 in 17 ask can see (28), 5 in 5 ask can hear (29). The PC is the first operand of the dialog condition only (script 1130 line 19, script 3073 line 5); **no installed sight or hearing clause names the PC as the observer** (the first operand is always the attachee or a looped object, never the Player or the Triggerer), so the hero-as-observer path of ARC-SCRIPT-1773 is used by the AI host's own checks only. Run against the installed content under the use host: script **2602** (an NPC's heartbeat, admitted whole by the heartbeat set) asks can see of the attachee toward the PC at line 0: false goes to line 4, true goes on to line 1, and the host asks the sight check once with the attachee and the PC; script **1130** (an NPC's heartbeat, admitted whole) asks in dialog of the PC at line 19: a conversation slot open anywhere returns run default at once, closed goes on through lines 20 to 22. Script 1130's line 0 returns run default, so a heartbeat never reaches line 19 here; the check enters it as a deferred call does. | Project (checked on installed data) |
| CQ1 | get social class, action 38 (0x447220 case 0x26, reading field 0x128 itself) | The first object of operand 0 (0x44B960) and the number store of operand 1. The object's type (field 0x14F) is tested first: an NPC (0x10) gives its social class field 0x128, every other type, the PC (0xF) included, gives 0. The value is stored with the one store routine (0x44BB50) and the run goes on. | Original |
| CQ2 | get origin, action 39 (case 0x27 → 0x45D290) | As CQ1 with field 0x123, the origin the reputation effects match (RP5) and the dialog action `or` writes (DAC12): an NPC only, 0 for any other type. | Original |
| CQ3 | get faction, action 89 (case 0x59 → 0x45D080) | As CQ1 with field 0x124, the faction a script's set faction writes (HW4): an NPC only, 0 for any other type. | Original |
| CQ4 | toggle invulnerability, action 57 (case 0x39, 0x4EFF50, 0x4EFEE0 over 0x43D0E0, 0x43D280) | Each object of the operand-0 list (0x44B4E0) in list order: its object flags (field 0x13) are read and, when bit 0x400000 is clear, the flag setter sets it (0x4EFF50 → 0x43D0E0), else the flag clearer clears it (0x4EFEE0 → 0x43D280); no type test, no result. Both routines treat bit 0x400000 as a plain bit (the special cases are bits 0x2, 0x4, 0x80000, 0x100000, 0x10000000 and 0x20000000, so no light or other side effect follows for this bit) and then tell the network layer in a network game. The run goes on. The flag is the one condition 30 reads (CS7). | Original |
| CQ5 | rename, action 92 (case 0x5C, 0x4EFDD0 with field 0x16) | The first object of operand 0 (0x44B960) and the number of operand 1 (0x44BA70); the field 0x16, the object's **name number** (the class that name conditions compare: is named 20, the dialog's follower-named test PA, the item finders; the shown text comes from the description, not from this field, [mes reader](../../formats/mes-reader.md)), is written with the plain field write (0x406D40) and sent to the network layer in a network game. No type test: any object type takes it; a null handle reads and writes through no object (undefined). | Original |
| CQ6 | what Steamwoven builds | Set **S56** is S55 plus the five actions (38, 39, 57, 89, 92). `IScrCritterFieldQueries` answers the three getters (`SocialClassOf`, `OriginOf`, `FactionOf`; a host whose set holds one implements it; null for an object the host has no row for ends the dispatch not modelled; an absent operand stops the run, the project rule of single-object reads). The use host reads the NPC's fields from the row (social class), the origin through `AreaConversationHost.Scope` (the dialog action's set origin over the map data's) and the faction the same way (a script's set faction over the map data's); the PC and any other row answer 0. | CQ7 | what the two writes do here | **Toggle invulnerability** goes through the session's object-flag write (`WriteObjectFlag`, field Object, bit 0x400000) for the hero and an NPC, flipped over the state the session has now (the stored bit under the written ones), so two toggles of one object in a run, or in two runs, follow each other and a flip back to the map data's value leaves no record. The interpreter reads a toggle back in the same run (a later condition 30 sees the flipped state, as the original's write is at once). **Rename** writes the new object field `npc.name` of the NPC (`SessionNpcName`, a mobile object field like `npc.origin`, kept only while it differs from the map data's, no schema step: an older save holds none); the readers of an NPC's name number (`AreaUseScriptHost.NameOf`, condition 20 and the dialog's follower-named test) consult it, and the interpreter reads a rename back in the same run for a later is-named test of the object. | Project |
| CQ8 | what is not modelled (limits) | **Toggle invulnerability** of any object that is neither the hero nor an NPC (the flags of a static or an item are the map data's: the run is left unapplied, refused by name). **Rename** of the hero (its name number is not kept; condition 20 reads 0 for it) and of any object that is not an NPC (an item's name number is read by the inventory layer and the item finders from the map data; a kit or created item has no row): refused the same way. **Get faction** after a set faction of the same object in one run: ends the dispatch not modelled (the host applies the set after the run). Since ARC-COMBAT-1832 the written flag also reaches the damage, targeting and hostility readers below (CQ9–CQ11). | Project limits |
| CQ9 | every exe reader of object flag 0x400000 (invulnerable, field 0x13), read by ARC-COMBAT-1832 on 2026-11-16 (full scan of the decompile and the listing; the getter is 0x406CA0 with field 0x13), F1 | **Damage step 0x4B4390** (the step every attack and spell damage passes, TEST at 0x4B4491): a target that is invulnerable or destroyed (bit 0x1) ends the step at once, for every object type: no hit-point, fatigue or poison loss, no critical effects, no text or sound; the target's taking-damage script (attachment 0x17) runs first and its false result also ends it; for a critter the nigh-invulnerable test (critter flags 2, 0x200) follows and zeroes the damage unless the weapon is "uber". The two flags are independent. The same step (TEST at 0x4B4E81): an invulnerable item in the defender's weapon slot (1004) clears the critical results damage-weapon (0x4000), 0x8000 and drop-weapon (0x10000). **Spell target filter 0x4F2D20** (TEST at 0x4F3B0F): the target is refused when the area's high-word bit 0x2000 is set (every spell record but 1, 2 and 9). **AI**: target validity 0x4AB990 (destroyed, off, not drawn or invulnerable is no target); find target 0x4AB460 (the searching NPC itself invulnerable, nigh-invulnerable or the decoy scans nothing); the spell and item choice 0x4ABC20 (such an NPC uses none); the danger setter 0x4AABE0 (such an NPC is forced to NONE and loses the flee and surrender flags); the attacked-by reaction 0x4A9650 (an off, undrawn or invulnerable victim returns before any reaction). **Script condition 30** (0x4451B0, TEST at 0x4463ED, CS7). **Hover windows**: the scenery window 0x555D80 and the container window 0x556040 leave out the hit-point line (the portal, item and critter windows do not test it). **Inventory on destroy 0x4639E0** (TEST at 0x463AD1): an invulnerable item of a destroyed container or critter is taken out and dropped. Every refusal is silent; no reader prints a message. | Original |
| CQ10 | the writers and the negative findings of flag 0x400000 | **Writers:** the built-in prototype builder 0x468930 (wall 1002 and scenery 4012, 4013, 4015, 4016, 4018–4025, 4027, 4047, 4049; prototype files may carry it as data); the object break 0x43F1C0 sets it on a broken scenery object without scenery flag 0x20 before the bust script, so a broken scenery object is never damaged again; toggle invulnerability (CQ4); the generic flag setter and clearer. No spell effect writes it (0x454A10 and 0x4CCF70 touch only 0x40, 0x80, 0x200 and 0x40000). **Not tested by the flag:** the critter hit 0x4B2870, the hit and miss roll, the hero's attack start and cursor, ranged and projectile start, the area collection (it goes only through 0x4F2D20), heal and status application, traps, steal, loot, pick lock, the hover window of a critter, a portal or an item. The hero may attack or aim at an invulnerable object: the taking-damage script fires and the damage step then does nothing. The kill path writes 32,000 hit points of damage (G, not re-read) so no flag reader is expected on it. | Original (the kill path F0) |
