| CQ11 | what Steamwoven models of flag 0x400000 (ARC-COMBAT-1832) | **Modelled, one source:** the damage step (`AttackResolver.DamageStep`, the life's damage `LifeRules`), the spell target filter (`SpellTargetRules`, high 0x2000), the item-wear weapon rule (`GameSessionWear`), the AI readers (`HostilityRules`, `SupportRules`, `DangerRules`), condition 30 and the hover lines were already built from the map data's flag; since this slice the **session's written flag** (the toggle of CQ4, the hero's and an NPC's `QueryObjectFlags`) lies over the data's in the life facts (`WithHoldFlags`: hero, NPC life target, damage), the NPC combatant the damage step reads (`SessionCritterCombat.WithInvulnerable` through `WithNpcEffects`, so the attack, throw, spell damage and AI paths) and the AI host's hostility facts of the hero and an NPC. A flag the session cleared turns the data's flag off. **Open:** the toggle of a non-NPC object (CQ8); the invulnerable items of a destroyed holder are dropped since ARC-WORLD-2493 for a script-destroyed map NPC ([DD1–DD8](#inventory-on-destroy-arc-world-2493)). | Project limits |

## Inventory on destroy (ARC-WORLD-2493)

Source: the exe read of 0x4639E0 and its callees by ARC-WORLD-2493 on 2026-11-17 (the Opus subagent's report, no decompiled text kept), F1.

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| DD1 | inventory on destroy (0x4639E0) | The one caller is object destroy 0x43CCA0 (call at 0x43CE00, mode 1), reached for a container (type 2) or a PC or NPC (types 15 and 16), after the destroy script (point 2) and the critter clean-up; it is reached for a critter alive or dead (the destroy does not test death), and the death routine 0x45DA20 reaches neither this function nor the destroy. Any other holder type returns. | Original (F1) |
| DD2 | junk pile and regeneration (0x4639E0, 0x49B290, 0x463E20) | A holder whose prototype number (0x49B290) is 3023, a junk pile, returns at once. A container with flag 0x200 of field 0x38 is regenerated first (0x463E20, which has the editor-global test inside and clears 0x200 after filling); a critter never is. | Original (F1) |
| DD3 | the walk | The holder's location is read once; the list (container fields 0x3B/0x3C, critter 0xED/0xEE) is walked from the last index to the first on the live list, in one call; a count that differs from the array length only logs. | Original (F1) |
| DD4 | what is taken out (0x463AD1) | The one condition is object flag 0x400000 of field 0x13 (getter 0x406CA0): no no-drop (item flag 0x20), no-display, slot or type test. The take-out 0x467860 runs no remove check (0x466DA0), unlinks the item and lowers the count, sets the item's slot to -1, unequips a worn one (slots 1000..1008), refreshes the holder, sets NPC flag 0x40 on an NPC holder and runs the item's script point 28 (remove item) with the holder as triggerer. | Original (F1) |
| DD5 | the drop (mode 1: 0x466E50 → 0x466EF0, 0x468090) | The item is placed on the holder's tile by the junk-pile placement of PI3 (an existing pile with room, else a new pile gathering the tile's loose items, else loose), then Item Decay (kind 25) is set 48 game hours on unless the item has flag 0x20000 or a parent that is not a junk pile. Mode 0 would be 0x4415C0 (the tile centre, no pile, no decay); the only caller passes 1. | Original (F1) |
| DD6 | the rest of the list | An item without the flag is not touched: not flagged, moved or destroyed by this function, and the function returns nothing. What becomes of those items later (the other clean-up calls of 0x43CCA0, the object's free) was not read. | Original (F1); the rest F0 |
| DD7 | what Steamwoven models (ARC-WORLD-2493) | `GameSessionOwner.DestroyMapNpc`: the script destroy of a map NPC of the shown map (the host asks it with the NPC's cell) writes the destroy and drops the items whose flag is set (the data's `SessionItemFacts.Invulnerable`, from object flag 0x400000; a script's toggle writes none on an item), last first, on the NPC's cell by the drop rule with its decay; the budgets come first (`DestroyMapNpcRefusal`, which the script host asks in its refusal phase), so a refused drop refuses the destroy whole. ARC-SCRIPT-2880 (DD4): each item taken out runs its remove-item script (point 28) with the NPC as the triggerer (`RunDestroyRemovals`, the removal of IE3): the runs are made and prepared before anything applies, committed with the destroy, their printed lines are the result's `Messages` (the NPC's line variant); a run the session cannot apply, or an item with the point and no script host, refuses the destroy whole (`ItemScriptUnsupported`, also in `DestroyMapNpcRefusal`). A made item's and a kit item's flags read as clear (the prototype is the host's), a given taken item's come from its origin seed. | Project limits |
| DD8 | what is not modelled | The NPC flag 0x40 is not set (the NPC is destroyed); the destroy of a created critter runs the rule since ARC-WORLD-2503 (DD10); the container path is DD9; the lines the remove-item scripts print in a transform's destroy (ARC-SCRIPT-2506) are not shown (the talk host has no printed-line carrier for it); the destroy of a created critter runs no remove-item script on the items it drops ([#2903](https://github.com/Simarglok/arcanum-steamwoven/issues/2903)); an added item the body walk would destroy (no-display or summoned flag of its origin seed) is not dropped by this rule either (the original makes no such test). | Project limits |
| DD9 | what Steamwoven models for a container (ARC-WORLD-2502) | `GameSessionOwner.DestroyMapContainer`: the script destroy of a container of the shown map (`AreaUseScriptHost.DestroyScenery`, which a scenery shares: the destroy point (2) runs first and a skip default keeps the container, TD2) records the object condition Destroyed with no spill cell (so the items it still holds stay in its list and lie nowhere, DD6) and drops its flagged items (object flag 0x400000, the walk and the drop rule of DD3 to DD5, each running its remove-item script with the container as the triggerer, the plain line variant, ARC-SCRIPT-2880: made and prepared before the regeneration, committed with the destroy, refused as for an NPC, a seed item as the `item.dropped` change that the view reads as lying on the container's cell, an item the hero sold into the container as the ground record it becomes). A junk pile (prototype 3043, description 3023) is only destroyed (DD2). A container with flag 0x200 that was never generated is regenerated first (0x463E20, the break's planning `PlanBreakGeneration` and `GenerateOnceWorld`; RK5 clears every item but the persistent ones, RK13 puts the walk after it): the walk is planned over the persistent items, so a refusal of the drop's budgets comes before the generation and the destroy is refused whole; the goods the generation makes are made items, whose flags read as clear, so none of them is dropped. A container destroyed already (map data flag 0x1, a break, an earlier destroy) is left as it is, for every script action on it (`AlreadyDestroyed`: toggle state, change art, damage skip it as they skip a destroyed scenery). Limits: with no ready `GenerateOnceWorld` (or an unsupported set, or a full budget) the regeneration is skipped and the walk covers the uncleared list, so a non-persistent invulnerable item is dropped where the original would have cleared it; the refusal phase plans each destroy against the state before the run (two containers that fit alone but not together, a destroy point script that changes the list, and the map-change budget of the destroy's own edits are met only at the apply, which then fails the run; a regeneration that already ran stays); a container row with no cell is only marked destroyed (no regeneration, no drop); the generation is one revision, the destroy one more and the drops one more when any item drops. | Project limits |
| DD10 | what Steamwoven models for a created critter (ARC-WORLD-2503) | `GameSessionOwner.DestroyCreatedObject` and `DestroyCreatedObjects` (a summon's expiry or dispel, the Destroy component) take the critter's invulnerable items out and drop them before the destroy discards the rest of the list (DD3–DD6): the walk, the drop on the cell by the drop rule with its decay event and the budget refusals are `DestroyMapNpc`'s (`PlanHolderDestroyDrop`, `DropWalk`), planned for all the critters first so a refused drop destroys nothing (`DropTileUnfit`, `BudgetExceeded`). The cell is the one the caller gives (`DestroyCreatedObject`'s `cell`: the summon host passes the critter's live cell), else the cell it was made on. **Reach, measured 2026-11-17 (census of the installed item prototypes, object flag 0x400000 by object type):** none of the 799 prototypes of object types 4 to 14 (the item types) carries the flag (it is set on 30 of 45 scenery prototypes, one wall and one container only), so the goods a restock gives a created critter (made items, read as clear) never drop; the only reachable case is an item the hero gave the critter that has the flag in its origin seed (a placed map item), which the walk reads from the origin seed's facts. A made item's flag stays unmodelled (no prototype carries it). The census was a throw-away real-data test over `WorldCatalog.PrototypeNumbers` (object flags, slot 18, bit 0x400000), not kept. A created holder has no seed items, so `DestroyMapNpc`'s item-dropped map changes have no counterpart. Limit: a drop the budgets or the tile refuse (`BudgetExceeded`, `DropTileUnfit`) refuses the destroy whole, as for a map NPC, so a summon holding such an item that cannot be laid down does not expire (the spell host logs the component as not done) until the room is there. | Project limits |

## Spell-state conditions and spell eye candy actions (ARC-SCRIPT-1734, group E4-g of the script census)

Source: issue #1734 and the [script refusal census](../script-refusal-census.md) (ARC-RES-1101: the E4-g group). Read on 2026-11-13 by the implementing session with the exports in `research-private/ghidra-export/c/funcs` (the condition dispatcher 0x4451B0, the action dispatcher 0x447220, 0x456E60, 0x44C7A0, 0x44C800, 0x4CD940) and the flag name table of the executable (`OSF_*` rows). Behaviour in prose; no bytes, listings or decompiled text.

Cases 12, 13 and 16 of the condition dispatcher are one routine with three masks: the objects of operand 0 (0x44B4E0, freed by 0x44B8F0) are asked their spell flags (object field 20, `obj_f_spell_flags`, the field getter 0x406CA0, any object type) and the objects whose flags hold the case's bit are counted; the shared tail then decides as for every object condition (SG6: all of the list, or one of it when the focus kind is an any kind; an empty list is false). Condition 14 (body spell, SC1) is the same routine with the mask 0x3C.

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| SC18 | obj is polymorphed, 12 (case 0xC), obj is shrunk, 13 (case 0xD), obj has mirror image, 16 (case 0x10) | Bit 0x20000 (`OSF_POLYMORPHED`), bit 0x80000 (`OSF_SHRUNK`) and bit 0x40000 (`OSF_MIRRORED`) of the spell flags word. Nothing else is read: no type test and no art test, so a critter that wears a monster art without the bit (the Trait component writes the art, the spell's own flag component writes the bit) is not polymorphed here, and the shrunk object flag (0x80, the draw size) is a different field. The names in the executable's flag table sit at positions 48, 49 and 50, after `OSF_STONED` (0x10000, position 47). | Original |
| WE11 | play spell eye candy, 104 (case 0x68 → 0x456E60) | Operand 0 a number, operand 1 an object (the first of its list, as for every object action of this kind; none: nothing is played, the original passes a null handle on). The number n picks the entry n % 10 + (n / 10) × 6 of the spell eye candy table (the table the spell runs use for their entries 6·spell + k, EC9) and plays it on the object with run id −1 and descriptor mode 1 (EC9: places nothing itself and starts an eye-candy goal only for an animating entry; a non-animating entry shows nothing). The run does not wait for it and no result comes back. | Original |
| WE12 | stop script eye candy, 124 (case 0x7C → 0x44C800 → 0x4CD940) | Operand 0 a number, operand 1 an object (none: nothing). The remove 0x4CD940 on the script eye candy table (the table of action 120, WE4) with that entry number and run id −1, two branches by the entry's animating flag (0x20): a non-animating entry takes its parts off the object's layer lists (0x4CD9C0, the first match per layer, no run id restriction); an animating entry ends the object's running eye-candy goal for it (0x4243E0). An entry number at or above the table's size, or an entry whose id is −1, only logs a warning. Presentation only; the run reads nothing back. | Original |
| WE13 | get magictech adjustment, 80 (case 0x50) | Read earlier, not new: the item's cancel chance for attacker and target (CP7, [combat ledger](combat.md)). The census counted it as refused only because its baseline set S33 predates the combat-point set; the combat points that carry it (the weapon scripts 30032, 30076, 30199, 28007) run it since ARC-SCRIPT-1562. | Original (done) |
| WE12a | what Steamwoven builds for stop script eye candy, 124 (ARC-SCRIPT-1828; WE12, WE4, GT71) | Re-read for ARC-SCRIPT-1828 (F1): 0x44C800 is `0x4CD940(script list 0x5E2FB0, object, number, run id −1)` and nothing else; 0x4CD940 reads the entry of the number (an entry past the table logs and does nothing, an id −1 entry logs), takes a non-animating entry's parts off the layer lists (0x4CD9C0) and calls the walk 0x4243E0 for an animating one with the entry's art and the run id −1. **The walk ends the first matching run only**: it visits the object's goal-45 runs, compares the current frame's art (+0x48) and tag (+0x60, the run id) and, at the first one whose interrupt at level 6 answers success, returns 1; every other run of the entry stays, so a number played twice on one object needs two stops. A looping entry played as 47, 58 or 75 and a run queued under another is not a goal-45 run and is not reached (GT71, GT85); a script's play (0x44C7A0, flags 0, tag −1) is always a goal-45 run, so a looping script entry is stopped like any other. Built: the interpreter and the sets S55, the dialog and use hosts' sets (the use set since ARC-SCRIPT-1734, the dialog set since ARC-SCRIPT-1828, which no installed dialog script needs), `AreaScriptEyeCandy.Stop` in the hosts' request lists in action order with the plays, and `AreaEyeCandyHost.StopScript` (the walk with the entry reference of the script list and the tag −1, first match only), which the viewer calls for each stop request on the hero or an object of the shown map (`SyncScriptEyeCandies`; the voice of a looping entry stops with its run, ARC-SCRIPT-1829). Installed use (census `ScrScriptEyeCandyCensusRealDataTests`): 22 clauses in 22 scripts, 2210 to 2230 and 2231, all on the looped object (script 2231: the scenery around an NPC, its entry number read from a variable), 21 literal numbers among 2 to 26 and one variable; the use, placed and dying sets admit 18 of them whole and the heartbeat set the heartbeat 2231 (opt-in `AreaScriptEyeCandyTests`); the other four (2213, 2217, 2221, 2225: temporal, fire, phantasm and nature) are refused at line 15 for the loop over every portal in the vicinity (focus kind 19), a command of another slice (#2041; superseded: 2213, 2217 and 2225 run since ARC-SCRIPT-2041 and 2221 since ARC-SCRIPT-2248, ledger PV6 and OT6), which kept their stop from running. A scenery, container or portal with a cell has a play run since ARC-SCRIPT-1829, so a stop takes it off like any object's; an object nothing played on (no cell, or an entry that plays no run) has no run, and its stop ends nothing and says nothing. **Not built:** the layer-list branch for a non-animating entry (0x4CD9C0; no installed entry is static); the original compares the art of the entry, ours its reference, so a spell-list entry, or a second script entry, with the same art is not taken off (the lists differ, GT70); a number below 0 reads before the table in the original and ends nothing here. | Project limits (as stated) |
| SC19 | what Steamwoven builds | Set **S55** is S54 plus the three conditions (12, 13, 16), answered by `IScrSpellQueries.SpellFlags` (the hosts that answered condition 14 answer them: the use, placed, dying, heartbeat, talk and combat-point sets, which take them with the other spell queries) and the two actions (104, 124), which the interpreter hands on as `ScrEyeCandy` requests (`Kind` PlaySpell and StopScript beside the play script of 120) and the use, placed, dying, combat-point and heartbeat sets admit (the talk set takes the conditions only). The interpreter runs at most S55. | Steamwoven structure |
| SC20 | what is not modelled | The bits are read from the spell flags the session already holds (a stored row's value, the bits a spell component wrote through the ObjFlag component, which is data driven: the shrink side effect is built, SideEffects). Whether the installed polymorph and mirror image spell records carry bits 0x20000 and 0x40000 in an ObjFlag component is not read here, so those two conditions are true only for a stored bit or a written one. **No host plays the eye candy of 104 or 124** (and 120): the request is handed on and dropped, as 120 is, until #1592 builds the viewer's play and stop by table and number; the scripts run whole in the meantime. A heartbeat set stays narrow by design; the 21 main-map heartbeats that stop a script eye candy (124, attachment point 19) were refused by it, which ARC-SCRIPT-1815 corrected (the one script 2231, one missing focus kind; [HPS1–HPS6](#the-heartbeat-set-scenery-object-variable-assign-object-and-float-line-arc-script-1815)). | Limit |

## The heartbeat set: scenery, object variable, assign object and float line (ARC-SCRIPT-1815)

Source: issue #1815. Read on 2026-11-14 by the implementing session with the exports in `research-private/ghidra-export/c/funcs` (the action dispatcher 0x447220, cases 0xF, 0x17 and 0x1D, float line 0x44B1F0) and a read-only scan of the attachment points of the whole installed main map (the refusal-census probe's walk, a temporary change not kept). Behaviour in prose, no bytes.

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| HPS1 | which heartbeats stop a script eye candy | Of the 60 installed scripts that use a spell-state command (SC18) only two are attached at the first-heartbeat or heartbeat point (10, 19) anywhere in the installation: **1169** (3 sites, admitted since ARC-SCRIPT-1734) and **2231** (21 sites, all on the main map, point 19). The "21 main-map heartbeats" of the earlier note are script 2231. The scripts 2210 to 2230 (play script eye candy 120, assign object, play sound 82, conditions 21, 38, 39, focus kind 18), 2020 and 2021 and the get reaction scripts 1812 to 1824 are not attached at the heartbeat points (other points, or attached nowhere and reached by calls). Script 2231 loops over every scenery in the vicinity of its NPC (loop for with the focus kind 15), stops script eye candy 2 on each looped object (124), clears a global flag and ends the loop: its **only** refusal under the heartbeat set was the scenery focus kind. | Data |
| HPS2 | action executor (0x447220), cases 0xF (assign object), 0x17 (float line, 0x44B1F0) and 0x1D (get reaction, 0x4C0CC0); list reader 0x44B4E0 | The executor takes the clause and the run's context and never reads the attachment point: the three cases run the same at every point, and the object list reader answers the scenery and critter vicinity kinds for any run. So nothing in the original makes a heartbeat different from a use or tile script for these commands; the limits are the host's answers. The AI tick's cost is the script's own: a loop over the vicinity is a scan of the attachee's neighbourhood (capped at 100 objects by the original's list rule), run each time the heartbeat comes due for the NPC. Steamwoven's `SceneryInVicinity` scans the shown map's scenery rows for each use of the focus kind (no cache; one scan per NPC per tick, 21 NPCs for script 2231), which the original's own sector-indexed query does not need; measured small, not optimised here. | Original |
| HPS3 | what Steamwoven builds | `HeartbeatAdmission` adds the focus kinds every scenery in the vicinity (15, answered by `IScrWorldQueries.SceneryInVicinity`, the shown map's scenery within the vicinity reach not destroyed or gone) and object variable (11, the run's own store: OV2, OV4), assign object (15, the interpreter's own state: it writes the run's triggerer, attachee, extra object or an object variable and nothing else) and float line (23, the same path as a tile script: the host reads the DLG line and hands it out through `TakeFloats`, which the viewer's AI tick takes right after the heartbeat run (`Awarded`, as for any other script run: its float lines, awards and created items, which would otherwise pile up in the host); a missing DLG floats nothing, a line the NPC-line step would do more for leaves the run unapplied, FL2–FL4). The dying set (placed plus heartbeat) and the placed set already held all of them. | Original |
| HPS4 | what stays out | Get reaction (29): this host has no reaction answer (`IScrReactionQueries`, the talk host's reads the dialog's state) and none of the 16 installed scripts that use it is attached at a heartbeat point, so it stays refused (a heartbeat that reached one by call script or call-ex would be refused whole at the call, as before; counter operands above index 3 stay refused too: the heartbeat set does not wrap counter indexes) (its own slice when a heartbeat needs it). Play script eye candy (120) stays out of the heartbeat set (one installed script, 2403, at the use and tile points, not a heartbeat). Get stat 33, teleport 44, play sound 82 and 87 and the conditions 17, 38, 39 and focus kind 18 of the issue's list are used by no heartbeat of the 60 and are other slices'. | Limit |
| HPS5 | what the installed scripts do now (opt-in checks, `DAT_ROOT`) | Heartbeat admission whole, per census, after merging main (S56 to S58 sets included): spell-state scripts 7 of 60 (was 1), NPC-state scripts 160 of 270 (was 98), attack scripts 32 of 79 (was 11), story and time 41 of 96 (was 29), world-object 45 of 117 (was 35), loop break 8 of 22 (was 5), spell stop 3 of 4 (was 0); the trigger census admits 98 of 141 heartbeat rows in the shown windows (the audit table said 97; now pinned as at least 98) and 23 of 23 first-heartbeat rows. Script 2231's 21 main-map sites are admitted whole (they lie outside the census's windows). | Data |
| HPS6 | what is not modelled | The stop eye candy (124) the script issues is handed to the viewer in action order (ARC-SCRIPT-1828, WE12a) and takes the entry's first run off each scenery around the NPC that has one (a scenery with a cell plays since ARC-SCRIPT-1829); the loop and the flag run. A heartbeat's float line is shown each time the heartbeat runs, as in the original (nothing here limits how often the AI tick runs a due heartbeat beyond the existing gating); the original's own throttle of the heartbeat (the AI tick's due times, 0x4AD2E1) is unchanged here. Not verified by a native smoke run of script 2231 (its NPCs are away from the Crash Site window); the synthetic host tests run its shape. | Limit |

## The portal vicinity focus kind (ARC-SCRIPT-2041, the loop of the ground rune tile scripts)

Source: issue #2041 (found in ARC-SCRIPT-1828). Read on 2026-11-15 by a read-only exe session with the exports in `research-private/ghidra-export/c` (the object-list builder 0x44B4E0, the first-object function 0x44B960, the vicinity scan 0x440FC0 and its filter 0x440B40, the action dispatcher 0x447220, case 0x12 loop for).

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| PV1 | focus kinds 19 and 20 in the list builder (0x44B4E0) | One switch case serves both: the vicinity scan with the type mask of bit 1 (portals, type 1 only), the centre being the run's attachee (field +0x10, the same field as kinds 1 and 15; no attachee gives an empty list). The builder does not tell "every" from "any": both build the same list, the caller decides (a list action walks it all, a single-object operand takes element 0). The container (17, 18) and item (22, 23) kinds have cases of their own in the same builder (read in ARC-SCRIPT-2322, [CV1](#the-container-vicinity-focus-kinds-arc-script-2322-every-and-any-container-in-the-vicinity)); this row's "NOT built here" of the first reading was wrong. | Original |
| PV2 | the scan and its filter (0x440FC0, 0x440B40) | The reach is the game window's rectangle centred on the centre object's location, about 10 tiles on both axes (PG13, as for scenery and critters). A portal is a static object, so the scan takes the sector tile lists: its only tests are the object flag word against the play mask (destroyed or switched off drop out) and the type mask. No portal state is read: open, closed and locked portals are all listed. | Original |
| PV3 | the cap and the list order | Each case copies entries in scan order (sectors of the rectangle, tiles row by row, each tile's list order) and stops at 100, dropping the rest silently (PG3). The list is built once: a portal destroyed or switched off while a loop runs stays in the list. | Original |
| PV4 | first object of an operand (0x44B960) and the loop (0x447220 cases 0x12, 0x13, 0x14) | The first-object function builds the same list into a 100-slot array and returns slot 0, the first portal in scan order, not the nearest; for an empty list slot 0 is never written, so the returned handle is stack garbage (an original hazard, F-level, not checked at run time). Loop for stores the list in the run, sets the looped object (focus kind 10) to element 0, loop end steps it, an empty list jumps past the loop end. | Original |
| PV5 | what Steamwoven builds | The set **S60** is S59 plus the focus kind 19 (`ScrFocusKind.EveryPortalInVicinity`), which the interpreter resolves for a loop for and for a first object through the new `IScrPortalQueries.PortalsInVicinity`, under the same cap as the scenery (`VicinityListCapped`, Original profile). `AreaUseScriptHost` lists the shown map's portal rows (type 1) within `ScrWorld.VicinityReach` tiles of the centre on both axes that are not gone, in snapshot order (the original's scan order is not read), and its use, placed, dying and combat-point sets admit the kind; the heartbeat set does not (no installed heartbeat asks for it). Kind 20 is not admitted (no installed script asks the any form of a portal). An empty list gives no first object (the original's garbage handle is not copied). | Steamwoven structure |
| PV6 | what the installed scripts do now (opt-in check, `DAT_ROOT`, `AreaScriptEyeCandyTests`) | Of the 22 installed scripts that stop a script eye candy, the use and placed sets admit 21 (was 18: the ground rune tile scripts 2213, 2217 and 2225 run whole, their loop for over every portal in the vicinity included) and the dying set all 22. Script 2221 was still refused by the use and placed sets at line 18 for toggle state (56), which only the heartbeat set held; ARC-SCRIPT-2248 admits it (ledger [OT1–OT7](#toggle-state-in-the-use-set-arc-script-2248-the-ground-rune-tile-script-2221)): 22 of 22. | Data |
| PV7 | what is not modelled | The list order is the snapshot order, not the original's scan order, so which portal a first-object read takes and the order a loop visits them can differ. A portal of the map a script runs on only (like the scenery's list); portals of other loaded maps, and a portal that a script created or the AI host moved, are not listed. | Limit |

## The group focus kinds (ARC-SCRIPT-2319, everyone and anyone in the group)

Source: issue #2319 (found in ARC-RES-2262, [census](../script-refusal-census.md#the-refused-focus-kinds-arc-res-2262-2026-11-15)). Read on 2026-11-15 by one read-only exe session (level F1) with the exports in `research-private/ghidra-export/c` (the object-list builder 0x44B4E0, the first-object function 0x44B960 and its list release 0x44B8F0, the follower list helper 0x4411C0 that kinds 2 and 3 also use, its field accessors 0x4079C0 and 0x407610).

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| GF1 | focus kinds 13 and 14 in the list builder (0x44B4E0) | One switch case serves both: the builder does not tell "every" from "any" (as PV1; the caller decides, a list action walks it all, a single-object operand takes element 0). The centre is the invocation's **triggerer** (the field kind 0 returns), not the attachee: kinds 1, 15 and 19 read the attachee, kind 12 the extra object. | Original |
| GF2 | what "group" is (0x4411C0) | The triggerer's **direct followers** (object field 0xF1, in array order; no recursion, so followers of followers are not listed) and then the triggerer's own handle as the **last** element: kinds 13 and 14 are kinds 2 and 3 (every and any follower) plus the triggerer. It is not the party (kinds 4 and 5, 0x441310), nor the team (6 and 7, 0x4413E0), and no leader field is read. A waiting NPC is outside the array (OL16), a forced or charmed follower stays in it. | Original |
| GF3 | filters, cap, empty group | No type mask, no flag test (inventory, destroyed, off), no dead-critter test, no distance test: whatever handles the array holds are copied (a slot that is no handle is stored as a null handle and counted). The copy stops at 100 followers and the triggerer is written into the next slot; the return is the follower count plus one, never 0. A full 100 would write slot 100, one past the 100-slot array of 0x44B960: an original overflow, unreachable in practice. There is no early "no centre, empty list" exit as the vicinity kinds have. A triggerer with no followers is its own group: a kind-14 operand is the triggerer. | Original (the overflow is a bug of the original) |
| GF4 | first object of an operand (0x44B960) | Builds the same list into its 100-slot stack array, releases it (0x44B8F0 names 13 and 14 among the kinds whose linked list it frees) and returns slot 0: the first follower in array order, or the triggerer when there are none. Slot 0 is always written for these kinds, so the stack-garbage hazard of PV4 does not arise. | Original |
| GF5 | what a host must answer | Two things through the generic field accessors: the length of the triggerer's follower array and its elements as handles. A **PC** triggerer gives the hero's followers and then the PC; an **NPC** triggerer gives that NPC's own followers (summons, NPCs it leads) and then the NPC (an NPC follower of the PC does not reach the PC); a **non-critter** triggerer has no field 0xF1: the accessor logs "accessing non-existent field", answers length 0 and the list is the triggerer alone. | Original |
| GF6 | what Steamwoven builds | The set **S61** is S60 plus the focus kinds 13 and 14 (`ScrFocusKind.EveryoneInGroup`, `AnyoneInGroup`), which the interpreter resolves for an operand list and for a first object (`ScrInterpreter.Group`): the host's direct followers of the triggerer (`IScrGroupQueries.FollowersOf`), cut at 100 under the Original profile only (`VicinityListCapped`, as the vicinity lists; the Steamwoven profile lists every follower, the original's overflow at 100 is not copied), then the triggerer. `AreaUseScriptHost` answers from the session's follower array (`QueryParty().Followers` for the PC, `QueryFollowers` for an NPC row of the shown map, nothing for any other object); its use, placed, dying and combat-point sets admit the kinds, and the dialog talk sets (`AreaScriptHost.TalkAdmission`, `SkillTalkAdmission`) admit them through the use host. The plain dialog sets and the heartbeat set do not (no installed heartbeat asks for them). | Steamwoven structure |
| GF7 | what is not modelled | A run with no triggerer gets an empty list (the original's accessor has no null guard in the export; whether it yields a null handle or faults is not read, F0 at 0x4E5900 and 0x4E5960). The followers are named on the shown map (a carried follower stays named there, as in the leader query, ledger SWB8); an NPC whose row is not on the shown map has an empty group. That the triggerer is the invocation's field +8 is inferred from kind 0 returning it (the layout itself was not re-read). | Limit |
| GF8 | what the installed scripts do now (opt-in check, `DAT_ROOT`, `ScriptTriggerCensusTests`) | The census probe (`--set S61` against S60, one installation, 1,704 attached roots on 6,237 placed sites) admits 1,649 roots on 5,949 sites whole (1,639 on 5,888 under S60): +10 roots, +61 sites, the 61 sites of the issue. Scripts 1160, 2350, 2506, 2507 and 1749 run whole under the combat-point (hit 7, miss 8, critical hit 34) and the placed (taking damage 23) sets, and 1160 (dialog 9) under the talk set. The three roots that name kind 14 (2232, 2234, 2239; dialog point 9) were refused until ARC-SCRIPT-2326: they ask the group in the is undead condition (38) and need get armor (action 32); set S63 admits both (AR4 to AR6). | Data |
| GF9 | conditions | The five guard conditions (6, 17, 20, 21, 33; SG1–SG6) test the object list of operand 0 and kind 14 is listed among the "any" kinds of SG6 (kind 13 is "all"); script 1160 asks wields an item named (21) of a group kind. The interpreter runs these five over the group list by the list rule (`GuardHolds`) and the admission takes the group kinds in a condition only there (slot 0 of those five). The list conditions the interpreter tests object by object (is undead 38 and the other list cases of AR4) take the group kinds too since ARC-SCRIPT-2326 (set S63, [AR4](#get-armor-and-the-group-in-list-conditions-arc-script-2326)); every other condition refuses them, as the vicinity kinds 8 and 9 are refused (TG4): they read one object here (the exe's first-object cases take the first follower, AR4). | Original (SG6, AR4), Steamwoven structure for the rest |

## Get armor and the group in list conditions (ARC-SCRIPT-2326)

Source: issue #2326 (found in ARC-SCRIPT-2319). Read on 2026-11-16 by one read-only exe session (level F1) with the exports in `research-private/ghidra-export/c` (the action dispatcher 0x447220 case 0x20, the condition evaluator 0x4451B0 and its list tails, the object-list builder 0x44B4E0, the field getter 0x406CA0, the art helper 0x504030, the store helper 0x44BB50).

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| AR1 | get armor, action 32 (0x447220 case 0x20, signature object and store) | Slot 0 is an object read as the **first object** of its list (0x44B960, no null test); slot 1 is the store target (0x44BB50: counter byte, global or local variable, global flag, PC variable or flag; a number literal is a silent no-op). The value is the **armour part of the object's current art id** (object field 1 through 0x406CA0, then 0x504030). No armour class, stat, resistance or worn item is read. | Original |
| AR2 | what the value is (0x504030) | Bits 20..23 of a critter art id (art type 2), bits 20..22 of a monster art id (type 12), 0 for any other art type (items, scenery, unique NPCs). It is the look of the body armour the critter wears (0 underwear, 3 chain, 4 and 6 plate; the nibble the footstep rule SN26 reads and the wield hook sets, BA1); the worn item matters only through the art it gives. **When the art is refreshed (ARC-SCRIPT-2342, exe read 2026-11-16):** the generic insert (0x466640) writes the slot into item field 0x5E and, for a slot 1000..1008, runs the wield-on hook (0x4677B0); the removal (0x467860) runs the wield-off hook (0x467CB0) for an item whose old slot is 1000..1008. Neither tests the holder's type, so the PC and an NPC take the same path. Both hooks change the art only for the slots 1004..1006; for the body armour (1006) they call 0x465AE0 and write the result through the art setter 0x43EB30 (it does nothing when object flags bit 0x1 is set, else writes field 1, clears bit 0x8000000 of field 0x147 and redraws). Wield-on puts the armour part of the item's use art (item field 0x5F, bits 6..9, 0x5044F0) into the current art and the use art's palette; the write is **refused** (field 1 left as it was) when the art's own armour part (0x504030) does not read back as that part, so a monster art holds parts up to 7 and a unique-NPC art only 0. Wield-off sets the part to 0 and the palette from the base art (field 0x18, which neither hook writes). **Who fires them:** the inventory window's wear and take-off (0x54FCF0, 0x550000 over 0x464AE0, 0x464C80) change the art at that moment, for the PC and for a companion dressed through it; an item only put into an NPC's pack (a script give, a transfer) has a slot below 1000, so no hook runs and the art stays; an NPC's best-items wield (0x4654F0: regeneration 0x463E20, the AI gate 0x4A84F0 → 0x4A8570 on NPC flag 0x40 of field 0x118, a polymorph's restore) goes through the same insert and so the hooks. No map or save load function is among the insert's callers, so a loaded NPC keeps the art its data stored. | Original |
| AR3 | lists, null and non-critters | One value for one object: a list focus kind gives its first element (kind 13 and 14: the first direct follower, else the triggerer, GF4); no loop over the list. A non-critter whose art is no critter or monster art answers 0. A null handle goes through the generic getter with no guard in the case itself; **what the handle lookup does with 0** (ARC-SCRIPT-2342, exe read 2026-11-16): 0x406CA0 → 0x408720 → 0x4E4F80 shifts the handle right by 29 for a pool index and reads the chunk table at index >> 13 and the slot at (index & 0x1FFF) times the stride; no step tests for 0, so the handle 0 resolves to pool slot 0's object and field 1 (valid for every object type) reads **its** current art: no crash, and get armor stores that art's armour part, 0 unless slot 0 holds a critter or monster art. What pool slot 0 holds at run time was not established (F0; it needs the emulator oracle). The first-object read (0x44B960) fills a stack array of 100 entries and returns entry 0 whatever the count: for the kinds that walk a list and give an empty one (the null-base exits of 8, 9 and 0xF..0x17) it returns stale stack data, not 0, and the kinds 0, 1, 0xA, 0xB, 0xC and 0x15 always write one handle that may be null; the kinds 0xD and 0xE always hold the triggerer. This build stops the run for an absent object, as the other getters (a hard difference nobody installed reaches: F0). The sibling get armor coverage (action 111, 0x465C30) reads an armour **item**'s coverage field and is unrelated. | Original (null F0) |
| AR4 | list conditions with a group kind (0x4451B0, list tail 0x4455BD) | Condition 38 (is undead) builds operand 0 with 0x44B4E0, counts the objects of type NPC (0x10) whose critter flags hold 0x4, and every list condition ends in the same tail: true when the count is above 0 and either every object counted or the operand's kind is one of 3, 5, 7, 9, 14, 16, 18, 20, 23; an empty list is false. So kind 13 means every and kind 14 means any for condition 38 and for the other list conditions (6, 17, 20, 21, 33 as GF9, and 8, 9, 11 to 16, 19, 22, 25, 26, 27, 30, 31, 35, 37, 38, 39, 42, 47 to 49, 51, 52), as kinds 8 and 9 do for the vicinity. The group list ends with the triggerer, and a PC (type 0xF) is never undead (the type test comes first): kind 13 on is undead is **always false for a PC triggerer**, kind 14 is true for an undead direct follower or for an undead NPC triggerer. The conditions 10, 18, 24, 28, 29, 32, 34, 36, 40 and 44 read the **first object** of each object operand instead (a group kind gives the first follower or the triggerer, which never leaves slot 0 unwritten, GF4); the ones with no object operand are 0, 1, 3, 4, 5, 7, 41, 43, 45, 46, 50. | Original |
| AR5 | what Steamwoven builds | The set **S63** is S62 plus get armor (`ScrCatalogue.Action.GetArmor`, `ScrAdmissionSet.CritterArtActions`), which stores `IScrCritterFieldQueries.ArmorOf` of the first object into operand 1 (an absent object stops the run, a null answer ends the dispatch not modelled, a write to a number literal is refused, as the other getters). The admission takes the group kinds in slot 0 of the list conditions the interpreter tests object by object (`ScrAdmissionSet.ListConditions`: 8, 9, 11 to 16, 19, 22, 25, 26, 27, 30, 31, 35, 37, 38, 48, 49, 51; with the five guard conditions of GF9), through the existing list rule (`ListHolds`, kind 14 "any"); the first-object conditions and every condition the interpreter reads as one object (such as is identified, 47) keep them out. `AreaUseScriptHost.ArmorOf` answers as AR8 says (the PC and an NPC follow what the session did to the critter; any other row is read from its **stored current art**, field 1). The use, placed, tile, sector, dying and combat-point sets and the talk sets admit the action; the heartbeat set does too (it carries the use set's getters). | Steamwoven structure |
| AR6 | what the installed scripts do now (opt-in check, `DAT_ROOT`, `ScriptTriggerCensusTests`; the census probe `--set S63` against S62, 1,809 attached roots on 7,766 placed sites) | S63 admits 1,766 roots on 7,597 sites whole (S62: 1,759 on 7,487): +7 roots, +110 sites, the seven roots and 110 sites of get armor, 98 of them tile or sector sites on three maps (points 9 and 19 among them). The three kind-14 roots 2232, 2234 and 2239 run whole under the talk set (dialog point 9); the plain dialog set still refuses them. The totals are the probe's (all 1,809 attached roots on 7,766 placed sites, as CV6 counts them; GF8 counted a smaller base of 1,704 roots on 6,237 sites), and the probe's S62 run already applies the list-condition rule of AR4 (a static admission rule), so +7 roots is get armor alone. Without the viewer's body read, a hero wearing body armour is not modelled. | Data |
| AR7 | what is not modelled | The null-handle read (AR3) and the polymorph's monster art bits (AR8, inferred) are F0/F1; the AI gate's re-wield of a live NPC (AR2) is the host's (`RewieldNpc`) and its record is what AR8 reads. The hero's armour is read only while the viewer's hero body reads the worn item's use art (null: not modelled). The list conditions the exe reads as lists but `ListConditions` leaves out (39, 42, 47, 52 and 2) are the ones the interpreter reads as one object or counts differently (is identified, 47, would give the wrong form of "any"); 24, 32 and 44 are not in the project's catalogue. | Limit |
| AR8 | what Steamwoven answers now (ARC-SCRIPT-2342, issue #2342) | `AreaUseScriptHost.ArmorOf` reads the critter's **current art as the wear hooks and the polymorph leave it** (AR2): a polymorphed critter (`QueryTraitArt`: the PC as `SessionSpellCaster.Hero`, an NPC by entity) answers **0** (the apply takes every worn item off, runs wield-off, writes a monster art built from the species with the armour bits 0 (inferred, the builder 0x503CD0 was not re-read for them) and the wear check 0x464D20 refuses a polymorphed critter, so nothing can be worn; the base art, field 0x18, stays); the PC otherwise as AR5 says (bare 0, worn the part of the use art of its body armour, not modelled when the viewer's hero body does not read it; a part of 9 or more is not stored: the part setter 0x504060 writes 0 for it and the wield check refuses the item, so the answer is 0); an NPC the session dressed (`QueryNpcWorn`: a regeneration's best items, the barter and loot windows, a generated copy) answers the part its body armour's use art gives (`AreaScriptWorld.NpcBodyArmor`, which the viewer answers from the AI host's combat reads, `AreaAiHost.WornBodyArmor`; null when not read: not modelled), 0 when it wears none (wield-off), 0 for a part its stored art type cannot hold (the wield is refused, `BodyArmorArtRules.WornArt`), and not modelled when the record names body armour the world finds none of (fields not read yet, a broken item); any other NPC row answers its **stored art**, except an NPC whose map-data body armour was taken off since (ARC-SCRIPT-2351, issue #2351): the original's removal hook sets the part to 0 (AR2) whoever fires it, and the session already knows the removal without a record of its own: `GameSessionOwner.QueryNpcBodyArmorTakenOff` is true for an NPC with no worn record whose seed lists an item at slot 1006 that is no longer in its current list (a script take by name or a steal, whose removal takes a worn item off its slot as the loot does, ARC-SCRIPT-1987 WL3; the loot of its corpse; a second break that deletes it; a barter take writes a worn record and is answered as a dressed NPC) or whose durability record says broken (the first break takes a worn item off, `TakeOffAndRunBust`), and `ArmorOf` then answers **0**. An NPC whose map data lists no body armour keeps the stored part, because its art may show armour its data never listed (the census below counts them), and so does one that lost another worn item. **Census (ARC-SCRIPT-2351, installed 1.0.7.4 data, `AreaBodyArmorArtCensusTests`, opt-in `DAT_ROOT`, 2026-11-16):** of the 2236 NPC rows of the 80 loaded maps with a stored art, 2141 have an inventory holder (95 have none and list no armour): **171** show an armour part (art bits 20..23 above 0) and list a body armour (slot 1006) in their data, **53** show a part and list none (their art shows armour the data does not list: answering 0 for every NPC without listed armour would have been wrong for these), 1 shows no part and lists a body armour, 1916 show none and list none. So the stored part stays for those 53, and the taken-off answer 0 applies only after the listed armour leaves. The table test `ScrListConditionTableTests` pins that all 21 members of `ListConditions` take the group kinds by the one list rule (kind 13 every, kind 14 any, the triggerer last) and the hero's worn-armour path has its tests (`AreaCritterFieldScriptTests`), the dressed NPC's one is in `AreaNpcSessionWearTests`. | Steamwoven structure |

## Toggle state in the use set (ARC-SCRIPT-2248, the ground rune tile script 2221)

Source: issue #2248 (found in ARC-SCRIPT-2041). Read on 2026-11-15 from the exports in `research-private/ghidra-export/c` (the action dispatcher 0x447220 case 0x38, the object-flag wrappers 0x4EFF50 and 0x4EFEE0, the flag setters 0x43D0E0 and 0x43D280, the light helpers 0x4DCEA0, 0x4DCF60, 0x4D62B0, 0x4D56C0); the earlier row SU6 of [NPC AI script surface](npc-ai.md) read the same action as a plain bit flip, which this section refines (the wrappers call the setters below, and the NPC gate it describes is unchanged).

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| OT1 | action 56, toggle state (0x447220 case 0x38) | The operand 0 object list is built by 0x44B4E0 (one object for a single-object focus kind, the vicinity list for the list kinds, as PV1). For each object in turn: the object flag word (field 0x13) is read; with bit 0x2 (OF_OFF) clear the set wrapper 0x4EFF50(object, 2) runs, else the clear wrapper 0x4EFEE0(object, 2). The object's type is never tested: a portal, scenery, container, item, NPC or PC is toggled alike. An empty list does nothing. | Original |
| OT2 | set wrapper 0x4EFF50 and setter 0x43D0E0, mask 2 | The wrapper calls the setter and, only when the multiplayer bit (0x62AC6C bit 0) is set, queues a record (kind 0x81, sub-kind 3) for the other side. The setter, when the bit was clear: if the object's lit flag 0x8 is set, ends its light (0x4D62B0: the light-table entry for the object is freed, else the flag is cleared); if flag 0x200000 is set, takes the object off the list at 0x6028E0 (0x4D56C0, which clears 0x200000); marks every light field of the object off (0x4DCEA0 with 1: field 0x149 or the bits of fields 0xD and 0xE, and the four entries 0x14A or 0x10 and 0x11). It then writes the word with bit 0x2 set. | Original |
| OT3 | clear wrapper 0x4EFEE0 and setter 0x43D280, mask 2 | The same wrapper shape (record sub-kind 2). The setter marks the object's lights on again (0x4DCF60 with 1, the inverse of 0x4DCEA0), writes the word with bit 0x2 cleared and clears the update bits 0x7000000 of field 0x147 (a redraw request). Its last step, when the bit was cleared and 0x5E2F58 is 0, calls 0x43F710 on the object (it acts only when the flags & 3 are 0 and field 0x14F is 3, then 0x433580 or 0x423FF0 by field 0x45 bit 0 and the tile); that step is not read further, so what a switch-on does beyond the flag, the lights and the redraw is F0. | Original |
| OT4 | what the flag does to the object | OF_OFF takes the object out of the sector tile lists' queries (the play mask 3, destroyed or off; RL9, PV2): it is not drawn, blocks no step and is listed by no vicinity scan, and its lights are off. The object keeps its record, so a second toggle brings it back. A destroyed object is gone for good; this is the difference. | Original |
| OT5 | what Steamwoven builds | The use set (`AreaUseScriptHost.Admission`) admits action 56 (`ScrCatalogue.Action.ToggleState` of S16), and the placed, dying and combat-point sets, built on it, and the heartbeat set (which held it already) too. The answer is the heartbeat host's, shared by every run (`ObjectStateRefusal`, `OffProperty`): an NPC of the shown map is switched off or on (the session's object-flag change over the map data for an NPC the data stores off, else the map-change key `obj.off`), and the NPC's other readers (the AI gate, the vicinity lists) follow the existing NPC off state; the post-step of OT3 is not modelled. The PC and any other object type (portal, container, an object of another map) refuse the run whole at plan time, "toggle state of X is not modelled", so nothing applies; a scenery of the shown map is applied since ARC-SCRIPT-2324 (OT8), an item of an item event's move by the item set (IV9). | Steamwoven structure |
| OT6 | script 2221 | Its last loop (lines 15 to 20) takes every portal in the vicinity of its attachee (focus kind 19), skips the ones that are not named 808 (condition 20, line 16) or that are open (condition 36, line 17), toggles the looped portal (line 18) and plays sound 8065 (line 19); before it a loop over the scenery around the attachee (lines 4 to 8, focus kind 15) stops and plays script eye candy 15 on one named 1642, the shape of the other ground rune scripts. A tile or sector script has no attachee (A2, SA18), so under a tile entry the list is empty (PV1) and line 18 is never reached; the toggle matters only to a run with an attachee. Admission is whole now (the opt-in check counts 22 of 22 in the use, placed and dying sets); a run that does reach a portal is refused by OT5. | Data |
| OT7 | what is not modelled | The off flag of a portal or container (OT4: not drawn, no block, not listed, lights off) and of the PC, and the lights of a switched-off portal or container (OT2: the object's lights end; none is built). A scenery of the shown map is modelled by OT8 and its lights by OT10; the survey that settled this is OT9. | Limit |
| OT8 | what Steamwoven builds for a scenery (ARC-SCRIPT-2324) | A toggle state of a scenery of the shown map applies. The object's off flag is the map data's bit under a record of the toggle (`SessionItemOff.Key`, the same `obj.off` field an item's and an NPC's toggle write: 1 is off, 0 is on over a data that stores it off, no record when the flag is back at the data's state), read by `GameSessionOwner.QueryObjectOff`. `AreaObjectTargets.Gone` names a scenery whose flag is set (so the view does not draw it, it blocks no step, the vicinity lists leave it out and it is no pick or attack target, OT4), and `IsSwitchedOff` of the use host reads the same state. A scenery the map data stores off is therefore gone from the start (16 sceneries of the 79 maps' data carry the bit; before, the view drew them; the 3 containers that carry it are still drawn, as only a scenery is read here), until a script's toggle of the object itself brings it on. The lights of the object follow since ARC-SCRIPT-2585 (OT10). Not modelled: the post-step of the switch-on (OT3). | Steamwoven structure |
| OT9 | which installed scripts toggle a non-NPC object (opt-in census `ToggleStateCensusRealDataTests`, `DAT_ROOT`; attachments from the research probe `--script-attachments`) | 209 installed scripts run toggle state; 198 of them toggle their attachee, which is an NPC for every placed row on the maps (the item rows are the item set's, IV9). The other eleven name the looped object (kind 10) or the extra object (kind 12): the NPC scripts 1179 (heartbeat of one NPC, Arronax's interior) and 2407 (first heartbeat of nine NPCs of the main map) loop over the sceneries around the NPC and toggle each; 1239 (an item's insert-item script) does so in an item event ([#2420](https://github.com/Simarglok/arcanum-steamwoven/issues/2420)); 2221 (OT6; portals) and 2475 to 2478 (sceneries) are tile scripts, which have no attachee, so their loops are empty; 2088 has no attachment in the module data; 1198 (an item's use point) and 1734 (two sceneries of the main map outside the census windows, points 2 and 30) toggle the extra object. No installed script runs a toggle of a portal or container from an attachee, and none toggles the PC. | Data |
| OT10 | lights of a toggled scenery (ARC-SCRIPT-2585; opt-in survey `SceneryToggleLightCensusRealDataTests`, `DAT_ROOT`) | The original's setters end and restore the object's light with its flag: 0x43D0E0 (switch off) ends the lit flag light and marks every light field off (0x4DCEA0), 0x43D280 (clear) marks them on again (0x4DCF60) (OT2). Steamwoven: `AreaObjectTargets.SceneryOffStates` lists the sceneries of the shown map with a record of a script's toggle (`GameSessionOwner.QueryObjectOffRecord`: true off, false on over a data that stores it off; a scenery no script toggled is not listed, so its lights stand as the map data loaded them), and `AreaLocalLighting.WithObjectsOff` (`AreaStationaryLightSource.WithObjectsOff`) sets or clears the OFF bit of the light fields (primary and overlay slots) of exactly those owners, in the night and the day form; the viewer applies it in `LightsNow` and gives the canvas the lighting again when the states change. Survey of the installed data: of the 49,917 sceneries of the 79 maps 6,645 carry a light; of the sceneries the scripts 1179 and 2407 toggle (named 1609 and 1668, OT9) one is in the maps, the one named 1609 in Arronax's interior (script 1179), with one light; none is named 1668 in the 79 maps (the main-map windows hold them, not read here). Not modelled: a destroyed scenery's lights (the object is deleted in the original), a portal's or container's flag (OT7), and the night rebuild of a nocturnal scenery's light (the original's load switches the object back on, LT4; here a recorded toggle stays in force over the loaded form). The smoke stage `map` toggles a lit scenery of the Crash Site map through the session and reads the lighting the canvas gets (off, other owners unchanged, restored). | Steamwoven structure |

## The container vicinity focus kinds (ARC-SCRIPT-2322, every and any container in the vicinity)

Source: issue #2322 (found in ARC-RES-2262). Read on 2026-11-15 by a read-only exe session with the exports in `research-private/ghidra-export/c` (the object-list builder 0x44B4E0 and its free routine 0x44B8F0, the vicinity scan 0x440FC0 with its window builder 0x440FF0 and filter 0x440B40, the mobile-object test 0x43D940, the first-object function 0x44B960, the condition evaluator 0x4451B0, the action dispatcher 0x447220).

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| CV1 | focus kinds 17 and 18 in the list builder (0x44B4E0) | One switch case of its own serves both: the vicinity scan with the type mask **4** (object type 2, the container; the filter maps mask bit n to object type n, so the portals' mask is 2 for type 1, the scenery's 8 for type 3, the critters' 0x18000, the items' 0x7FE0), the centre being the run's attachee (the same field as kinds 1, 15 and 19). No attachee gives an empty list (the head and count are cleared and the case returns 0: a loop for jumps past its loop end, a condition is false). | Original |
| CV2 | the scan and its filter (0x440FC0, 0x440B40) | The same window-sized rectangle centred on the centre object's location as for the portals (PV2, PG13). The filter differs from the portals' branch: a portal, scenery, wall or trap mask walks the sector tile lists of static objects, but a container (like an item or a critter) is a mobile object, so the filter walks each sector's mobile-object list (iterator 0x4E3B30, 0x4E3BA0). An object must be of a mobile type (0x43D940: 2, 4, 5 to 14, 15, 16), must not carry the in-an-inventory flag 0x1000, must pass the play mask (destroyed or switched off drop out) and must have its type bit in the mask, and its own location is tested against the rectangle (64-bit bounds). No container state (locked, open, contents) is read. | Original |
| CV3 | the cap and the list order | The copy loop is the portals' (PV3): entries in scan order (sectors of the rectangle, then each sector's mobile-object list order), stopping at 100 and dropping the rest silently (PG3). The list is built once per call; a loop for stores it in the run. | Original |
| CV4 | 17 against 18 | One list, one case. The difference is only in the condition evaluator (0x4451B0, the tail shared by the list conditions, SG6): it counts the list objects that pass the test; zero is false for "every" too; otherwise it is true when all pass or the kind is one of the "any" kinds (3, 5, 7, 9, 14, 16, 18, 20; 23 by a second test). List actions (0x447220: the loop for and loop end of PV4, toggle state OT1, and the single-object operands through 0x44B960 and 0x44BA70) never tell the two apart; an empty list leaves the first-object read on stack garbage as for the portals (PV4, not copied). | Original |
| CV5 | what Steamwoven builds | The set **S62** is S61 plus the focus kinds 17 (`ScrFocusKind.EveryContainerInVicinity`) and 18 (`AnyContainerInVicinity`); the interpreter resolves both for a loop for, a first object and a condition's list (the SG6 test, "any" by kind 18) through the new `IScrContainerQueries.ContainersInVicinity`, under the same cap as the scenery and the portals (`VicinityListCapped`, Original profile). `AreaUseScriptHost` lists the shown map's container rows (type 2) within `ScrWorld.VicinityReach` tiles of the centre on both axes that are not gone, in snapshot order, and its use, placed, dying, combat-point and heartbeat sets admit both kinds (the heartbeat set: two point 19 sites). The interpreter runs at most S62 (was S61; ARC-SCRIPT-2319 made S61). | Steamwoven structure |
| CV6 | what the installed scripts do now (the census, `ARC-RES-2262`, set S62, `DAT_ROOT`) | Under S62 the eight roots that named kind 17 are admitted whole: 1,759 of the 1,809 attached roots on 7,487 of 7,766 sites (S61, the group set of ARC-SCRIPT-2319: 1,751 and 7,477; S60: 1,740 and 7,416). By point the refused kind-17 sites go from 19 (2), 21 (5) and 3 tile or sector to point 21 (1). The point 21 (insert item) sites still run under the session's item set (`SessionItemScripts.Admission`, S14-item), which admits 3 of the 28 point 21 attachments for other reasons (toggle state 56 in 16 rows, action 18 in 5): the item host has no world query and no loop for, and answers no vicinity, so those sites waited for [#2335](https://github.com/Simarglok/arcanum-steamwoven/issues/2335), which is [IV1–IV8](#the-item-sets-world-commands-arc-script-2335-point-21-insert-item-container-vicinity-name-and-change-art-number) | Data |
| CV7 | what is not modelled | The list order is the snapshot order, not the original's sector scan order (as PV7); the in-an-inventory flag 0x1000 needs no test (the host lists map rows, never a carried object); containers of other loaded maps, and a container a script created or the AI host moved, are not listed; the item host (point 21, CV6) does not answer the kinds. | Limit |

## The any-scenery focus kind (ARC-SCRIPT-2321, the tile script of the k'na tha map and the bust script 1734)

Source: issue #2321 (found in ARC-RES-2262, [census](../script-refusal-census.md#the-refused-focus-kinds-arc-res-2262-2026-11-15)). Read on 2026-11-15 by a read-only exe session with the exports in `research-private/ghidra-export/c` (the object-list builder 0x44B4E0, its list release 0x44B8F0, the first-object function 0x44B960, the condition evaluator 0x4451B0 and the action executor 0x447220, case 0x12 loop for).

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| AS1 | focus kinds 15 and 16 in the list builder (0x44B4E0) and its release (0x44B8F0) | One switch case serves both, as the pairs 17/18, 19/20, 8/9 and 22/23 each share one (PV1): the centre is the run's attachee (the object handle at run offset 0x10, none gives an empty list), the scan 0x440FC0 with type mask 0x8 (scenery), at most 100 entries copied in scan order. Nothing in the builder or its release tells 16 from 15. | Original |
| AS2 | first object of an operand (0x44B960) and loop for (0x447220 case 0x12) | The first-object function has no per-kind code: kind 16 gives the first element of the same list as 15 (stack garbage for an empty list, PV4). Loop for builds the list with the builder directly, keeps the kind in the run only to release it, and loops the same up to 100 objects for 15 and 16; an empty list jumps past the loop end. | Original |
| AS3 | the list conditions (0x4451B0, the shared tail after the count loop) | The only place the two kinds differ: the count of objects that pass must be above zero, then the test holds when all of them passed, or when the focus kind is one of 3, 5, 7, 9, 14, 16, 18, 20 or 23 (SG6). So kind 16 is the "any" kind (one object is enough), 15 the "every" kind; an empty list is false for both. The admission already reads 16 as an "any" kind in `ListHolds` (the interpreter's `ScrFocusKind.AnySceneryInVicinity` arm of SG6). | Original |
| AS4 | what Steamwoven builds | The set **S64** is S63 (S62 plus get armor and the group kinds in the list conditions, AR) plus the focus kind `ScrFocusKind.AnySceneryInVicinity` (16); the interpreter runs at most S64. The interpreter resolves 16 as 15 for an object operand's list, for a first-object read (PC variable and flag operands included) and for a loop for, through the same `IScrWorldQueries.SceneryInVicinity` under the same cap (`VicinityListCapped`, Original profile); the "any" reading of a condition was already in `ListHolds`. `ScrAdmissionSet.WorldQueryFocusKinds` holds 16, so a host that admits it must answer the world queries. The use, placed, dying and combat-point sets and the heartbeat set (the taking-damage, heartbeat and other object points; the bust point since ARC-SCRIPT-2338 has its own set, which inherits the kind from it) admit 16 wherever they admit 15 (`AreaUseScriptHost`). | Steamwoven structure |
| AS5 | what the installed scripts do now (opt-in checks, `DAT_ROOT`) | The two roots that named kind 16 are no longer refused for it: **2561**, the tile script of 20 sites on the k'na tha map (tile entries), is admitted whole by the placed set and its 20 tile sites run (`AreaUseScriptsOriginalTests`: 1,788 tile runs without a teleport and 910 refused, from 1,768 and 930); **1734** (the destroy point 2 and the bust point 30 of the main map, 2 sites) is admitted whole by the heartbeat and dying sets with the commands of S64 and the focus kind, and the heartbeat set as built still refuses it at line 0 for set PC quest state (16), not for the focus kind; the bust host's own set admits it whole since ARC-SCRIPT-2338 ([BS1](#the-bust-scripts-story-effects-arc-script-2338)). | Data |
| AS6 | what is not modelled | The list order is the snapshot order, not the original's scan order (PG3, PV7), so which object a first-object read takes and the order a loop visits them can differ. A scenery's destroy point (2) runs when a script destroys the scenery (BS3, ARC-SCRIPT-2501), so script 1734's destroy site runs too; a scenery destroyed any other way (a break, a spell) does not run it. | Limit |

## The bust script's story effects (ARC-SCRIPT-2338)

Source: issue #2338 (found in ARC-SCRIPT-2321). Read in the repository on 2026-11-16 from the installed script 1734 (12 lines) with `DAT_ROOT` (the opt-in check `AreaScriptEyeCandyTests`); the original's script executor (0x447220) is read in AS1 to AS6 and OD15 (combat ledger) and has no per-point restriction. No new exe read.

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| BS1 | bust dispatch (0x43F1C0 → 0x441980, combat OD15) and the host's set | The BUST script runs under the same executor as every other point, so its commands are the use script's commands. Script 1734 (main map, the scenery that stops the script eye candy of its neighbours) uses two commands the heartbeat set lacked: **set PC quest state (16)** at line 0 and **play script eye candy (120)** at line 8; the rest is in the heartbeat set. Steamwoven's bust host (`AreaUseScriptHost.RunBust`) now runs under `BustAdmission`: the heartbeat set plus the story actions of S13 (16, 49, 50, 72, 73) and 120. The set stays apart from the heartbeat set because a heartbeat runs every few seconds for every NPC near the PC, and a break runs once. The run's effects apply through the same code as a use click (`AreaScriptStory.Apply` for the quest, rumor and reputation requests, `TakeEyeCandies` for the viewer); a bust with no attacker has no PC triggerer, so a quest request that names the triggerer asks for nobody. | Original (built) |
| BS2 | destroy point 2 of a scenery (0x43CCA0, TD2) | The destroy (TD2) runs the object's point 2 script, and so does every destroy branch of the break (0x43F1C0 calls 0x43CCA0 for a door, a container after its drop, a trap with no destroyed ART and a busted scenery broken again, 0x4F06E0 for a door; ARC-SCRIPT-2644 corrects the first reading that "the break (OD15) does not"); only the BUST dispatch (OD15) is a different script point. Until ARC-SCRIPT-2501 no host destroyed a scenery: a script's destroy (68) of any object but an NPC of the shown map refused the run whole ("not modelled (only an NPC of the shown map)"), so the destroy point of script 1734's scenery was never run. Superseded by [BS3](#the-destroy-of-a-scenery-arc-script-2501) for a scenery. | Superseded |

## The destroy of a scenery (ARC-SCRIPT-2501)

Source: issue #2501 (found in #2338). Exe read 2026-11-17 (an Opus read-only session over `research-private/ghidra-export`: the object destroy 0x43CCA0 and its callees 0x423FF0, 0x459740, 0x4601D0, 0x443770, 0x43DFD0, the script dispatcher 0x441980, the break 0x43F1C0) and a numbers-only census of the installed scripts (`DAT_ROOT`, throwaway probe over the content stack: 60 of 2,399 scripts hold destroy (68); the sceneries that carry a destroy point script).

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| BS3 | destroy of a scenery (0x43CCA0, scenery = type 3) | A scenery takes no type branch (no 0x4639E0, no wall list). Order: network play goes to 0x43CEA0 (not read); object flag 0x1 set leaves it as it is; the destroy point (2) runs (0x441980, the object as triggerer and attachee, no extra object, line 0; no script at the point answers 1) and an answer 0 returns at once (the object stays); the screen rectangle of the object is marked for redraw (0x43DFD0 mask 7); the four common calls (0x423FF0 ends every goal run of the object, 0x459740 ends the spell runs that aim at it or cast from it, 0x4601D0 the interface hook, 0x443770 frees its lights, shadows, render handle and cache); last the object flags take 0x1 (0x406D40). It stays a live object at its tile: the draw, pick and tile and rectangle lists skip an object whose flag 0x1 is set (through the global mask 0x5E2F88). Flag 0x1 is set last, so a destroy script that destroys its own object runs the point again (re-entry). A scenery already busted that is hit again goes to this destroy without the BUST point (OD15). | Original (clean-up callees F0) |
| BS3a | what the installed data does (census, numbers only) | 60 scripts hold destroy (68). Only four can destroy a scenery: 2092 (a container's destroy and bust points, 1 main-map site: loops over every scenery around it and destroys those that pass condition 20), 2827 (a container's taking-damage point, 1 site: same loop, then attacks), 1483 (a dialog, the destroy line is reached only by a resume line) and probably 2545 (an item's use script: the extra object). 19 sceneries carry a destroy point script: 1734 (1 main-map site), 1194 (1, main), 2403 (1, Ashbury underground), 2560 (16, k'na tha); none of them holds destroy, so no installed script destroys its own scenery, and no prototype of type 3 carries a script. Script 1734 itself holds no destroy (it loops over the sceneries around it, toggles open or closed and plays a script eye candy). | Data |
| BS3b | what Steamwoven builds | `AreaUseScriptHost.DestroyScenery` applies a script's destroy of a scenery of the shown map: an object already destroyed (map data flag 0x1, or the session's destroyed condition) is left as it is; otherwise the destroy point runs first (`RunDestroyPoint`, under `DestroyPointAdmission` = the bust set plus the dying set) and an answer of skip default (`DyingStops`, which also covers a script that runs past its last line) keeps the object; else the session records the object condition Destroyed (`object.condition`, no schema step; for a container through `DestroyMapContainer`, DD9, which also drops its invulnerable items), which the view already reads as gone (`AreaObjectTargets.Gone`: not drawn, not picked, the vicinity lists skip it through the world's gone answer, no longer blocks a step). The plan checks before anything of the run applies that the destroy point script is readable and admitted whole by the destroy-point set, else the run is refused whole with the reason. The bust set `BustAdmission` also holds destroy (68), so 2092 is admitted whole at its bust point. | Built |
| BS3c | what is not modelled | ARC-SCRIPT-2514: the viewer ends the goal runs and tells the spell runs of an object a script's destroy applied (`AreaScriptWorld.ObjectDestroyed` → `AreaSceneryHost.ObjectDestroyed`, which kills every run of the object with the session's kill, no rule and no level, as 0x43CCA0 → 0x423FF0 does, gives up its loop and lists its looping voice for the viewer to stop; the cast host's `ObjectGone` is 0x459740), and a host built for a window or map shown again starts no loop for a scenery the session holds destroyed; ARC-SCRIPT-2620: the break paths end them too (a hero's attack, a spell, a burn, a script's or a trap's damage that destroys a scenery, trap or container: the session names the step `SessionObjectHostSteps.ObjectDestroyed`, the viewer's break steps run `EndDestroyedObjectRuns` after the trap and before the sound, as 0x43F1C0 → 0x43CCA0 → 0x423FF0, 0x459740 do; a door or window is left to the door host, an item is deleted, not destroyed, and a first break that only busts a scenery ends nothing; ARC-SCRIPT-2646: the break's own interrupt of the object's goal runs, OD7: every break of a container, scenery or trap starts with the level-5 interrupt (`SessionObjectHostSteps.ObjectBroken`, run by the viewer after the trap and before the BUST script and before the destroy's kill): a scenery's loop (goal 1 or 77, priority 1) ends with its end node, so its looping voice stops, the eye-candy and hover overlays (no-interrupt goals) stay, and the loop starts again when the object is switched on (`AreaSceneryHost.RestartBroken`, the respawn's 0x43F5F0 → 0x43D280 → 0x43F710) or the sector is shown again; ARC-SCRIPT-2670: the break then writes the busted ART through the art writer 0x43EB30, whose restart 0x43F710 decides at once: a busted ART of several frames (or one frame with a kind-2 sound, not modelled) starts the loop again on the busted frames (`AreaSceneryHost.Reframe`, the viewer's art scan, the busted art wins over a script's art; the destroyed art stays read by `SyncSceneryArt` at a sector or map show too), a one-frame art kills every run of the object, the no-interrupt overlays included (0x423FF0); a busted scenery the next hit destroys gives up its art and restarts nothing; the census over the installed maps (`AreaExtraArtOriginalTests`, DAT_ROOT, core objects of the whole maps) finds 4 sceneries that bust, all with a one-frame busted art, one of them animated before the break, so the kill is the case that occurs and the restart on several frames has no installed object, a door or window's runs are interrupted the same way (ARC-SCRIPT-2669: `ObjectBroken` is named for a portal too, and the viewer's break steps hand the door to the door host, `AreaDoorHost.ObjectBroken`: goals 70/71/72, priority 3, not no-interrupt, end at level 5 in slot order, a closing cut short snaps the door shut by its end node 0x42B6F0 as any interrupt of it does unless the door is held, an opening or a pending close leaves it where it stands; the attempt goals 12 and 50 are the opener's no-interrupt runs, not the door's; the interrupt runs after the trap and before the BUST script; one recorded difference: the session has written the break's damage and condition before the host steps run, so a door a break destroyed is snapped closed after the destroy, not before it), and the walk's stop at a run that refuses (a priority-5 goal that is not no-interrupt) is the table's rule, rare for a scenery; a refusal of the kill or the interrupt for a pass in progress is tried again, also with no scenery host); not ended: the sound and lights clean-up beyond the view's hiding; a scenery, trap, container or door destroyed by a break runs its destroy point first and a skip default keeps it (ARC-SCRIPT-2644: the host's gate on the session, `AttachObjectDestroyGate`, `AreaUseScriptHost.BreakMayDestroy`, run before the break's own writes, so the point sees the object as it was before the hit's write, a recorded difference from the original, which writes the damage and the drop first; a refused break still writes the hit's damage, spills a container's items (a kept container is no spilled one: its seed items lie loose through the dropped-item change) and plays the sound or the spill eye candy, and ends no runs); a destroy point that destroys its own object is left to the running destroy (no second run of the point, the original re-enters); a destroy of a portal, trap or item (other than the one in use) and of the PC is still refused whole (a container's, ARC-WORLD-2502, is applied: DD9); script 2827 (taking-damage point) is admitted since ARC-SCRIPT-2513 (BS3d) and 2545 (item use) is not admitted by its host for other commands and waits for its own slice; a container's destroy runs the inventory rule (ARC-WORLD-2502, DD9). The destroy point's nested run applies its effects through the same host (the rule mode of the run that destroyed it); a point run that is not applied (a nested refusal the plan cannot see, such as a teleport of the scenery) leaves the object and fails the outer run, and the nested run's floating lines and eye candies are queued before the outer run's own and its dialog or teleport effects are not carried out. The 16 k'na tha sites of script 2560 stay refused (it stops on focus kind 19), so a destroy of those sceneries still refuses the run whole. A respawning scenery that is switched off (condition Off) is overwritten by Destroyed, which cancels its pending respawn. A scenery the session holds destroyed (a break, an earlier destroy) now counts as already destroyed for every script action on it (toggle state, change art, damage): they leave it as it is (TD2 for destroy; no object to act on). | Limit |

Pinned by `Script2545ExtraObjectCensusRealDataTests`. | Data |
| BS3d | taking-damage point 23 and the container script 2827 (ARC-SCRIPT-2513, #2513) | The taking-damage run (`RunTakingDamage`, GT198-GT203) ran under the heartbeat set, which refuses 2827 at play script eye candy (120) and destroy (68). It runs now under `TakingDamageAdmission` = the heartbeat set plus those two commands (the original's executor 0x447220 has no per-point restriction, BS1); census over the installed data (`DAT_ROOT`): 2827 is refused by the heartbeat set and admitted whole by the taking-damage set (its everyone-in-vicinity attack loop and the scenery loop are focus kinds the heartbeat set already holds). A destroy of a scenery goes through `DestroyScenery` (BS3b), the eye candy through the run's queue as at the bust point. A destroy of a container, portal or item stays refused whole (#2493), an NPC of the shown map is destroyed as at the bust point; the goal and spell runs of a script-destroyed scenery end at the destroy (ARC-SCRIPT-2514, BS3c). Tests: `A_taking_damage_script_may_play_an_eye_candy_and_destroy_a_scenery_and_the_run_applies`, `The_container_taking_damage_script_2827_is_admitted_whole_by_the_taking_damage_set`. | Built (admission and host; the run over the real map is not smoke-tested) |

Steamwoven shows what the break's point does: its printed lines go to the message window (IS2, PW1) and its dialog action is queued as a heartbeat's is; a refused destroy is named by `SessionObjectHostSteps.DestroyKept`. ARC-SCRIPT-2723: the ordinary trap script's and the BUST script's runs of a break are taken the same way (awards, floats, creations, printed lines, dialog action); before, only one of the two (`ran.Bust ?? ran.TrapScript`) handed over its awards and floats, and neither its lines or its dialog. The BUST set holds no print line command (24), so a BUST script that prints is refused whole. | Data |
| BS3g | the item case of the break (0x43F1C0 label 0x43F44A) and the order of the trap (ARC-SCRIPT-2722, read 2026-11-18 by an Opus reader from the exports, F1; census `DestroyPointCensusRealDataTests`) | An item that is hit again after its first break (the art already carries the destroyed bit) is deleted by a branch that makes only these calls in this order: the object destroy 0x43CCA0, the sound (0x4F1050 with 1), the sound 0x41B930 when sound is on, then the break clears bits 0x0B000000 of field 0x147. It writes nothing before the destroy (the hit points were written by the caller 0x4B4390) and dispatches no BUST script (OD15). Inside 0x43CCA0 the destroy point (2) runs first with the item as triggerer and attachee; an answer 0 returns at once, so the item stays in its holder with its art bit and its zero hit points, the break still plays the sound and does not test the answer, and (the reader's inference) the next hit that reaches 0 hit points breaks it again: trap test, destroy point and sound run again. After a point that lets it go, an item in an inventory leaves it through the remove path (wield-off point 25 if worn, then remove-item point 28 with the holder as triggerer) and is placed on the holder's tile before the common calls and flag 0x1. The caller 0x4B4390 orders the steps for every non-critter target at 0 hit points, items included: trap test 0x4BBDD0, trap trigger 0x4BCB00, the break 0x43F1C0, the witnesses 0x4AEE50; so the trap fires **before** the destroy point of a door, container, trap, busted scenery or item. The critical hits on armour (0x100000) and weapon (0x4000) break an item through 0x43F1C0 without the trap test, and the critical "destroy weapon" calls 0x43CCA0 on the wielded weapon with no break.
