Steamwoven: an item's deleting break after a hit (the hero's attack on an item, damage with no attacker) asks the item gate (`AttachItemDestroyGate`, `AreaUseScriptHost.ItemMayDestroy`) before the deletion is written, shows the point's lines like a break's (ARC-SCRIPT-2686) and keeps the item on a skip default (`ItemDurabilityResult.DestroyKept`, host step `DestroyKept`, the sound stays); the hit's damage is written as the original's caller writes it before the break (0 hit points, still broken), so the next hit of any size asks again. The item must be a row of the shown map or an item the hero carried here from another map (ARC-SCRIPT-2749: the origin map's row stands for the gate's length); an item worn by a critter or made or kit item has no row the host reads and is deleted without a point (open). ARC-SCRIPT-2749 keeps every run in order (a queue, not one slot), so two deleting steps of one pass show both runs' lines. The trap-before-point order is not built: the census finds 17 destroy-point rows on the 79 maps and none has a trap use script, so the order cannot show with the installed data. ARC-SCRIPT-2749 runs the point for the wear paths too (`ApplyAttackWear`, `ApplyArmorWear`: an armour or weapon whose wear deletes it, the destroy-weapon critical; a kept item is a `DestroyKept` step) and the frame pump shows its lines. Per caller: with the installed rows (weapon 1275, armour 1256 reached by wear; generic 2519, which always skips the default) only the wear paths and the generic item's callers matter; open: the point is not run for the other callers of 0x43CCA0 on items (use-up 0x462CC0, ammunition and gold merge, decay, junk pile dissolve, inventory clear, schematic assembly, projectile end), which no step of ours lets a skip default stop (a refused destroy would need each caller's own keep-branch read); the item scripts 1275 (item type 5, one row) and 1256 (type 7, two) print a message and answer, and 2519 (type 14, one row) always skips the default, so that item cannot be destroyed by a break. | Original (built for the break; F1 for the order and the item branch) |

## The item set's world commands (ARC-SCRIPT-2335, point 21 insert item: container vicinity, name and change art number)

Source: issue #2335 (found in ARC-SCRIPT-2322). Read on 2026-11-15 by a read-only exe session with the exports in `research-private/ghidra-export/c` (the insertion 0x466640 and its point-21 callers 0x46687A and 0x46697F, the script runner 0x441980, the list builder 0x44B4E0, the vicinity scan 0x440FC0, the action dispatcher 0x447220, the toggle setters 0x4EFF50 and 0x4EFEE0) and a throwaway census over the installed data (74 placed point 21 rows on 37 root scripts, 12 prototype-default rows).

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| IV1 | who runs (0x466640, 0x441980) | The merge path (0x46687A) and the main path (0x46697F) of the insertion both call the runner with point 0x15: the attachee is the **item** (its script slot is read from the item), the triggerer is the **holder** the item enters (the hero for a pickup), no third object. The gold and ammunition callers (0x4648C2, 0x465977) are other paths. | Original |
| IV2 | the vicinity centre (0x44B4E0 kinds 17 and 18, 0x440FC0) | The centre is the attachee's own location field, never the holder's. The insertion does not write it: it writes the item's slot, parent, the holder's list and count, the weights (0x45F820), the in-an-inventory flag and parent (0x4417A0) and the art overlay (0x4CBAA0), so a held item keeps the **stale** location it had (RL10: only a drop, an animation parameter and a cross-map teleport rewrite it). In the installed data 62 held rows have a location that differs from their holder's in 49 and equals it in 13; the 12 ground rows have the tile they lie on. A run-time item's location is what its creation wrote (not read). | Original |
| IV3 | toggle state on a list (0x447220, case 0x38; OT1–OT4) | Flips the off flag (0x2) of each object of the list, whatever its type; on a container it hides it (no draw, no block, not listed, lights off), it does not open or close it and does not change its art. In the installed point 21 rows toggle state never runs on a container: 25 rows toggle the attachee itself and one (script 1239) the looped scenery. | Original |
| IV4 | what Steamwoven builds (session) | The item set (`SessionItemScripts.Admission`, S14-item, a subset of the widest set: **no new numbered set**, the sets S61 to S64 belong to #2319, #2322, #2326 and #2321) adds loop for, loop end and loop break (18 to 20), the focus kinds looped object (10) and every and any container in the vicinity (17, 18), the name condition (20) and change art number (59). `ISessionItemWorldHost` (a new, optional part of the item script host) answers the vicinity of the item, a name, the mind-controlled test and the art plan; the run's host adapter implements the world and object question interfaces and forwards them, and a host without that part, an unknown centre or an unknown object makes the run unanswerable: the session refuses the move (`ItemScriptUnsupported`), as it refused these scripts before. The scenery, critter, random, location, quest, held-item, wielded-item, reputation and object-type questions are not admitted and throw if asked. | Steamwoven structure |
| IV5 | what Steamwoven builds (art) | A change art number is **planned** by the host (`SessionObjectArtPlan`: the art id against its baseline, and for a container the lock identity and baseline of the closed-frame reset, as the use host's art plan, ARC-SCRIPT-1735/1778) while the run is made and **written by the move's own commit, without a revision of its own**: the art id (`obj.art`) and, for a container whose art id is written (also when it stays: the plan is the use host's, which resets the frame whenever the art file exists), the closed frame 0. The plan refuses (the move too) an object the host reads no row for, an art class it does not model or a missing art index; one change of an object a move (a second refuses the move: not modelled). The move checks the map-change budget before it applies anything. `AreaItemScriptHost` takes the shown map's use-script host from the viewer (`AreaInventoryHost.ShownScripts`) and answers from it: `ContainersNearItem` (the shown map's containers within `ScrWorld.VicinityReach` tiles of the item's tile, not gone, in snapshot order, under the cap), `NameOf`, `IsMindControlled`, `PlanObjectArt`. | Steamwoven structure |
| IV6 | what the installed point 21 scripts are | 74 placed rows: 12 on the ground, 47 in NPC lists, 15 in map containers; 48 on the main map, 16 in the Tulla interior. Admitted before: 1300, 1341, 1351, 1443, 1691, 2187, 3051 (and 30017 by prototype). Toggle state on the attachee alone: 2892 (2 rows), 3030 (18); with toggle item inventory display (127): 3028, 3059. Quest and object-type tails: 3056, 2722, 2734, 1027, 1029, 1021; set rumor 1103, 1566; story state 1094; rename 1573; hour and call script 1309; name tests 1721, 2760, 1494, 2084, 2109. The **display-case family** (loop for over the containers of the vicinity, name test on the looped one, change art number to a closed case): 2992, 2993, 2994 (the Eye of Kraka-tur, the Glass Key and the Finger of Mannox in the Caladon Panarrii temple, names 1205, 1203, 1201, art 12) need nothing more and run whole now; 1191 (the quest experience, 63) and 2947 (assign object 15, get object type 34) need one command more each. Other loops: 1239 (scenery 15 and the toggle on it), 2096, 2390, 2702 and 2103 (everyone in the vicinity 8, dialogs through call script in 54). | Data |
| IV7 | what the installed scripts do now (the opt-in census, `ScriptTriggerCensusTests`, `DAT_ROOT`) | Of the 28 point 21 rows on the shown maps' windows (12 scripts) the item set admits **6** (3 before): the three display-case scripts join (**22** since ARC-SCRIPT-2346, [IV12](#the-item-sets-toggle-state-arc-script-2346-the-off-flag-of-an-item)). The other 22 stop on toggle state (56) in 16 rows, get story state (42), is dead (condition 22), condition 46, quest state (condition 6), and the scenery and critter vicinity kinds (15, 8), one row each. Taking the Finger of Mannox, the Glass Key or the Eye of Kraka-tur from its case no longer refuses the pickup; the case's art becomes the closed one. | Data |
| IV8 | what is not modelled | (Toggle state of the attachee item, [#2346](https://github.com/Simarglok/arcanum-steamwoven/issues/2346), is built: [IV9–IV12](#the-item-sets-toggle-state-arc-script-2346-the-off-flag-of-an-item).) The commands of the other refused roots ([#2347](https://github.com/Simarglok/arcanum-steamwoven/issues/2347), also the item location a drop writes); a point 22 (transfer) script, whose attachee is the hero, not an item (refused when it names kinds 17 or 18); the vicinity of an item made at run time and never dropped on the ground (refused; the item the session dropped and took again is built, IV51); containers of other loaded maps and containers a script created (the host lists the shown map's rows only); the list order is the snapshot order (as PV7, CV7). | Limit |

## The item set's toggle state (ARC-SCRIPT-2346, the off flag of an item)

Source: issue #2346 (found in ARC-SCRIPT-2335). Read on 2026-11-16 by a read-only exe session with the exports in `research-private/ghidra-export/c` and the listing (the inventory window paint 0x576520, hit test 0x575fa0, item pick 0x571aa0, take all 0x579770, the insertion 0x466640 and its wrapper 0x461810, the finder 0x462480, remove 0x467860, transfer 0x461ca0, place in world 0x4415c0, throw 0x4B3C00, projectile end 0x4B2690, the ground hide mask 0x43A330/0x43AAB0, the draw and pick lists 0x43AAF0, 0x43D9F0, 0x4407C0). Level F1; the OFF flag is bit 0x2 of the object flag word (field 0x13), flipped by action 56 ([OT1–OT4](#toggle-state-in-the-use-set-arc-script-2248-the-ground-rune-tile-script-2221), [IV3](#the-item-sets-world-commands-arc-script-2335-point-21-insert-item-container-vicinity-name-and-change-art-number)).

| # | Subject | Fact | Level |
|---|---|---|---|
| IV9 | when the toggle lands (0x466640, 0x461810, 0x4417A0) | On the main path the pickup first unlinks the item from the map, then the insertion writes the holder's count and list, slot, parent, the item trigger executor, the worn-slot hook and the weight, and only then calls the insert-item script (point 21); nothing after the script reads or writes the flag word. The script's toggle is therefore the item's final state. On the merge path (the item joins an existing stack) the script runs on the incoming item, which is placed in the world at the holder's tile and destroyed afterwards, so a toggle there leaves nothing. Wield, unwield and transfer go through remove and the insertion again, so the insert-item script runs again and flips the item once more. | Original |
| IV10 | what an OFF item in an inventory does | The inventory window paint skips it in both lists (the hero's and the holder's) and its hit test returns nothing for the grid and the worn slots; the item cannot be picked into the cursor (0x571aa0) and take all (0x579770) skips it. No inventory logic reads the bit: the insert, finder, remove, transfer, weight, list snapshot, grid build, room check and free slot all ignore it, so an OFF item keeps its cells and weight and counts in the room check, stays in the holder's list, and a script finder still finds it. Nothing in the inventory code clears OFF (remove, transfer, wield, unwield, drop, place in world, a death drop); the one exception is the throw, which sets OFF on the thrown item and the projectile's end clears it. A dropped OFF item lies on the ground invisible and unclickable (in game mode the hide mask is 3: the draw, screen pick, tile and rectangle lists leave it out); an NPC's ground pickup tests that mask too. | Original |
| IV11 | what Steamwoven builds (ARC-SCRIPT-2346) | The item set (`SessionItemScripts.Admission`) admits toggle state (56). The runs plan it in the move (`ItemScriptRuns.PlanOff`): an exclusive-or over the item's off state, which is the record of the object field `obj.off` (1 off, 0 on: `SessionItemOff`, the field an NPC's toggle writes and the residency reads for items on the ground) else the data's bit that the host reads from the item's row (`ISessionItemWorldHost.ItemOffInData`; a made item reads on, no row refuses the move); the record goes when the new state is the data's. The edit is applied by the move's own commit and revision, joins the change art number edits, is saved with the objects section (no schema step: a save made before has none), follows a placed or made item (`made:n` joined the mobile objects for this field) and is dropped with a destroyed item. Two toggles in one run or one move follow each other. Only an item of the move is toggled: the hero or another object refuses the move whole, nothing applied. The viewer's inventory view (`AreaInventoryHost.View`) leaves an OFF item out of the hero's lines and grid, the loot lines and the loot grid (which keeps its cells in the arrangement), as the window paint, pick and take all do. | Steamwoven structure |
| IV12 | what the installed scripts do now, and what is not modelled | The 25 point 21 rows that toggle their attachee (scripts 2892, 3030, 3028, 3059; the 25 are counted over the installed placed rows, 16 of the 28 rows on the shown maps' windows stopped on this command) are admitted as far as their other commands are: the item set admits **22** of the 28 point 21 rows on the shown maps' windows (6 before this slice); the other 6 stop on get story state (42), is dead (condition 22), condition 46, quest state (condition 6) and the scenery and critter vicinity kinds (15, 8), one row each (those commands are [#2347](https://github.com/Simarglok/arcanum-steamwoven/issues/2347)). Not modelled: a toggle of an item the move does not carry. (The doll, the barter window, the dropped and the thrown item, the merge path, the hotkey bar and the save of the flag word are read and built or settled in [IV13–IV17](#off-flag-of-an-item-doll-barter-dropped-and-thrown-items-arc-script-2386).) | Data and limits |

## Off flag of an item: doll, barter, dropped and thrown items (ARC-SCRIPT-2386)

Source: issue #2386 (found in ARC-SCRIPT-2346). Read on 2026-11-16 by a read-only exe session (level F1) in the exports of the retail 1.0.7.4 `Arcanum.exe`: the inventory window paint 0x576520 and hit test 0x575FA0, the barter window (mode 1 of the inventory window, global 0x683464, set in 0x5727B0; the dialog's barter case 0x567E30), the throw node 0x42AB90, the throw 0x4B3C00, the projectile end 0x4B2690, the drop placement 0x466E50, place in world 0x4415C0, the hide mask 0x5E2F88 (set by 0x43AAB0) and its tests, the pickup act node 0x42A9B0, the sector and object writers, and the merge path of 0x466640. The 174 functions that read the flag word (field 0x13) were listed, so "not read" below means none of them is the function named. Behaviour in prose only.

| # | Subject | Fact | Level |
|---|---|---|---|
| IV13 | the doll | The paint 0x576520 tests `flags & 2` for every item of the hero's list and of the holder's list (field 0xEE, or 0x3C for a container) before it works out where the item sits, so a **worn** OFF item (locations 1000..1008) is not painted, on the hero's doll and on an NPC's body doll in the loot window. The hit test 0x575FA0 resolves a worn slot (0x4649C0) or a 2000..2009 slot (0x465690) and applies the same test to every case: it returns nothing. The equipment logic never reads the flag (armour class 0x43D880, item adjustments 0x465BA0, resistance 0x43D6D0, weapon damage 0x465F70, displayed rating 0x464630, weapon and attack art 0x465020, 0x4650D0, the critter art setters 0x504030..0x504180, wield and unwield 0x464AE0, 0x464C80, the wear check 0x464D20, the hooks, the item light 0x467E80): an OFF worn item still counts for armour and damage and still shapes the body art, and its worn state is its slot number. | Original |
| IV14 | barter | Barter is mode 1 of the same inventory window (the dialog's barter case opens it with mode 1), so it paints with 0x576520 and hit-tests with 0x575FA0: an OFF item of the hero's list or of the merchant's (the merchant or its substitute container, 0x45F650) is not drawn and cannot be taken into the hand (the handler 0x573850 takes the held item only from the hit test; the hover quote 0x578330 is asked on that result). There are no offer lists: each item is priced and moved when it is dropped (0x575200, 0x575180 buy; 0x575360, 0x575100 sale; 0x575770 put back; 0x575930 restore; prices 0x4C6FD0, 0x4C7010, 0x4C7090, 0x45F790) and none of them reads the flag, so an OFF item never enters a deal but stays in the merchant's stock, weight and room. The merchant restock was not traced. | Original |
| IV15 | the thrown item and the dropped one | The throw node 0x42AB90 refuses an item whose `flags & 3` is not 0 (OFF or destroyed). 0x4B3C00 (a non-PC thrower removes the item from its holder first, 0x466E00; the PC's removal was not followed) runs the item's point 6 script (0x441980): a refusal places the item in the world at the thrower's tile and puts it straight back into the thrower's inventory (0x4617F0, 0x461810, slot -1) with OFF never set; otherwise it builds the attack record (flags |= 0x40240), **sets OFF** (0x4EFF50 → 0x43D0E0, which also removes a speech bubble or floater and calls the light helper 0x4DCEA0), only then places the item in the world at the thrower's tile (0x4415C0) and launches the projectile (0x4B3170; 0x4B24F0 puts the item in field 0x50 and copies the flags into field 0x4D). During the flight the item is in the world, OFF, at the thrower's tile. The end 0x4B2690 (from the tile crossing 0x4B2870 or 0x4B2650; a network client returns first) with bit 0x40 of field 0x4D: moves the item to the projectile's location (0x4EDF20), **clears OFF** (0x4EFEE0 → 0x43D280), destroys the projectile (0x43CCA0), then resolves the hit (0x4A9650); it does not test that the item still exists. No stack split (only gold and ammunition stack, 0x462410). A drop placement (0x466E50, then 0x466EF0 or the world placement 0x4415C0 and 0x468090) **keeps** OFF: 0x4415C0 rewrites the flag word clearing only 0x1000 when the item is not destroyed, and the removal 0x466E00 does not touch the word. The hide mask is 3 in game mode (0x43AAB0) and 0 in the editor; `flags & mask` is tested by the draw (0x43AEC0, 0x43B390, 0x43AAF0), the screen pick (0x43D9F0: hover and the hero's click), the screen rectangle (0x43DFD0) and the object lists at a tile and in a rectangle (0x4407C0, 0x440B40), so a dropped OFF item is invisible and cannot be clicked; the NPC pickup node 0x42A9B0 refuses `flags & 3` itself and its source lists are those two (the starter 0x4356C0 and the move 0x42AA70 are read in [IV21](#off-flag-of-an-item-doll-barter-dropped-and-thrown-items-arc-script-2386)). | Original |
| IV16 | the leftovers of IV12 | (a) The flag word is saved and loaded like the other flag bits: none of the sector writer 0x4F1A90, the object file writers 0x4E6D10..0x4E70C0, the sector difference save 0x4D2750 and the save and evict 0x4D0BC0 reads field 0x13, the loaders test other bits (0x4F1590: 0x800000; the fold 0x4F1CD0: 0x1000), the post-load fix-up 0x410C50 clears only 0x40000000 (the generic field serializer is settled in IV20). (b) 0x570EF0 is not the hotkey bar: it paints the multiplayer player portraits (it skips a slot's player object when `flags & 2`) and is irrelevant to items; the hotkey bar list is still not identified. (c) The merge path of 0x466640 (gold and ammunition only): 0x462930 finds the holder's stack of the prototype, the incoming quantity is added to its quantity field, the incoming item is placed in the world at the **holder's location** (0x4415C0, OFF kept), 0x466D60 refreshes the inventory, the insert-item script runs at point 21 with the incoming item as attachee, then the item is destroyed (0x43CCA0); the existing stack's flag word is not touched and its finder does not read OFF, so an OFF stack still absorbs the quantity and stays OFF. | Original |
| IV17 | what Steamwoven builds (ARC-SCRIPT-2386) | The viewer's inventory view (`AreaInventoryHost.View`) leaves an OFF **worn** item out of the hero's doll and of an NPC body doll (the slot shows empty; the session's equipment still counts it: IV13). The barter lines (`AreaBarter.View`: the hero's, the merchant's, the merchant's worn ones) leave OFF items out, so such an item cannot be bought or sold (IV14); the window's art takes its doll from the inventory view. A thrown item in flight **is** off: `QueryItemOff` answers true while the item's shot is in flight, which is the throw's set (0x4B3C00) and the end of the shot (landing or given up) is the clear (0x4B2690), so no record is written for it and the flag follows the shot. A hero's throw of a weapon the session records as switched off (a script's toggle; the map data's own bit of a worn weapon is not read) is not made: the weapon stays in the hand (0x42AB90). A switched-off item lying on the ground (a session-dropped one, a thrown one in flight) is not drawn (the viewer's ground draw, and its T0 body through `AreaInventoryHost.Taken`) and not a pick or attack target (`AreaInventoryHost.Target`, `AreaObjectTargets.Target`), and the NPC ground pickup leaves it out of its lists (IV15). The ground readers first asked the session's record and the shot only; ARC-SCRIPT-2397 (IV22) adds the map data's own bit. A hero's throw of an item recorded as off is refused in both throw paths (`ThrowingHero`, `ThrowHeroItem`). The record of an item's toggle already followed the drop (the field is kept by the item's identity). The throw's refusal by the point 6 script putting the item back stands from ARC-COMBAT-1761 (`ThrowByNpc`, `ThrowHeroItem`: the item goes back to the thrower's list, off never set); the NPC throw's off refusal, the data bit on the ground and the leftovers are IV22. The merge path's incoming item is destroyed in the move, so its toggle leaves nothing (IV9) and IV16 (c) needs no code. | Steamwoven structure |
| IV18 | the PC's thrown item (ARC-SCRIPT-2397) | The type test in 0x4B3C00 reads the object type (field 0x14F: 0xF = PC, 0x10 = NPC), so its removal 0x466E00 is skipped for a PC. The PC's item has already left the inventory when it was lifted onto the cursor: the lift chain 0x573740 → 0x5788C0 → 0x578B80 → 0x578EA0 runs the remove check 0x466DA0 and then 0x466E00 → 0x467860 for a non-gold, non-ammunition item (if the hero cannot act, the press puts the item back, 0x575770); the game-view press 0x54DE50 then clears the cursor (0x573840) and starts goal 37 (0x434F80). The goal's end node 0x42B440 (descriptor 0x5A9FD0) acts when the run was not killed: for a PC thrower whose goal item slot is still set it puts the item back into the inventory (0x4664C0 finds a slot, 0x466640 inserts; with no free slot 0x466E50 drops it at the PC's location). The slot is cleared only when the throw node succeeds, so a refused throw (flags & 3, or an interrupted one) returns the item to the PC; NPCs get no such return. OFF is written only by 0x4B3C00 (0x4EFF50): the lift and 0x467860 write no flag word (0x467860 reads bit 0x1000 only). Not resolved: the item-cursor tile path 0x571AA0 → 0x571CB0 → 0x434F80 (IA9) refuses an item with flags & 2 at 0x571AA0 but no removal of the PC's item was found on it (0x571CB0, 0x4415C0, 0x4B3170 and its callee list hold none), so whether the item stays in the inventory while it flies needs the oracle. | Original |
| IV19 | merchant restock (ARC-SCRIPT-2397) | Time event 0x16 (match 0x464150, handler 0x464180 → 0x463C60) is set by 0x4640C0 with a random delay of 12 to 24 game hours (a quarter of that in multiplayer) when the inventory or barter window is set up for the barter partner (0x5727B0), and 0x463C60 sets it again while the container is on screen. The handler runs only for an object with an inventory source (0x4641A0); a PC or NPC must not be dead (0x45D8D0), an NPC must also pass 0x45DDA0; a visible container (type 2 without flag 0x400 of field 0x38) is only rescheduled, else its merchant is found through the substitute link 0x45F650 and the container is restocked only if that merchant is alive. Object creation (0x442260) calls the handler too, and the first-open and destroy paths with bit 0x200 of field 0x38 call 0x463E20 directly (0x572640, 0x463630, 0x463730, 0x463860, 0x4639E0). The regeneration 0x463E20 clears the inventory first (0x4EFAE0 → 0x463B30 keeping only items with item flag 0x2000, field 0x57: every other item is removed 0x467860, placed at the holder's location 0x4415C0 and destroyed 0x43CCA0), then adds gold (0x464830), then rolled items (0x4ED8B0 with 0x4617F0; a failed transfer destroys the item); for an NPC 0x4654F0 follows. None of 0x463B30, 0x463C60, 0x463E20, 0x4640C0, 0x461810, 0x4ED8B0, 0x464830, 0x4654F0, 0x466640, 0x466510 and 0x45F650 reads or writes field 0x13: an OFF item of a merchant is destroyed by a restock like any other unless it carries item flag 0x2000, and nothing skips OFF items when counting or removing. | Original |
| IV20 | the flag word in the saves (ARC-SCRIPT-2397) | The generic object serializer (reader 0x409AA0, writer 0x409980, walking the present fields with 0x40CBA0 and 0x40CE20; memory variants 0x409E80 and 0x409F10) passes each field to callbacks (0x40A290 read, 0x40A210 write → 0x4E44F0, 0x4E47E0; memory 0x40A250, 0x40A2D0 → 0x4E4990, 0x4E4660) that receive only the field's storage type and slot, never its number, and copy 4 raw bytes for the int32 type, so no field-specific mask is possible. The difference save writer 0x4067F0 (callback 0x40A310) writes only the fields marked modified, unmasked (it reads field 0x13 and discards the value); its reader 0x406930 (callback 0x40A370) reads field 0x14C, not 0x13; 0x409AA0 only writes field 0x14C = 1 after loading. The flag word, OFF included, is saved and loaded whole, in the full save and in the sector difference. | Original |
| IV21 | the NPC pickup and the NPC throw refusal (ARC-SCRIPT-2397) | Neither the pickup starter 0x4356C0 nor the move 0x42AA70 (also called by the network dispatcher 0x49D690, which has no OFF test) reads or writes field 0x13, nor do their callees 0x4681A0, 0x436ED0, 0x4B7CD0 and 0x461810; the only OFF test of the pickup is node 0x42A9B0 (IV15). The starter's only caller is the AI ground search 0x4A8F90 (from 0x4A8E70 and 0x4AC380), whose ground list 0x4AE4E0 → 0x440B40 skips flag 0x1000 and the hide mask (3 in game mode), so a loose OFF item is never handed over; a pile's item (description 0xBCF) is tested explicitly for flags & 2, with item flags 0x80010, worth 0x461F60 and 0x466510. Goal 37 (node table 0x5A9FF0): node 7 is the throw node 0x42AB90; false goes to node 8 (0x42BD40, animation art), node 9 (0x433270, 2 action points unless the local PC) and the end (0x90000000), then end node 0x42B440 (nothing returned for an NPC): the goal answers 0, a failure, with no retry and no other AI action from it. A refusal for flags & 3 therefore ends the goal. The grenade path (0x4AC910) takes the item from 0x462760 and starts through 0x434F80 with no OFF test; as 0x4B3C00 never runs, nothing was removed and the OFF item stays in the NPC's list. The attack starter 0x434B00 (a "default throws" weapon, weapon flag 0x200, in the weapon slot) removes the weapon with 0x466E00 before 0x434F80 and tests no OFF, so a refusal at node 7 leaves the item in no list and not in the world (inferred, not run on the oracle; on success 0x4B3C00 calls 0x466E00 again on the holderless item, which only logs a warning). Who calls 0x434B00 for the PC was not traced. | Original |
| IV22 | what Steamwoven builds (ARC-SCRIPT-2397) | `SessionItemFacts.OffInData` carries the map data's own off bit (object flag 0x2 of field 0x13) of a seeded item (`AreaInventoryMap`); `QueryItemOff` flips it by a script's toggle record as before. The ground readers ask it with that bit: the viewer's ground draw and its T0 body (`AreaInventoryHost.Taken`), the pick (`AreaInventoryHost.Target`), the attack target (`AreaObjectTargets.Target`) and the NPC ground pickup lists (`AreaAiHostGround`), so an item the data stores off that reaches the ground (a spill, an NPC's drop, a map's own loose item) lies invisible and unpickable, as the flag word is kept through every move (IV15, IV20). An NPC's throw (`ThrowByNpc`) refuses an off item (a toggle or the data's bit) with `ItemNotAvailable` before any roll or removal and the AI's throw answers false, which ends the goal as node 7 does (IV21); the item stays in the list (the grenade path's behaviour). The attack starter's weapon choice (`AreaAiHost.ThrowableWeapon`) answers an off weapon as not throwable, so the NPC attacks with it instead of repeating a throw that always fails. The hero's throw paths (`ThrowHeroItem`, `ThrowingHero`) still read the session's record only, without the data bit (the window leaves a data-off item out, so only a worn data-off weapon is affected). Not built: the original's loss of an OFF "default throws" weapon the attack starter removed (IV21, inferred only: the item is kept here), the PC's cursor tile path (IV18 not resolved), nothing for the merchant restock: it already clears every item but the persistent ones without reading OFF (`GameSessionRestock`, ledger items RK5), which is what IV19 reads, so an OFF item of a merchant is destroyed like any other. The spell area queries (0x4407C0, 0x440B40) take their hide mask from the callers' flags (`SpellTargetObject`), not from this flag path. | Steamwoven structure |


## The item set's read commands (ARC-SCRIPT-2347, point 21 insert item: quest state, object type, assign object, is dead, generator test)

Source: issue #2347 (found in ARC-SCRIPT-2335), slice 1. No new exe read: these are commands whose dispatcher reads are already in SG1, OV2, OV3, HW1 and WO9; the rows say what the insert-item point adds to them (IV1) and what Steamwoven builds.

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| IV13 | read-only object questions on an item run: quest state condition (6), get object type (34), assign object (15) (ARC-SCRIPT-2347; SG1 0x4C4CB0, OV2, OV3) | Not new original behaviour: the point does not change the commands. The condition (jump table 0x44714C, entry 6) and the actions (0x44ADBC, entries 14 and 33) are the same dispatcher functions at every point, so an insert-item run reads a quest state, writes an object into a run store and reads a type exactly as SG1, OV2 and OV3 say (the hero's own quest entry, 6 when botched, any other object the quest's global state; the type field, 15 for the PC; assign object writes the triggerer, attachee, extra object or an object variable). On the insertion's runs the triggerer is the holder and the attachee the item (IV1), so a script may re-point either (the tests assign into an object variable; the re-pointing of the triggerer or attachee is the interpreter's, OV2); the commands that follow read and change the re-pointed object, and the session's checks of an effect's or art change's target (the wearer, the shown map's rows) refuse what they do not model, as before. | Original |
| IV14 | obj is dead (22) on an item run (HW1, 0x45D8D0) | The same test as HW1: the object's current hit points are 0 or less. The session answers it for the hero (its life state) and for an NPC the use host holds a row of (map data or a kill); for any other object, an item or a container among them, no hit points are read here, so the host answers null and the move is refused (as an absent object stops a run). | Original; the limit is Steamwoven's |
| IV15 | monster generator disabled (46) on an item run (WO9, 0x4BABA0) | The same table read as WO9: bit 0x80 of the generator counter table's slot, false for a slot outside 0 to 255. The session reads its own generator table (`QueryGeneratorSlot`; the table the toggle action and the spawner write), so the answer does not depend on the shown map. A move that earlier toggled a slot is a case since ARC-SCRIPT-2378 (toggle monster generator, 110, is admitted by the item set): the read sees the move's earlier toggles first ([IV32–IV35](#the-item-sets-toggle-monster-generator-arc-script-2378-point-21-insert-item)). | Original |
| IV16 | what Steamwoven builds (ARC-SCRIPT-2347, slice 1) | The item set adds the conditions 6, 22 and 46, the actions 15 and 34 and the focus kind object variable (11). `ISessionItemWorldHost` gains `QuestState`, `ObjectType` and `IsDead`, which `AreaItemScriptHost` forwards to the shown map's use-script host (the object type and the death need a row it holds; the quest state needs none); the run's host adapter now implements `IScrObjectStateQueries` and `IScrWorldObjectQueries` too, answering only what the set admits (the switched-off, open, can-open and sector questions throw if asked). A question the host does not answer makes the run unanswerable (`ItemScriptUnsupported`, nothing applied). The opt-in census (`ScriptTriggerCensusTests`, `DAT_ROOT`) admits **23** of the 28 point 21 rows on the shown maps' windows (22 after ARC-SCRIPT-2346, 6 before it): of the two rows that stopped on condition 22 and 46 one joins and the other now stops on toggle monster generator (110, [#2378](https://github.com/Simarglok/arcanum-steamwoven/issues/2378)); the 5 left stop on that, set PC quest state (16), get story state (42) and the scenery and critter vicinity kinds (15, 8), one row each (the census counts the first refusal of each row, so a later one is not seen). The quest state, get object type and assign object commands are run by the synthetic tests of `ItemWorldScriptTests`. Nothing is written: the quest write (set PC quest state 16, give quest experience 63), the story-state actions, rename, the scenery and critter vicinity kinds and the item location after a drop stay in [#2347](https://github.com/Simarglok/arcanum-steamwoven/issues/2347) as later slices (listed in IV17). | Steamwoven structure |
| IV17 | what #2347 still holds after slice 1 | Writes: (set PC quest state (16) and give quest experience (63) are ARC-SCRIPT-2374, [IV18–IV21](#the-item-sets-quest-writes-arc-script-2374-point-21-set-pc-quest-state-and-give-quest-experience)), destroy (68), grant fate point (117). (Get and set story state (42, 43), set rumor (50) and rename (92) are built: [IV28–IV31](#the-item-sets-story-state-rumor-and-rename-arc-script-2375-point-21-insert-item).) Writes: toggle monster generator (110, [#2378](https://github.com/Simarglok/arcanum-steamwoven/issues/2378), built: [IV32–IV35](#the-item-sets-toggle-monster-generator-arc-script-2378-point-21-insert-item)). Reads: random (37), get social class and origin (38, 39), the scenery (15) and critter (8) vicinity kinds with call script in (54) into a dialog script, the item location a drop writes (IV8). Each group is its own issue: quest writes [#2374](https://github.com/Simarglok/arcanum-steamwoven/issues/2374), story state, set rumor and rename [#2375](https://github.com/Simarglok/arcanum-steamwoven/issues/2375) (ARC-SCRIPT-2375, built), the critter and scenery vicinity kinds with call script in [#2376](https://github.com/Simarglok/arcanum-steamwoven/issues/2376), random, social class and origin, destroy, grant fate point and the item location after a drop [#2377](https://github.com/Simarglok/arcanum-steamwoven/issues/2377). (Toggle state of the attachee item is ARC-SCRIPT-2346, merged.) | Limit |

## The item set's quest writes (ARC-SCRIPT-2374, point 21: set PC quest state and give quest experience)

Source: issue #2374 (split from #2347, found in ARC-SCRIPT-2335). Read on 2026-11-16 by a read-only exe session (one reader, text exports plus the asm of 0x447220), level F1: the quest setter 0x4C4D20, the internal setter 0x4C4E60, the experience award 0x45F110, the level lookup 0x4C53C0, the action cases of 0x447220 (16 at 0x447543, 63 at 0x448B57) and the insertion's runner call (0x466640, 0x441980). Builds on the dispatcher rows SS2, SQ10 and SQ16.

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| IV18 | the giver of set PC quest state on an item run (0x4C4D20, 0x4C4E60; case 16 at 0x447543) | The action calls 0x4C4D20 once for every object operand 0 expands to, with the run's attachee as the quest giver; 0x4C4D20 never reads the giver itself, it goes to 0x4C4E60, which uses it in three places: the network client's packet (not reached in single player), the reaction change (only for a non-null giver: at state 2 the reaction getter reads 50 unless the PC is type 15 and the giver type 16, so nothing is raised; at state 4 the +10 write tests the same two types) and nothing else (no sound, logbook mark or notification depends on it). On an insert-item run the attachee is the **item**, so the reaction step is a silent no-op: no corruption, no error. A non-PC object does nothing at all (0x4C4CB0 answers the global state; 0x45F110 and 0x4C4E60 stop at their type-15 test). | Original |
| IV19 | what an insert-item run changes about both actions (0x466640 end, 0x441980) | Nothing. The insertion calls the one runner (the same function every point uses) synchronously with the holder as triggerer and the item as attachee after the item's location, weight and light writes; there is no queue, deferral or re-entrancy guard. The merge branch destroys the inserted item right after the point 21 run, which the actions do not touch. Action 63 uses only the first object of operand 0 (0x44B960) and no giver; its amount is the quest experience of a **level** (0x4C53C0: a pure lookup, 0 without an entry), given to a PC by 0x45F110 (0 changes nothing, the difficulty adjusts it, a dead PC gets none, the level steps follow). A completion (request 4) gives the experience **before** the internal setter, even when the shared state then turns the request into 5, as the session's SetQuestState already does (SQ16 should read: 0x4C4D20 gives the experience, 0x4C4E60 the alignment adjustment, the sound 0xBD4 and the logbook light). | Original |
| IV20 | what Steamwoven builds | The item set (`SessionItemScripts.Admission`) adds set PC quest state (16) and give quest experience (63). `ItemScriptRuns` plans a run's state requests as the run makes them (the interpreter tells a host that implements `IScrQuestWrites` each quest request as its action runs, a call-ex callee's too; [#2410](https://github.com/Simarglok/arcanum-steamwoven/issues/2410)), in action order, as `AreaScriptStory.Apply` applies them (a request for a PC only; the quest rules are `GameSessionOwner.QuestStep`, the pure step `SetQuestState` now shares) over copies of the PC's quest record and the quest's shared state, so a later quest read (a later run's, or the same run's after the write, as the original applies a write at once) reads the planned state (`PlannedQuest`); the commit writes the records, the shared states and the hero's awards **without a revision of its own** (the move takes one, `Changes` is true for them). The refusals (`ItemScriptUnsupported`, nothing applied): a quest outside 1000..1999 or an undefined state (the session rejects them, the original stores them unchecked), a state request of a kind the set does not admit, a **further award after one that raises a level** (each award is planned over the hero the earlier ones left, so two level-free awards apply in order and the second may raise the level as the move's last award; over a level-raising one the auto-level scheme's purchases would read the stats it changed, not carried; nor is a level-raising award planned after an effect change of an earlier run of the move, whose stats the scheme would read in the original while the commit applies the effects after the awards), an award that the run's trace shows after an effect change of the hero's list of the same run, or a run with a call-ex that has both (the award reads the experience gain effect of the list as the actions before it left it; the run's own effect changes are collected and applied after it, and a callee's actions are not in the trace; an award after an effect change of an **earlier run** of the move is modelled: it is computed under the planned list, and the commit gives the planned experience, not a new award), an experience amount beyond the session's bound, a run the dispatcher ended with its effects dropped (the line budget, a call-ex outside the points) after a quest request (the original applied it as it went), and (ARC-SCRIPT-2394) a **get stat or power ratio read after an award that raises a level** (the aptitude derives from the magick and tech points an auto-level scheme buys), in the same run (the trace shows it after the award, or a call-ex run) or in a later run of the move (the award wrote the hero's level and stats, which the read answers as stored). An award that **raises a level is carried** (ARC-SCRIPT-2394): the commit writes the hero as `Give` leaves it (the level, the unspent points, the stats an auto-level scheme bought) and queues the `SessionExperienceAward` in the session (`GameSessionOwner.TakeItemScriptAwards`, not saved), which the host takes in the frame of the move to show the level-up messages and to pass the follower level step to the followers and the waiting ones (`AreaAdvancement.LevelFollowers`), as for the award of a talk or a use script (the viewer's `SyncAreaAwards`); an award with no level written queues its follower step too. A session without a hero or without experience tables gives no experience and the move goes on, as the use host does. The commit gives the planned experience again, in order, to the hero as it is then (a hero rewield writes its magick or tech points between the plan and the commit; the store survives, covered by `SessionSpellCasterTraitIndexTests`). A get stat of the hero's experience is not answered by the session (the run is refused as not modelled), so no read can see the award before it is applied. Not applied here, as in the use host: the alignment adjustment of a completion, the sound, the logbook light and the "experience received" display. | Steamwoven structure; limits |
| IV21 | what the installed scripts do now (the opt-in census, `ScriptTriggerCensusTests`, `DAT_ROOT`, 2026-11-16) | The item set admits **24** of the 28 point 21 rows on the shown maps' windows (23 after ARC-SCRIPT-2347): the one row that stopped on set PC quest state (16) joins. The 4 left stop on toggle monster generator (110, [#2378](https://github.com/Simarglok/arcanum-steamwoven/issues/2378)), get story state (42, [#2375](https://github.com/Simarglok/arcanum-steamwoven/issues/2375)) and the scenery and critter vicinity kinds (15, 8, [#2376](https://github.com/Simarglok/arcanum-steamwoven/issues/2376)), one row each. The synthetic tests of `ItemWorldScriptTests` run the two actions (the quest log and the experience in the pickup's one revision, a save, the hero-only rule, the ended and botched quest, the refusals above). | Data |

## The item set's critter and scenery vicinity kinds (ARC-SCRIPT-2376, point 21 insert item)

Source: issue #2376 (split from #2347, slice 2). Read on 2026-11-16 in the exports of the retail 1.0.7.4 `Arcanum.exe` (`research-private/ghidra-export/c/funcs/0044b4e0.c`, the object-list builder), in the same session that built the answer. The list builder's cases for the kinds were already read for the other points (PG3, TG4); the rows below say what an insert-item run adds (IV1, IV2) and what Steamwoven builds.

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| IV25 | object list focus kinds 8, 9, 15, 16 of an insert-item run (0x44B4E0 cases 8/9 and 0xF/0x10, 0x440FC0; PG3, TG4, IV2) | The four kinds are two cases of the one builder that also serves the container kinds (17, 18, IV2): each reads the run's **attachee** handle (the object operand slot of the invocation at +0x10 and +0x14; with a null handle the list is empty) and calls the one vicinity scan (0x440FC0) with it and a type mask: 0x18000 (PCs and NPCs) for kinds 8 and 9, 8 (scenery) for 15 and 16, 4 (containers) for 17 and 18. The copy loop stops at 100 objects. So on an insert-item run the centre is the **item's own location field** (the stale tile a held item keeps, IV2) for critters and scenery exactly as for containers; the holder is not the centre. A critter list holds the hero and the dead when they stand in the rectangle, and any critter the map data places there; the builder's rule of the editor mask and of the inventory flag is the one TG4 and PG3 give. Kinds 8 and 9 are one handler (the condition tail tells "any" from "every", SG6), as 15 and 16 are. | Original |
| IV26 | call script in (54) into a dialog script on an insert-item run (0x44867B, TI8) | Not new original behaviour: the action stores a deferred call (script, line, triggerer, attachee, delay) in the event queue as it does at any point; the four roles are read when the action runs (an object-list focus kind such as the looped object gives the current object) and whether the objects still exist is decided when the call fires (TE9). The installed scripts 2096 and 2702 (a loop over everyone in the vicinity, a name test on the looped critter, call script in with the looped critter as attachee and a 2-second delay), 2390 and 2103 (the same kind with other tests) and 1239 (a scenery loop and a follower loop, a name test and a call script in with the looped object as attachee) use it. | Original |
| IV27 | what Steamwoven builds (ARC-SCRIPT-2376) | The item set (`SessionItemScripts.Admission`) admits the focus kinds 8, 9, 15 and 16 (the critter kinds in actions only, as the interpreter admits them: a condition over a list is not modelled, ARC-SCRIPT-047) and keeps call script in (54), which the move schedules in the session's event queue as it commits (TI8). `ISessionItemWorldHost` gains `SceneryInVicinity(item)` and `CrittersInVicinity(item)` (default: no answer, so a host without them leaves the run unanswerable, as for the container kinds); `AreaItemScriptHost` forwards them to the shown map's use host (`SceneryNearItem`, `CrittersNearItem`), which scans around the same centre as `ContainersNearItem` (IV2: the placed cell of a session-dropped item, else the tile of the map data or of the carried item's row; an item of another map, one made at run time or one without a tile has none, and the session then refuses the move). The scans are the use host's own (`SceneryInVicinity`, `CrittersInVicinity`: the reach of `ScrWorld.VicinityReach` tiles on both axes, not destroyed or switched off or gone, the NPCs at their live cells, then the hero at its live cell; the list order is not read). The run's host adapter answers `IScrWorldQueries` for the two kinds from the host's world part (the random, location and within-range questions still throw: not admitted). The deferred call is checked and put in the event queue as before (the roles are the hero or an object of the shown map; at most `MaxScriptCalls` pending). The opt-in census (`ScriptTriggerCensusTests`, `DAT_ROOT`) admits **26** of the 28 point 21 rows on the shown maps' windows (24 after ARC-SCRIPT-2374, [IV21](#the-item-sets-quest-writes-arc-script-2374-point-21-set-pc-quest-state-and-give-quest-experience)): the two rows that stopped on the kinds 15 and 8 join; the 2 left stop on toggle monster generator (110, [#2378](https://github.com/Simarglok/arcanum-steamwoven/issues/2378)) and get story state (42, [#2375](https://github.com/Simarglok/arcanum-steamwoven/issues/2375)), one row each. The set is the one of every item point (get, drop, insert, wield, remove, transfer), so the kinds are admitted at each, and only point 21 is counted. Only an item has the centre (a map row of an item type, a placed or carried item): the callee of a call script ex run for an NPC or scenery gets no answer and the move is refused. Of the five installed scripts the set admits 1239, 2096, 2390 and 2702 whole wherever they are attached (the census counts the shown rows only); 2103 stops on action 110. Script 1239 still ends in a refusal of the move at its toggle state of the looped scenery: the session keeps the off flag of an item of the move only (IV11), so it refuses a pickup of the item that carries it (a limit, no scenery flag is written). | Steamwoven structure |

## The item set's story state, rumor and rename (ARC-SCRIPT-2375, point 21 insert item)

Source: issue #2375 (found in ARC-SCRIPT-2347, split from PR #2391). No new exe read: the dispatcher reads are already in SS1 (get and set story state, 0x445090, 0x4450A0), SS4 (set rumor, 0x448497, 0x4C57E0) and CQ5 (rename, 0x447220 case 0x5C with 0x4EFDD0), and the insertion's order is IV9; the rows say what the insert-item point adds to them and what Steamwoven builds. Level F1 (reads of 2026-11-16 in the existing exports; nothing new needed).

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| IV28 | get and set story state (42, 43) on an item run (SS1) | The dispatcher cases are the same at every point, and the story-state number is the one game-wide number, so an insert-item run reads and raises it exactly as a use or dialog run does: get story state (42) writes the number into a store, set story state (43) stores its value only when it is larger (a signed compare), whoever the triggerer is (no object operand). The write lands with the run's other writes, before the next event, so a later run of the same move reads the raised number. The four installed point 21 scripts that name it: 1094 (a placed row on the shown maps, read story, raise it to 19 when it is above 17, set a global flag), the others below. | Original |
| IV29 | set rumor (50) on an item run (SS4) | The same function as SS4: the rumor (operand 0) for the first object operand 1 names; a PC that does not know it stores the current game time, a known one keeps its first time. The triggerer of an insertion is the holder the item enters (IV1), so the hero's pickup teaches the rumor to the hero, an NPC holder's ground pickup does nothing (no PC; the original's learn function acts for a PC only). The installed scripts 1103 (the fake horror book, rumor 2002) and 1566 (the amulet in the Joachim room, rumor 2145) are prototype scripts: no placed row on the shown maps, so the opt-in census does not count them (it reads them whole in its own test). | Original |
| IV30 | rename (92) on an item run (CQ5) | The same function as CQ5: the first object of operand 0 and the number of operand 1; the name number (field 0x16) of the object is written with no type test, so the item takes it. The insertion has already linked the item into the holder when the script runs (IV9), so the write is to the item that sits in the holder's list. 1573 (the female servant's clothes) renames its attachee to 2805. The name number is the class the name conditions and the item finders compare; the shown text comes from the description, which a rename does not touch. | Original |
| IV31 | what Steamwoven builds (ARC-SCRIPT-2375) | The item set (`SessionItemScripts.Admission`) admits get and set story state, set rumor and rename (the dialog action stays out). `ItemScriptRuns` plans them in the move: the story-state number the runs raise (`StoryNow` is what the next run reads, the session's number else) and the hero's rumors are written by `Commit` in the move's own revision (no revision of their own; a known rumor keeps its time; a rumor outside the session's 1000 to 2999 or any other state request, such as a quest or a reputation, refuses the move whole, nothing applied; a rumor asked for an object that is not the hero is skipped as SS4 says). A rename of an item of the move is the item's own object field `item.name` (`SessionItemName`, a mobile object field of the directory entry: carried with the item, saved in the objects section, no schema step), kept only while it differs from the data's number (`ISessionItemWorldHost.ItemNameInData`, read from the loaded rows; a made item has no row and the hero's name number is not modelled, so such a rename refuses the move); a second rename of the item in the move follows the first, and a later run of the move reads the pending number (the run host's name read). The item's record goes with the item when it is deleted. Only the item event path writes an item's name number: the use host's rename still skips a non-NPC (CQ5 use set limit). The name readers (`AreaUseScriptHost.NameOf(session, row)`, which the name condition and the dialog's follower-named test use) read the item's record. The opt-in census admits **27** of the 28 point 21 rows on the shown maps' windows (26 after the vicinity kinds, IV27: the row of 1094 joins); the one left stops on toggle monster generator (110, [#2378](https://github.com/Simarglok/arcanum-steamwoven/issues/2378)). Not built: a rename of an item the move does not carry (the hero, another object, a made item). The item finders and wielded-item reads follow the record since ARC-SCRIPT-2408 (IV32, IV33). | Steamwoven structure and limits |
| IV32 | who reads the name number (field 0x16) of an item (ARC-SCRIPT-2408) | Read on 2026-11-16 (level F1) in the exports of the retail 1.0.7.4 `Arcanum.exe`. The field getter 0x406CA0 (→ 0x408A20) answers the instance's own value when the instance holds one (0x40D320) and the prototype's only when it does not; rename (92, 0x447220 case 0x5C → 0x4EFDD0 → the instance setter 0x406D40) gives the instance its own field, so every reader that goes through the getter sees the new number. Outside the object-system internals nothing reads the prototype's name directly. The readers of an item's name: the inventory finder **0x462480** (a container's or a critter's list, the first item whose live field 0x16 equals the name), condition 17 (has item named, 0x4451B0: a count of holders through 0x462480), condition 21 (wielding item named: the item at each wield location 1000..1007 through 0x4649C0, its live field compared), action 41 (0x29: the item the finder returns is moved to the target) and action 126 (0x7E: the finder's item is removed), the dialog conditions has item (0xB) and does not have item (0x17) and the dialog item action (0x415BA0 case 9), all through 0x462480 (the NPC side first, then the 0x45F650 object; the PC side the hero, then the group list 0x4411C0); the spot function 0x4BBE40 compares the live name of the item at wield location 1005 with the constant 0xB32 (2866, the shield). A renamed item is therefore found by its new number only; nothing compares the prototype's number after a rename. Not read: 0x45F650, 0x4617F0, 0x43CCA0, the callers of 0x4BBE40 and a read of the field through a computed field id. | Original |
| IV33 | what Steamwoven builds (ARC-SCRIPT-2408) | `GameSessionOwner.QueryItemName(entity, dataName)` answers the item's renamed number (`SessionItemName`, found by the item's identity wherever the item is) and the data's otherwise; a made item has no record and keeps the data's. Every reader that matches an item by number asks it per item: the use host's held and wielded item lists (`HoldsItemNamed` 17, `WieldedItemName` 21) for the hero (kit, pack, made items) and for a holder, the deferred call's hero wield read, the script item finder (`QueryNamedItem`), the dialog's item tests (`PartyHolds`, `NpcHolds`) and take, the NPC give-by-name finder (`GiveItemToPc`), and the spot-item and NPC wield-location reads of the trap code. Not built (limits unchanged): a rename of the hero's own or a made item (it refuses the move, IV31), the callers of 0x4BBE40 beyond the trap spot (not read), and the item count and the other holders' reads that do not match by name. | Steamwoven structure and limits |

## The item set's toggle monster generator (ARC-SCRIPT-2378, point 21 insert item)

Source: issue #2378 (split from #2347). No new exe read: toggle monster generator (action 110, condition 46) is WO9 (0x4BABF0, 0x4BABD0) and the dispatcher is the same at every point; the rows below say what the insert-item point adds to it (IV1) and what Steamwoven builds.

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| IV32 | toggle monster generator (110) on an insert-item run (case 0x6E; WO9, IV1) | Not new original behaviour: the action reads one number, flips bit 0x80 of that slot's byte in the game-wide generators' counter table (set when clear, cleared when set) and changes nothing for a slot outside 0..255. The toggle is written at once, so the condition 46 of the same run or of a later run of the move reads the new bit, and a second toggle of the slot puts it back. The table belongs to no object: the holder, the item and the triggerer play no part. Script 2103 (everyone in the vicinity, condition 46, call script in) is the installed script that holds it at this point. | Original |
| IV33 | what Steamwoven builds (ARC-SCRIPT-2378) | The item set admits action 110. `ItemScriptRuns` plans each run's generator requests (the interpreter's request carries the state the slot ends in; a second toggle of the slot in the same run or a later one of the move simply overwrites the plan) in `_generatorBlocked`, so the run's host adapter answers condition 46 under the move's earlier toggles (`PlannedGenerator`) before the session's table; `Commit` writes the final state of each slot into the table without a revision of its own (the move takes one, flag writes and quest writes alike), and `Changes` counts a slot only when its final state differs from the session's (two toggles of one slot are no change). A world change of any other kind (the sector toggle, 106) or a slot outside the table refuses the move (`ItemScriptUnsupported`, nothing applied), and an action 110 with a slot outside 0..255 never reaches the plan, as the interpreter drops it. | Steamwoven structure |
| IV34 | what the installed scripts do now (the opt-in census, `ScriptTriggerCensusTests`, `DAT_ROOT`, 2026-11-16) | The item set admitted **27** of the 28 point 21 rows on the shown maps' windows (still 27 after [ARC-SCRIPT-2426](#the-item-sets-distance-between-arc-script-2426-point-21-insert-item), IV36–IV39), as before: the row of 2103 passes toggle monster generator now and stops on distance between (71) in the same script (the run's host adapter answered no location then), [#2426](https://github.com/Simarglok/arcanum-steamwoven/issues/2426). The synthetic tests of `ItemWorldScriptTests` run the toggle in a pickup (one revision, a save, a blocked slot cleared), two toggles of the same slot in one run and across slots, a condition 46 read after the toggle, a slot outside the table and a move the session cannot apply (the sector toggle) refused with nothing written. | Data |
| IV35 | what is not modelled | The toggle of a move that is refused is not applied (the move is refused whole). A move whose later run reads condition 46 is answered under the plan; a call-ex callee's reads and toggles are inside the interpreter's own run store, so they agree with the plan. Spawning is the generator host's (ARC-WORLD-1157): this slice changes the table only. | Limit |

## The item set's distance between (ARC-SCRIPT-2426, point 21 insert item)

Source: issue #2426 (split from #2378). No new exe read for the action itself: distance between (action 71) is HW3 (case 71 at 0x448E25, object distance 0x441AE0, tile distance 0x4B96F0), the same dispatcher case at every point. What this slice re-checked in the exports is the one fact the item run adds: the generic field reader 0x406DA0 (handle, then the field validity check 0x40C260, then the field getter 0x408A20) takes field 2, the location, for any object type, since fields 1 to 36 belong to every object (OM8), so an item answers it as a critter does and nothing in the dispatcher tests for null or for the type.

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| IV36 | distance between (71) on an insert-item run (case 71 at 0x448E25, 0x44B960, 0x406DA0 field 2, 0x4B96F0; HW3, OM8) | Not new original behaviour: the first object of operand 0 and of operand 1, each one's location field (field 2, a 64-bit tile pair) without a null test, the larger of the two axis differences into operand 2 as its low 32 bits; the run goes on. The roles are the run's: the attachee is the item, the triggerer the holder (IV1), the looped object of a loop for (IV3), the player (focus kind 21). Script 2103 (everyone in the vicinity, the one installed point 21 row that holds it) measures the looped critter against the player and stores the number in a local variable. | Original |
| IV37 | what a location is for each kind of object | A critter's field 2 is its live tile and the hero's its cell; a scenery, a container or a portal row's is the tile of its map data. An item's field 2 is its **own** location field, never the holder's (IV2): an insertion does not write it, so an item in a holder's list, in a container or in an NPC's list reads the stale tile it had (where the map placed it, or the tile it lay on when it was picked up), and an item lying on the ground the tile it lies on. A run-time (made) item's location is what its creation wrote, which the model does not read. | Original (item fields) |
| IV38 | what Steamwoven builds (ARC-SCRIPT-2426) | The item set (`SessionItemScripts.Admission`) admits action 71. `ISessionItemWorldHost` gains `LocationOf(obj)` (default: no place), which the run's host adapter asks for each of the action's two objects; the item host (`AreaItemScriptHost`) passes it to the shown map's use host (`AreaUseScriptHost.LocationOfObject`): an item reads the centre the run's vicinity scans use (`ItemCentre`, IV2: a session-dropped item's tile, else the tile of its map data or of the carried item's row), any other object its place (the hero's live cell, a critter's live cell or row tile, a row's tile). The distance itself is the interpreter's (HW3), so a host's answer is the one thing the item run adds. | Steamwoven structure |
| IV39 | what is not modelled | An object the host cannot place (a made item, an item of another map, an unknown entity, no shown map) stops the run as an absent object does, and the session refuses the move whole (`ItemScriptUnsupported`, nothing applied). The held item is never placed at its holder's tile. An item the hero dropped and picked up again reads the tile of its map data here (the session keeps the dropped tile only while the item lies on the ground), where the original's field 2 would hold the dropped tile: the same limit as the vicinity scans of IV2. The condition within range (34) and get location (95) stay outside the item set, so only distance between asks for a location. The opt-in census (`ScriptTriggerCensusTests`, `DAT_ROOT`, 2026-11-16) still counts **27** of the 28 point 21 rows on the shown maps' windows: the row of 2103 passes distance between now and stops on change script (99, line 10: the looped critter's dialog point, NS4), the only command of the script outside the set, [#2432](https://github.com/Simarglok/arcanum-steamwoven/issues/2432). The synthetic tests of `ItemWorldScriptTests` run distance between of a looped critter and the hero, of the item and the hero, and an object the host cannot place (refused, nothing applied); `AreaHeartbeatSceneryLoopTests` reads the use host's locations. | Limit, Data |

## The item set's change script (ARC-SCRIPT-2432, point 21 insert item)

Source: issue #2432 (split from #2426). Read on 2026-11-16 by one reader (level F1) in the exports of the retail 1.0.7.4 `Arcanum.exe`: the script action interpreter 0x447220 (case 99), the item event runner 0x441980, the field reader 0x407840 with its worker 0x408BB0, the writer 0x4F01D0 with 0x4078A0 and 0x4088B0, and the field type test 0x40C260. Behaviour in prose; no bytes. The independent check is the review of this task.

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| IV40 | change script (99) on an insert-item run (case 99 of 0x447220, the runner 0x441980, the reader 0x407840 → 0x408BB0, the writer 0x4F01D0 → 0x4078A0 → 0x4088B0; NS4) | Read on 2026-11-16 by one reader (level F1) in the exports of the retail 1.0.7.4 `Arcanum.exe` (ARC-SCRIPT-2432). Nothing in the action depends on the item event: the runner builds the run record (the triggerer, the attachee item, the point 0x15, a copy of the item's own point element) and compares operand 0's handle with the record's attachee and operand 1 with the record's point; only the item itself at point 21 takes the pending-result branch (the run's result, written back by the runner after the run, only when the element changed). A looped critter always fails the handle test, so its write goes the other way, whatever the point: the element is read (0x407840, a stack buffer), the third word (the script) replaced, and the buffer written back through 0x4F01D0 (the writer is 0x4078A0; 0x407840 is the reader, which NS4 lists with it), so the flags and counters stay. The field (0x20, the attachment array) is allowed for every object type (the field test 0x40C260 passes it in the common range), so there is no type test: a critter, a scenery, a portal, an item and a PC all take it. A missing element is as NS4 says. | Original |
| IV41 | what else the write does | On an instance the write makes sure the field's slot exists, sets the field's modified bit and the object's dirty halfword (+0x44, the "changed from the prototype" mark the sector save reads: medium, the save path was not followed); on a prototype only the modified bit. With the multiplayer flag set it sends a network packet (not reached in a single-player game). No event, no dialog-state reset, no AI or notice call, no art or flag change: the new script takes effect the next time the point fires on the critter (its dialog, for script 2103's point). | Original |
| IV42 | what Steamwoven builds (ARC-SCRIPT-2432) | The item set (`SessionItemScripts.Admission`) admits action 99. The run's own attachee and point stay the run's result as before; any other slot write of a run (a looped critter's, a call-ex callee's) was refused as "no home in the session" and is now kept as map changes: `ISessionItemWorldHost.PlanObjectSlot` (default: none) asks the host, which gives the object's edits; `AreaItemScriptHost` passes it to the shown map's use host (`AreaUseScriptHost.PlanCritterSlot`), which plans the same edits a use run writes for an NPC's slot (`dialog.script`, `dialog.lf`, `dialog.ctr` under the object's own keys, a part back at its loaded value clears its record) and answers only for a **critter** row with the loaded element at a point the host keeps. The session adds them to the move's map changes (one revision, the save keeps them), remembers the slot so a later run of the move reads it, and checks the edits name the object. A critter's attachment is read through the use host (the loaded element under the session's map changes: an earlier move's change is seen) instead of the loaded row alone. | Steamwoven structure |
| IV43 | what is not modelled | Any other object (a scenery, a container, a portal, an item that is not of the move, the hero), a critter with no loaded element at the point (the original reads an uninitialised buffer, NS4), a point the use host does not keep, and a host with no shown map refuse the whole move (`ItemScriptUnsupported`, nothing applied). The object's "changed from prototype" mark (IV41) is the session's own map change. The opt-in census (`ScriptTriggerCensusTests`, `DAT_ROOT`) counts **28** of the 28 point 21 rows on the shown maps' windows (27 before: the row of 2103 stopped on action 99 only). The synthetic tests of `ItemWorldScriptTests` run the change of two looped critters (one revision, the held flags and counters kept, a save), a second change of the same point, an object the host cannot keep (refused, nothing written), the hero, and a host with no slot plan; `AreaItemScriptHostTests` runs the use host's plan. | Limit, Data |

## The item set's random, social class, origin and fate point (ARC-SCRIPT-2377, point 21 insert item)

Source: issue #2377 (split from #2347). Read on 2026-11-17 by one reader (level F1) in the exports of the retail 1.0.7.4 `Arcanum.exe` for the destroy question (the insertion 0x466640 with its callers 0x461810 and 0x461CA0, the destroy 0x43CCA0, the removal 0x466E00 / 0x467860, the runner 0x441980); the other four commands are the generic dispatcher cases already read (PG5, CQ1, CQ2, SQ11).

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| IV44 | random (37) on an insert-item run (PG5) | The same dispatcher case as at every point: the bounds in either order, both included, from the game's generator (0x4CEF00), into the store of operand 2. The item event changes nothing about it. Steamwoven draws from the session's own generator: the runs of a move take a copy of the stream, continue one sequence, and the commit makes the copy the session's (no draw is spent by a refused move; a generator at its end refuses the move as `RandomExhausted`). The draws take no revision of their own; the move's one revision covers them. | Original; Steamwoven structure |
| IV45 | get social class and get origin (38, 39; CQ1, CQ2) | The same cases at every point: an NPC's field, 0 for the PC and any other type. The item set asks the host's world part (`ISessionItemWorldHost.SocialClassOf`, `OriginOf`), which forwards to the shown map's use host (the map data's class, the origin as a dialog action left it). An object that host has no row for (a made or created object, an object of another map, an item the use host does not know) stops the run and refuses the move; the original reads 0 for such a type. The commit adds the draws of a move's runs to the session's position (a move with two sets of runs, the hero's and a merchant's, keeps both counts; their runs may draw the same numbers). Get faction (89) and get armor (32) stay out. | Original; Steamwoven structure |
| IV46 | grant fate point (117; SQ11, FT7, FT8) | The same case at every point: stat 20 plus one for the first object when it is a PC, clamped to 100 by the setter. The move counts one grant per request for a PC (any other object's request does nothing) and the commit raises the hero's points over the hero as the move's awards left them, in the move's own revision; at 100 the grant is lost; a session without a hero grants nothing. | Original; Steamwoven structure |
| IV47 | destroy (68) of the attachee item on an insert-item run (0x43CCA0, 0x466E00, 0x467860) | By the time the point-21 runner is called the insertion is complete on the main path: the item is in the holder's list with its slot, parent and count, the worn effects (slots 1000 to 1008) have run and the weights are recomputed. The destroy (a destroyed object returns at once) runs the item's own destroy point (2, which can refuse), then removes the item from its parent (the inverse of the insertion: list, count, slot, worn effects, weights, interface hooks, and the item's own remove-item point 0x1C script nested in the run), lays it on the parent's tile, clears its animations and spells and flags it destroyed; the script goes on. The insertion does not look at the item again: the holder refresh and, for the player, the interface hooks run, and the transfer reports success with the item gone. The destroyed handle stays valid until the map is cleared. On the merge path (0x46687A) the incoming item is free-standing, so the script's destroy only flags it and the insertion's own destroy afterwards returns at once. A destroy of the triggerer has no player guard: only the holder's invulnerable items drop (OM18), the new item stays in the destroyed holder's list. A key destroyed this way is still copied to a key ring (an original stale-state quirk). Any other object takes the generic destroy. | Original (the key-ring copy likely an original bug) |
| IV48 | what Steamwoven builds | Destroy stays outside the item set: a move cannot undo an insertion it has planned (the item lands in the hero's list or a holder's list in the same revision, with the destroy point and remove-item script of the item as nested runs). Follow-up: [#2593](https://github.com/Simarglok/arcanum-steamwoven/issues/2593). No installed point 21 script uses it (IV49). | Steamwoven limit |
| IV49 | what the installed scripts do now | The 28 point 21 rows on the shown maps' windows were all admitted before this slice (28 of 28, IV40); none of the four commands added here appears in them as the census read them, so the census counts do not move. The commands are admitted for completeness and for prototype or run-time scripts. | Data |
| IV50 | what is not modelled | The item location a drop writes (IV8): built by [#2594](https://github.com/Simarglok/arcanum-steamwoven/issues/2594), IV51; what stays is IV51's list (one made inside a pack and never on the ground; the loose map-data drop is built by [#2701](https://github.com/Simarglok/arcanum-steamwoven/issues/2701), IV52) and the cross-map teleport write of the field (RL10). | Limit |
| IV51 | what Steamwoven builds for IV8 (ARC-SCRIPT-2594, schema 105) | The session keeps, for each item it put on the ground (a hero's drop, a throw's landing, a script's creation) and a pickup (the hero's, an NPC's, a give or a loot of a junk pile) took up again, the tile it lay on: `inventory.lastDrops`, by entity, saved and upgraded (an older save starts with none), forgotten when the item is destroyed, a drop again lets the placed cell answer until the next pickup, bounded by the placed rows' bound. The item host's centre of the vicinity kinds (containers, scenery, critters) and of distance between is the placed cell of an item lying on the shown map, else this tile, else the tile of its map data or its carried row. The tile counts also when the item's reference names another map: the original scans the current map around the stale coordinates (IV2). At the bound a new pickup records no tile (the item then reads the tile of its map data or has none), deterministically and across save and load. Not covered: an item made inside a pack and never on the ground, which has no tile (the move is refused); a map-data item that lay loose by an NPC's critical drop, a throw's landing, a spill, a body decay or a push is IV52. Level: F0 (the field's rewrite rules are IV2, RL10; no new exe read). | Built |
| IV52 | what Steamwoven builds for the loose map-data drop (ARC-SCRIPT-2701, no schema step) | A map-data item that lies loose on another tile than its map data holds (an NPC critical's drop, a container's spill, an NPC throw's landing, a body decay, a push: `LooseCell`, `MovedCell`) reads that tile as its location field. While it lies there the item host's centre of the vicinity kinds and of distance between is that tile (`QueryItemLooseCell`), so the insert-item run of its own pickup, which runs before the move commits, already centres on it; when the hero's pickup (also from a junk pile) takes it, the session records the tile in `inventory.lastDrops` (IV51) and the held item keeps reading it. An item taken from its own data tile records nothing (the map data answers), a drop again lets the placed cell answer. Not covered: a pickup by an NPC and a later give to the hero (the NPC carries the location field in the original, an NPC's pickup is not recorded); the hero's own loose drop of a seed item stays IV51. Level: F0 (the field's rewrite rules are IV2, RL10; no new exe read). | Built |

## Follower focus kinds (ARC-SCRIPT-2320)

Source: issue #2320 (found in ARC-RES-2262). Read on 2026-11-15 by one reader (level F1) in the exports of the retail 1.0.7.4 `Arcanum.exe`: the object-list builder 0x44B4E0 (cases 2 and 3 and their neighbours), the first-object function 0x44B960 with its list free 0x44B8F0, the direct follower list 0x4411C0, the recursive follower list 0x441260 and its free 0x4410E0, the party list 0x441310, the team list 0x4413E0, and the field readers behind them (0x4079C0, 0x407610, 0x40C260). The assembly of 0x44B960 was checked for the result buffer. Not read: what the pool lookup does with a null handle, and the network-game party iterator.

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| FK1 | focus kinds 2 and 3 in the list builder (0x44B4E0) | One switch case serves both: the list is built from the run's **triggerer** (the record field at +8, the one kind 0 returns), not from the attachee (+0x10) that the vicinity kinds use. The builder does not tell "every" from "any" (as PV1): the caller decides. No leader lookup (0x45DDA0) happens: the followers of the triggerer itself are listed, so a script run by a PC lists the hero's party followers and one run by an NPC leader lists that NPC's followers. | Original |
| FK2 | direct followers list (0x4411C0) | The list holds the handles of the object's follower array (object field 241, the one a PC and an NPC have; any other type has no such field, the count reads 0 and the list is empty), in array order (join order), one node each. **Direct followers only**: the recursion (0x441260, followers of followers) belongs to the team list (kinds 6 and 7) and to bad associates (CS12), not to kinds 2 and 3. No filter: destroyed, switched off, dead and carried objects all stay. The copy loop of the builder stops at 100 entries (the cap of PG3). | Original |
| FK3 | first object of an operand (0x44B960) | Builds the same list into a 100-slot array and returns slot 0, the triggerer's first direct follower. It ignores the count and the buffer is not zeroed, so for an empty list the returned handle is whatever was on the stack (an original hazard, as PV4). There is no null test for a missing triggerer either (what the field read does with a null handle was not followed). | Original (hazards) |
| FK4 | the neighbours of the case (0x441310, 0x4413E0, group kinds) | Party kinds 4 and 5 in a single-player game list only the triggerer itself (no type test; the network game walks the party iterator). Team kinds 6 and 7 list the party and, for a PC, each member's recursive followers. Group kinds 13 and 14 list the same direct followers as kinds 2 and 3 and append the triggerer last (n + 1 entries). | Original |
| FK5 | what Steamwoven builds | The set **S65** is S64 (the group kinds of ARC-SCRIPT-2319, the container kinds of ARC-SCRIPT-2322, get armor of ARC-SCRIPT-2326, any scenery of ARC-SCRIPT-2321) plus the focus kinds 2 and 3 (`ScrFocusKind.EveryFollower`, `AnyFollower`), which the interpreter resolves for a list action, for a condition that walks its list by the rule of SG6 (the five `GuardConditions` and the `ListConditions` of ARC-SCRIPT-2326, as for the group kinds; kind 3 is an "any" kind) and for a first object, through `IScrGroupQueries.FollowersOf(leader)`, the answer the group kinds already use (GF2: the same direct follower list, which the group kinds follow with the triggerer), under the same cap as the vicinity (`VicinityListCapped`, Original profile). A run without a triggerer has no followers (the original has no test; the safe model, not read). The five guards (quest state, holds an item named, is named, wields an item named, has a reputation) walk the list by the rule of SG6 (`ScrInterpreter.GuardHolds`).
