A condition that reads one object (following, jilted, met before, can see, can hear, obj is open) refuses the kinds at admission, as it does for kinds 8 and 9 (SG6's note: the dispatcher admits single-object focus there); a follower the run itself disbanded (action 22, held until applied) is left out of the list. `AreaUseScriptHost.FollowersOf` answers for the hero the session's party followers and for an NPC of the shown map the NPC's own followers (`QueryFollowers`), in join order, unfiltered; any other object, and a session without a hero, give an empty list. The use, placed, heartbeat, combat-point and dying sets admit the kinds; the dialog-talk host admits them with the group kinds (`GroupFocusKinds`); the item event hosts admit them since ARC-SCRIPT-2336 (FK8). | Original |
| FK6 | what the installed scripts do now (opt-in checks, `DAT_ROOT`; census probe `--set S65`) | The 10 attached roots that name kind 2 (9 roots, 22 sites) or kind 3 (script 1056, 1 site) are admitted whole under S65 (counts in the census note; +10 roots and +23 sites against S64, as the census of #2262 expected); no root is left that the two kinds alone refused; the host sets run 22 of the 23 sites, and since ARC-SCRIPT-2336 the item event host admits the kinds as well (the 23rd site, point 21: FK8). The pinned host counts of the real-data checks moved with them. | Data |
| FK7 | what is not modelled | The followers of an NPC that is not on the shown map are not listed (the host has no row for it); an NPC leader's followers are the session's, so a follower an NPC was given by a script of another map is listed only while the session holds it. The item event host (attachment point 21, 1 of the 23 sites) admits the kinds since ARC-SCRIPT-2336 (FK8). Group kinds 13 and 14 (the same list plus the triggerer, FK4) are admitted by ARC-SCRIPT-2319 (GF1–GF8); party and team kinds 4 to 7 are not admitted. The order of followers is the session's list order. A follower the run itself recruits (make follow, held until applied) is not listed before the run ends, where the original's array has it at once. | Limit |
| FK8 | the item event host (ARC-SCRIPT-2336, point 21 insert item) | The session's item set (`SessionItemScripts.Admission`, S14-item) admits the focus kinds every follower and any follower (2, 3; the dispatcher's guard, that a set with them needs a host that answers `IScrGroupQueries`, is met by the run's host adapter), and the adapter answers the follower list through the host's world part (`ISessionItemWorldHost.FollowersOf`; null, no shown map, makes the run unanswerable and the session refuses the move, as for the container vicinity). The triggerer of an insert-item run is the holder the item enters (IV1): the hero's party followers for a pickup, so the list is those of the hero (`AreaItemScriptHost` hands the question to the shown map's use host, FK5), and the same follower list serves the item events of an NPC (pickup, throw) for an NPC leader on the shown map. The one installed point 21 site that names the kinds is script 1239, attached to the item of the void cave of Kriggyrd's falchion; it also reads the scenery vicinity kind 15, which the item set still refuses (issue #2347, the scenery loop), so the site stays refused for that reason and the census pin of the item set (6 of the 28 point 21 rows on the shown maps) does not move. Of the six scripts the follower kinds free in the use set (1165, 1239, 2341, 2346, 2616, 2763), only 1239 has a point 21 site on the shown maps; the other five stop the item set on other commands (a leader the shown map has no row for, such as a created critter, makes the host's answer null and the move refused, as for the name test; the group kinds 13 and 14 stay outside the item set). See FK6 and FK7 for the other sets. | Original (kinds), Limit (site) |

## Final line to a node the DLG lacks (ARC-SCRIPT-2790)

Read 2026-11-19 from the exports (Opus subagent; the token table 0x5A0750 row 72 `fl`, row 73 `co`). Earlier rows: CA1–CA2 (the ids are FLM1 here to stay apart from the tile-script FM1).

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| FLM1 | action interpreter, case 3 `fl` (0x415BA0), final-line start (0x413A30), NPC-line builder (0x416840), record lookup (0x416AB0, compare 0x417E00) | The case stores N as the current line and calls 0x413A30, which calls the line builder. The lookup is a binary search by id over the loaded DLG's records and tests existence only, never the record's role. When no record has id N the builder sets the voice id to -1 and the text to a single space, returns 0 and shows no message; the case then sets the dialog state to 4 (final line) and the string goes on, so a later `co` still sets its flag and the attack runs at the string's end (CA2). The caller returns before the normal next-line step and the interface closes the dialog after showing the (blank) final line. No error, no fallback text. | modelled: the reply stays offered (no `TargetInvalid`), the line is blank, the dialog ends (`NpcFinalLine`), the string's other operations and the `co` event run. Installed: Fahrkus' "I will kill you" reply (dialog 2792, record 42, `fl 179 ,co`, no record 179) and 23 more replies in 8 files. A node that exists as a Player record is used by the original too (existence test only) but what it then does with the record's text prefixes and action is unread: 14 replies stay refused (#2804). jo/uw/et with a missing node are another routine (0x413A90) and unread. |

### Leader killing, point 20 (ARC-SCRIPT-2859)

Read in the kill step 0x45DA20 (exe export, 2026-11-19); behaviour in prose.

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| LK1 | kill step 0x45DA20 (the call 0x441980 with point 0x14) | The loop sits inside the death processing, so it runs only when the dying script (12) allowed the death. It follows, in this order: the kill notice, the experience award to the killer's PC leader (0x45F110, worth fifth), the leader's alignment step (0x45DC90), the kill log (0x4F5330). The whole block, award included, needs a killer and a PC leader behind it: the killer itself when it is a PC, else the chain of leaders of an NPC killer up to a PC (0x45DDA0); no PC leader (an NPC fighting another NPC, a death with no killer) runs nothing. | F1; built after the award and the alignment step (ARC-COMBAT-2873 [#2873](https://github.com/Simarglok/arcanum-steamwoven/issues/2873): the hero's stored alignment moves by the flag-1 step of 0x45DC90, before the kill log; a victim the hero summoned changes nothing), and the session's kill log is written on the hero's and an NPC's attack paths only, not for a spell or trap kill: the loop follows what the session has; [#2874](https://github.com/Simarglok/arcanum-steamwoven/issues/2874) kill log for spell and trap kills) |
| LK2 | list copy 0x4411C0 (field 0xF1) | The list is the PC leader's direct follower array, copied before any script runs (so a script that changes the party does not change the loop), each follower in array order, no map, distance, wait or alive gate. The dead NPC is still in its leader's array: the party leave (0x45DFC0, 0x45DE50) comes after the loop. | F1; built (the session's array order of the hero's followers; a follower with a death time is left out because the session never takes a dead follower out of its party order, the original's array holds the living, but the dying victim itself is asked; a follower that is not a row of the shown map runs nothing) |
| LK3 | call 0x441980 arguments | Triggerer: the dead NPC; attachee: the follower; extra object: the PC leader; line 0; the return is ignored (no skip-default meaning). | F1; built (`AreaUseScriptHost.RunLeaderKilling`, the dying set, slot kept as map changes) |


### Unlock, point 3 (ARC-SCRIPT-2860)

Read in the open attempts 0x4AEB70 (portal) and 0x4AED80 (container) and their callers (exe export, 2026-11-19); behaviour in prose.

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| UL1 | portal attempt 0x4AEB70, container attempt 0x4AED80 | Order of the portal attempt: a busted portal answers "opens" first; then the opener must be a PC or an NPC (else answer 5); then the unlock script (point 3) runs through 0x441980, the opener as the triggerer, the portal as the attachee, no extra object, line 0; answer 0 refuses with result 4; only then the lock tests (jammed, held, locked with the leaving-a-building and key rules). The container attempt has the same order but for a junk pile (the proto test 0xBCF answers "opens" before everything), no opener type test and the container's own flag word. The script is asked whether or not the object is locked, so an unlocked door runs it too. | F1; built (`AreaUseScriptHost.RunUnlock`, `AreaPortals.OpenRefusal`, `AreaContainerLoot.Open`: busted objects and junk piles never ask; a run that did not finish keeps the old refusal, `ScriptGuarded` / `UnlockScriptUnsupported`, since the original has no such case) |
| UL2 | callers of the attempts | The portal attempt is called by the door goals' nodes (0x428550, 0x428690, 0x428750, 0x4287E0, 0x4288A0: the open goals 11 and 12 ask it up to three times per opening, as `AreaDoorHost.DoorRefusesOpen` does), by the step test of path planning (0x43FE00, for each portal on the stepped tile when neither the bypass flag nor the always-open flag is set and the walker is a critter) and by the script condition 44; the container attempt by the loot window's admission (0x572370, once per open) and by the script condition 24. A nonzero answer of the loot admission shows the attempt's message and plays the locked sound; a nonzero answer of the door node plays the door's locked sound. No installed script uses the conditions 24 and 44. | F1; built for the door goals (hero and NPC openers), the hero's use, the planners' step tests and the loot window; the lock rules of the walker (a free NPC opening a locked door) are still the hero's in the passage, as before |
| UL3 | step test 0x43FE00 | The step test asks the attempt per crossed portal at each candidate step (the same script is asked for every planning pass and step start); a nonzero answer (the script's 0 included) blocks the step. | F1; built: `AreaPortals.Passages(session, walker)` asks the script for the walker (the AI host passes each NPC's id, `AreaAiHost.PassagesFor`; the hero's planner asks for the hero); a refusal is `Blocked`, a run that did not finish leaves the portal's T0 rule (`Unresolved`). Only a closed portal that is not busted is asked. Limits: the original skips the attempt (the portal just blocks) for a walker that cannot handle portals or has field-0x14 bit 0x20000 and for a path request with flag 2 (0x425840, 0x4257E0, 0x41F570), which the AI host does not model (`DoorOpenerCapable` is always true), so such a walker runs the script here ([#2888](https://github.com/Simarglok/arcanum-steamwoven/issues/2888)); whether to ask comes from the T0 attachment flag, so a point-3 script attached at run time is not asked; the portal attempt's opener type test (answer 5) is not enforced, every caller is a critter |
